# Continuity State

Bootstrap canon is established. **Volume 01, Batch 0001 (Chapters 1–10) and Batch 0002 (Chapters 11–20) are now drafted** and carry the locks below. Everything in the "Fixed by Batch 0001" and "Fixed by Batch 0002" sections is canon taken from finished prose and outranks the outline where the two differ.

## Series canon locks

- The First Witness is a civic intelligence assembled from one chosen memory offered by each of 312 volunteers. Names and repeated acts were recorded as identifiers and anchors, not as alternative non-memory offerings.
- Volume 11 reveals only that the First Witness is a civic intelligence rather than an original god. Volume 12 fully explains its 312 memories, Tomas's counter-witness, and Elias's ability.
- The name lock, witness lock, and return lock are three functions. The central lock is the separate keeper core beneath the Museum; the final lock is the chamber's last automatic safety interlock, which cannot release automatically and requires a deliberate opening after a tested citywide custodian network can receive the First Witness's burden. The coalition finds the final lock in Volume 11, breaks the central lock in Volume 12, and opens the final lock in Volume 14.
- Orrin is detained after the Volume 12 chamber breach, released under a surviving Accord emergency order during the Volume 13 transfer, and returns to the ward-lines for the final conflict.
- Volume 13 causes only a temporary, uneven loss of Elias's object-hearing. The irreversible loss of most of that ability belongs to the Volume 14 climax.
- The Volume 11 cost is a permanent inability to re-read one specific father memory; the Volume 14 cost is the separate loss of Tomas's clear voice when his final echo is released. They are cumulative, not the same event.
- Open Hand is the final civic practice produced by Commons, not an additional personal stage.
- In the final charter, **tended** means an active relationship remains under Museum shared-trust custody, **returned** means its custody and interpretive authority have transferred to external living stewards, and **left absent** means no living community accepts an active relationship. These are disposition statuses, not synonyms for a successful reading.

## Volume 01 canon locks (pre-existing, unchanged)

- **Wendel Case is reconciled by the payroll, not the Museum's books.** He is absent from every Museum personnel file, current and archive, for eighteen months, and has no leaver's record either. His pension and union deductions continue because the Museum's hourly staff were administered by the **municipal payroll bureau**, which holds a remittance file the Museum does not own and that nobody told to remove him from. The deduction proves existence and money moving; it does not prove the Museum's books ever carried him.
- **The corridor service bell is not a second god and not a second relic.** Pressed brass, part of the Museum's staff-call system for rooms that are on no floor plan, hard-piped to the ward-bell repeater line. It repeats what it hears, slightly out of tune. It cannot count, reach a person, make a request, or be the source of anything. It exists so the volume's escalation stays on *people who can be hurt*.
- **The unlisted room is only ever seen through a vision panel.** Its door has clouded wired glass; the chair, the coat hook, the blank lanyards and the dustless chair are all seen through it. **Elias never opens that door in this volume** and the volume's last chapter depends on him not doing so. The service bell that answers hangs in the corridor, not in the room, and is not the dead civic repeater bracket in the same corridor — which has no bell and has had none for years.
- **The eighteen-year-old Casket and the eighteen-year-old Quiet Wing instruction are one span of eighteen years.** The instruction is the paperwork of the Casket cover-up; Tomas's initials are in its attachments and are found in Movement 4, Chapters 31–40, not earlier. The name on the instruction's face belongs to nobody who has ever worked at the Museum. Volume 01 introduces Orrin Sable as a missing signature only.
- **The museum's records are true, and that is the trap.**

## Fixed by Batch 0001 — new canon taken from finished prose

*(These entries are the post-review state of the batch. Where the review of Batch 0001 changed a drafted fact, the corrected fact is the one recorded here.)*

### The four counts

**94** — people on the final Sillick closing shift. **92** — the paid payroll figure. **2** — worked and never entered in any book. **61** — short-paid on the final pay run (a pay figure, a different and overlapping group, which includes the two who were never entered). Fixed, separate, and never to be substituted for one another. No fifth figure may appear.

**On the page as drafted:** 94 in Chapter 10 only; 61 in Chapter 6 only, in Hattie Bramm's mouth. **92 and 2 are held** for the Chapter 25 midpoint reversal, per the Volume 01 outline, and must not be spent in Batch 0002. *Recorded deviation:* the Batch 0001 outline's aftermath section says all four figures are "now on the page"; the two that are not are deliberately withheld so a reader who already has 94 can be told it was paid as 92. This hold is reversible but should not be broken without recording it here.

**The year in a Museum accession reference is the year the district series was opened, not the year the object came in.** This is a Low Ward house convention and it is stated aloud by Mara Iles in Chapter 3. `1961/LW/44` therefore means *Low Ward series, opened 1961, item 44*, which is why the Sillick box can be accessioned long after the series began and why nobody can date the box from its own label. A reference with no year in it, or with one letter where the form takes two — `Acq. 4471 / B` — **is not a Museum accession number at all.** Do not reintroduce 1961 as an event year anywhere; the 1961s that were atmospheric in the first draft have been removed. The mill closed **thirty-one years ago**, and Hattie Bramm has carried her sheet for thirty-one years, and those are the only thirty-ones in the timeline.

**The numeral sixty-one does double duty** — it is the short-paid count *and* Perry Loach's age. In Chapter 6 it belongs to the count. In Chapter 10 Perry is rendered **"a night cleaner in his early sixties"** and the numeral never appears in the chapter at all. Canon age stays 61 in the reference tables; only the prose avoids it.

### The six bell events, and the seven notebook lines

**Tue 02:52** (Ch 1) · **Wed 20:55** (Ch 2) · **Wed 23:40** (Ch 3) · **Thu 00:05** (Ch 5) · **Fri ~00:25** (Ch 8) · **Sat 21:07** (Ch 10), then the interval after the ward-bell fails at 21:14.

**The notebook holds seven entries about the bell itself.** Six soundings plus one written null result. The Saturday interval sits inside the 21:07 entry as a continuation line, not as an entry of its own. The notebook also carries other matter — the device-book times, the label room, the locker number, the accession number, the payroll reconciliation — and that is a different kind of writing. *Recorded reconciliation:* the pre-draft notes said "seven" in one place and "six" in another; seven entries, of which six are soundings, and no eighth.

**There is no schedule, and Chapter 0005 proves it by failing.** Elias waits 23:40–00:05 for a pattern to confirm, gets nothing, writes the null result, and is answered at 00:05 the moment he stops watching. A schedule could be handed to somebody. This cannot.

### Object states, as dramatised

The Sillick bell is **answering** from Monday onward and **stays answering** through the end of Chapter 10. It repeats one short part of its last act — a single note, or the same note on an interval — and asks nothing of anybody. It never addresses Elias, never prevents him leaving, and is never `demanding` in this batch (`demanding` is Chapters 22–24) and never `hollowed` (that belongs to a later volume). The **false name on the card is why the crew keeps calling it unremarkable, not why it is quiet.** The route out of `stored` is the *absence* of a lock: nothing is connected to anything; something stopped being held down. The act's form changes with whether a question is asked — noise when nothing is asked, content inside the Chapter 10 protocol.

The **repair staple is a repair of a crack and the crack is still visible.** Mara identifies it in Chapter 3 as "a decision somebody made about a Tuesday morning." This is the cracked bell the series closes on; **Batch 0005 must not let it heal.**

The **god is never named in Batch 0001.** Narration uses *the hall bell*, *plinth thirty-four*, *the Sillick bell*. **"The Bell of the Unpaid Shift" is a Batch 0005 payoff.**

### The Museum's physical and documentary layout, as drafted

Five floors. **Ground:** public entrance on Civic Hill, Hall of Former Faiths (31 plinths, no-touching rule), Gallery of Unnamed Hands, security room, loading bay off the freight lift, service entrance with the device tray and book. **First:** conservation floor, registrar's office, label room, director's office. **Second:** Quiet Wing restricted rooms and the corridor outside door 7, plus the maintenance stair landing between the second and third floors. **Sub-level B:** unprocessed storage (bay 9 holds 1961/LW/41–52), staff lockers (41 of them) under the stair, wet-cardboard smell. **The Undercroft stair is not opened in this volume.**

**Cameras, and what each one does and does not cover — this is load-bearing.**
- *Label room (Ch 4):* lintel dome covering the doorway and the card cutter. **Does not cover the drawers.**
- *Quiet Wing corridor (Ch 8):* fixed opposite the stair-side service alcove. Covers the corridor, **stops two feet short of the alcove mouth.** Shares the door 7 controller circuit, so the outage *is* the camera gap.
- *Level-two archive corridor:* a restricted door that **logs card events**, and **no camera on the landing.** Elias's **02:20 Thursday** card read is in the log in his own name and Anja reads it back to him in Chapter 7. Inside: an eight-foot corridor, a painted-over window, a fire plan in a frame, and behind it the **personnel record as paper** — annual ring binders on a steel shelf, one per year from 1974, every year present, every binder undisturbed, columns of surname / grade / start / department / leaver's date and reason code. Four C surnames: Callaghan R (grounds), Cunliffe A (porter), Coverley D (porter), Crowther M (conservation). **Of the four, only Cunliffe and Coverley were ever porters; Callaghan and Crowther never were, and Chapter 5 now says so.** No W. Case, and no leaver's record for Case either. **A card index of posting sheets is tied into the same rings** — where the Museum told the bureau to send a man's letters. Grade two porter, 2001 to 2012, two hundred and eleven lines, every one the same twelve words, *c/o Museum letting, Sillick Lane, Low Ward.* **The personnel-binder scene is staged in Chapter 4** (Wednesday, 02:20, no camera on the landing). **The card index of posting sheets is staged in Chapter 5** (Thursday), immediately after the payroll reconciliation. Chapter 6 refers back to Chapter 5 for the street, not to Chapter 4. Do not merge the two.
- *Hall of Former Faiths door:* not a restricted door. A night card on it at **01:26 Thursday** logs a refusal and nothing else, and Anja has that in the access log too.

**Record formats, now stable and reusable:**
- Museum accession numbers: year / letters / number — **1961/LW/44** (Sillick Mill: administrative papers, disposal, level B bay 9), where the year is the **series** year. The plinth 34 card's false number is **Acq. 4471 / B**, which is not a Museum number in any form.
- Museum personnel file references: **PF** + three digits, e.g. **PF 118**. Personnel files are never retired. *The number on the relabelled box is a live reference and is not Case's, because Case has no file.*
- Municipal payroll: surname/forename, grade, cost centre **M.Q.S. 4**, pension, union subs, hours. One person = four lines.
- Case's locker is **81**, in a run of **forty-one lockers numbered 61–101** in two rows under the basement stair, on a printed locker card that Elias had read over for two years.
- **Staff lockers are 61–101 and there are forty-one of them.** Any later reference to a total number of lockers, or to locker 81 sitting in a run of one to forty-one, is wrong.
- **Corridor camera arrival times at 01:12**: two on the seventh night back (01:12:41, 01:12:44) and three on the fourteenth night back (01:12:42, 01:12:45, 01:12:43) — five arrivals, **all inside four seconds of one another**, on the two nights the frame smeared. The smear is in the same four seconds on both of those nights and in the same place in the frame. Of the last fourteen nights, ten show a full arrival, two show nothing at all, and two show the smear. There is no eleven-second spread anywhere in the camera material, and the retention artifact is credited with **two** lost men in two weeks, not four.
- The Hall of Former Faiths **master key** goes out in a conservator's name and returns before the galleries are closed; the night guard's own key opens public galleries and nothing else.
- **Work order 4412-S**, dated Friday 16:20, authorised 23:40–00:30 (fifty minutes), signed by a director, logged from the maintenance shop terminal under **PELL, I.** The logged outage is **23:41–00:28 — forty-seven minutes inside the window.**

**The device policy, as drafted.** Personal phones live in an unlocked grey steel tray of numbered slots (Elias's is **12**) at the service entrance, established on the page in Chapter 1 and weaponised in Chapter 4. The book beside it has name / time out / time back, and a printed head line: *NO PHOTOGRAPHY OF COLLECTIONS MATERIAL, LABELS, OR RECORDS. THE BOOK IS THE RECORD. THE TRAY IS NOT.* The desk at that tray is unmanned from 23:00 to 07:00; the only person who comes down the lane overnight is the duty manager on her round at about two, which is why Elias can be seen taking the phone in the small hours of Thursday and cannot be seen taking it on Friday night. **Elias's signed entry is 01:52 out / 02:41 back on Thursday morning, with no destination written.** On Friday night he takes the phone again with **no entry at all**, so the book shows it going home and the book is right; on Saturday morning he returns it to slot 12 and signs only a back time — a name, and 08:20, and no time out — which is itself a discoverable half-entry.

**The shared protocol log has three fields, not one, and two of them are empty.** As drafted in Chapter 10: an **occupancy line** above the rule, which Mara completes truthfully (*one cleaner*); the **result field**, which says **Result: a number**; and a box headed *account of any person present during result*, which wants a name, a grade and a countersignature, and which is **left blank**. The box is blank because Mara declines to fill it in, correctly and for stated procedural reasons: it takes a voluntary statement in the person's own words, she will not take one off a night cleaner at a plinth without his supervisor, and she will not put a guard's second-hand version in a box that wants the man's words, *because the day she starts recording what a person said to a guard, in a guard's handwriting, she has stopped keeping a record and started keeping a rumour.* Elias, who could have supplied the name in ninety seconds at a service desk, decides not to, because a name on that sheet starts a record. **The object has been shown to reach a person who has not consented, and that fact is now in a document, and the document cannot represent it.** Batch 0002 inherits this as its most important open engine.

### Elias's costs, as dramatised

**Batch 0001's entire memory cost is one thing:** in Chapter 8 he writes one notebook entry, in order, in a hand that changes twice, and afterwards cannot put the two halves in the order he wrote them. There is no second entry because he does not have one. Do not add a second memory cost. The first *major* memory-displacement cost is reserved for Volume 03, and the night Tomas left is reserved for Movement 4 and must not be staged.

**The two-hands entry, as it stands on the page — the batch's single most important notebook artefact, and it is quoted in Chapter 8 in two blocks in this order:**

> 23:52 in. No event on the panel. Corridor as plan. Draft under door 7 room — room has air, not a cupboard. 00:12 photo x4, corridor, 00:12–00:18. 00:18 now.

> Room behind wired glass: chair (no dust on seat), coat hook, lanyards on shelf, name panels all blank, dust on everything else. Door shut and latched. Did not open it. Not going to.

The **upper** block is in his flat block hand — the one he uses for things he wants to be able to find again — squared, unhurried, every letter its full height. The **lower** block is in a different hand entirely — smaller, faster, pressed harder, the hand of a man writing at a door with a torch off, because a torch on would finish something. **He is as sure as he is ever going to be that the upper one is the second half he wrote and the lower one is the first, and there is no way for him to be sure of it at all, because both halves are complete and neither one refers to the other.** Afterwards he cannot put the two halves back in the order he had written them, and never does. **Sixteen minutes of the night are in neither block and in neither hand.** Do not restate this entry in a single hand, in one order, or as recoverable. It is not recoverable in this volume. **The chapter's wording is the authority: the terms are *upper* and *lower*, not *first* and *second*, and the load-bearing clause is the reason there is no way to be sure. An earlier version of this file carried a paraphrase — "as sure as he will ever be that the first block is the second half he wrote" — which is wrong in its middle clause and must not be copied back.**

Sensory and physical costs, all real and reversible: a nosebleed and a headache that **begins at 23:41 Wednesday and breaks at 10:42 Thursday — eleven hours and one minute**, clocked from the start because a cost you cannot time is a cost nobody has to believe in (Ch 3, the same figure repeated by Elias to Mara in Ch 10); phantom rope texture across the right palm for a week (Ch 10); tinnitus for two days; **twenty minutes of lost time in the corridor, of which **sixteen minutes are not on the page at all** — they are in neither block and in neither hand** (Ch 8).

**The Chapter 10 headache is a second, separate headache.** It is a new one, it is on the page from 21:41 Saturday, and the outline gives it two days. It does not conflict with the eleven-hour figure, which is the *previous* one. Batch 0002 inherits the two-day headache and should pay it off on the page.

**The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is a Saturday-morning entry, and its interval to Chapter 10 is twenty-one hours.** The entry is written at **00:18 on Saturday**; Chapter 10's service-lane walk is at about **21:50, after the reading ends at 21:41,**  on Saturday**. Any figure connecting them is **twenty-one hours**. The *thirty-one hours* that an earlier draft used in both chapters was wrong twice, is superseded, and must not return.

**His permanent cost for Volume 01 is professional and relational:** he loses his post, and Mara stops trusting the shared record once she realises the shared log says *a number* where the reading gave ninety-four. The Chapter 10 withholding is **on the page, in front of both witnesses — and it happens inside the protocol, not against it.** Mara's own rule is that her log does not take a bare figure: no object, no *a number*, because a figure with a guess under it is a rumour she will not sign. She writes *a number* **correctly**, on the evidence she was given. Elias has four seconds in which to supply an object — he has a mill, a catalogue number and nine seconds on a terminal — and he does not, and he hands her an **empty hand** when she asks for the notebook. **Do not restage this as Elias correcting Mara, or as Mara making an error.**

**Her two Chapter 9 conditions are now in different conditions, and this is the corrected, current position. Do not collapse them back into "both intact."**

1. *The result goes in the shared log in her hand exactly as it comes.* **Intact to the letter.** He answers every question she asks, accurately and in order, and the sheet contains nothing but what he said. The condition is defeated by a correct procedure, which is the cruelty of it.
2. *He tells her everything, every time, and not the parts he has decided were relevant.* **Broken in Chapter 10, deliberately, by him, and it is the batch's clearest single act of dishonesty.** It breaks in two ways, and a later batch must not blur them. (a) **By silence:** he decides that a man with a mop is not part of what he is telling her about, and does not volunteer Perry Loach's name, which he could have had in ninety seconds at a service desk. (b) **By a direct lie, at the end:** when Mara asks him point-blank whether there is anything in the notebook that is not on the page, he says no. There is — the headcount, the cleaner's name, and the four seconds he cannot account for. **What is true, and must be stated precisely so that nobody over-corrects it: he tells her no untruth whatever about the reading itself.** The result field is correct, the units are correct, the cost is correct, the stop word is hers, and every question she asks him about the reading he answers accurately. The lie is about the notebook, after the reading is finished, and it is the only lie he tells a person in this batch. Batch 0002 inherits a promise broken once, by hand, which is what makes the large deliberate withholding in Movement 2 an escalation rather than a first offence.

### Minor working people (stable, not characters)

- **Fenn** — surname on a badge, the label-room finisher on the overnight shift in Chapter 4. Fifty-five or so, has done it a very long time, notices a stranger in the drawers. Surname only, always.
- **Priest** — the Museum's Saturday night duty manager on the freight lift, nineteen years in post. Tells Elias that Perry Loach was on the Sillick job before this building.
- **Perry Loach** — now named on the page (Chapter 10), so Batch 0003's public tour does not have to introduce him twice. Night cleaner, Saturday shift, eleven years on these floors and forty years of floors before. A Sillick Mill fitter, and one of the short-paid.
- **Helen Venn** — the letter is eighteen years old and lives on a shelf above the boiler at the laundrette. Elias has asked for it three times in one week and has never actually asked.
- **Cliff Sowerby** — Sillick Mill fitter's mate, number 14 Sillick Lane, mended the row's standpipe every autumn for a pint he would not take; somebody put a different one in and charged the row for it once, and the row paid again out of its own money. A Low Ward neighbour Hattie Bramm mentions. **He is deliberately not a second Wendel Case and the name must not drift back into the erasure plot.**
- **Callaghan, Cunliffe, Coverley, Crowther** — the four C surnames in the Museum personnel record, Chapter 4. Two were porters; none is Case. They are furniture in the erasure, not candidates.

### Elias's home

A rented room above his mother Helen's laundrette in Low Ward, close enough to the tram line to make the hill practical. He rides the tram and walks the last stretch up Civic Hill. **Transport in Low Ward is the tram, both ways.**

### The four counts — state after Batch 0002

**94** — on the page in **Ch 10, 16, 19 and 20**. **It is never said aloud to anybody, anywhere, in any of the ten chapters.** On 16 October Elias came within four seconds of saying it to Hattie Bramm in a street in front of a library door and took the four seconds to refuse instead; what went into the street was a room, a row, a plinth, a date and a case, and never the number and never a name. **Chapter 18's *You were careful with a number. You were not careful with a room* is built on that refusal and must stay built on it.** It is still in Elias's pocket notebook and is **not** in the shared log, which says *a number*. **92 — still HELD.** **2 — still HELD.** Neither figure appears anywhere in Chapters 11–20 and neither is referred to as a figure. **61** — on the page in Hattie Bramm's mouth in Ch 16 and in Mara's in Ch 20, and in Ch 18 in Doreen Sarn's; it is never a headcount and never interchangeable with 94. **The number 61 still also means the low end of the Museum's locker run and nothing else in this batch.**

**The volume's second figure, which is a different kind of figure and is the number that is wrong in a room: the Gallery of Unnamed Hands holds nine objects and forty-one drawers.** Nine is on a printed inventory sheet dated 1988 and has been on it for thirty-six years. Nine is also the number the contract returns for the cleaning service use as a head — **CLEANING — 0**. The same arithmetic twice, in two rooms, by two bodies, and neither of them is lying. Six flat files of eight drawers in the Gallery; the same steel units, in the same building, hold the personnel record on the level-two archive corridor. Forty-one and forty-one are the *same number in the same building* and must not be conflated. No fifth Sillick figure has appeared.

**The other new number, also not a Sillick figure: `ATTENDANT: 2 (TWO)`** on a lorry consignment note at the Sillick Mill site, Saturday 17 October, with one man on the slipway. A lifting-operation requirement, filled in at a yard, never read back. It is the volume's fifth document and the reason a seventy-four-year-old is in a hospital.

### Objects, records and formats introduced in Batch 0002

- **1961/LW/51** — Museum accession, *Low Ward series, item 51*: a **fitter's lamp, electric, navy enamel**, Birch & Sloane of Northgate, worked brass record strip, `1961/LW/51 — FITTER'S LAMP, ELECTRIC, NAVY ENAMEL. Presented to the Museum, 1974.` with a **ballpoint margin entry in a different hand: *Purchased at the closure of the works, 30 Oct 1974. — D. SARN.*** It stands on plinth 9 of the Gallery of Unnamed Hands, case removed to the loading bay on 12 October, drawer 6B open and the card *SEE NOTE ON DRAWER 6B*. **The Museum's lamp and the lamp in Doreen Sarn's press are a pair, not the same object.**
- **The 1974 carbon receipt**, a single sheet on a carbon, office copy, in the Gallery's own acquisition-correspondence file in drawer 6B: **RECEIPT OF MATERIAL — 7 NOV 1974**, received from **Quill & Frayle, 14 Lincoln's Lane**, eleven items collected at the works by the estate's agent on **30 Oct 1974, being the last working day**, *no disposal fee payable to the Museum*; item 3 *Gate book (leaves 1–96 present)*, item 4 *Posted wage sheets, loose, approx. 2 dozen*, item 5 *Envelope of loose papers, sealed, contents not examined*, item 8 *withdrawn, soiled*, item 9 the lamp, **item 10 — Bundle of wage sheets, tied with tape** *(the two words *marked pay office* are the BUYER'S wording and appear on the buyer's carbon only, per the Batch 0004 prompt's hard lock; they were wrongly given to the Museum's own copy in this entry and are corrected here)*, item 11 *Clock, works office, stopped*; **collected by: P. LOACH (fitter), Sillick Lane, Low Ward.** Never photographed. Copied into Elias's notebook in longhand and quoted aloud in Doreen Sarn's flat. **The Museum's own copy of this carbon is a single sheet and there is only ever one of it: it was lifted from drawer 6B at 08:20 on Saturday 14 November by Mara Iles with a slip in with it, went to Irving Quay in her wallet, and went back into drawer 6B with the slip at about four o'clock that afternoon before she left. Hattie Bramm read that sheet in the Gallery on Sunday 15 November. It has never been in two places at once and a later batch must not put it anywhere else.**
- **QA/INSP/4 (rev. 2)**, Registry inspection serial **QA/4/1187**, Monday 12 October: classification **Schedule 12 — carried object, no activity observed in the period of inspection**; the note that a sound reported by museum staff before the inspection *was not observed*, and that the assessor *makes no finding as to prior report*; photographs **1187/1 to 1187/4** on a Registry body camera, *not for reproduction*, of the plinth card described as *hand-cut, ink, recent, maker's line present name illegible, second line erased partial, number is not in the form of a Museum reference.* **The Registry assessor of 12 October is Ottilie Grange**, of Northglass, who signs it *Grange, O., assessor, quiet accord registry.*
- **QA/A/2** — the brass floor-anchor plate, two of them, fitted 12 October under plinth 34 and bolted into the terrazzo. It does nothing. Its whole function is to make the object *located*. The only brass on the floor of thirty-one plinths.
- **QA/1187** — the tag on the padlock of the cage.
- **Annexe D** — the **permanent** Quiet Accord classification, *attached* by the assessor **Aurelia Roth on Monday 19 October**, on the strength of the five-hour examination of 12 October and a file that had come into the Registry from three places in three days: *a plain bell with no maker's mark, no devotional content, and a mechanical repair is not a Quiet Saint*; **confidence: medium**; *the object is to be displayed. Under no circumstances is it to be removed from public view by a museum acting alone.* **This is the document that holds the bell in a public room for the rest of the volume and it was produced by a stranger with a statutory duty who has seen none of the case's evidence.**
- **Annexe C** — the registrar's certificate of condition, **one sentence: *No undue deterioration has occurred since the last certification.*** Re-issued 20 October to permit a public event in the Hall. Mara Iles calls it *a lie of omission of a size I have signed about four hundred times.*
- **QA/N/34** — the Registry/Civic Works notice of 15 October: works at sub-level B *completed*; third ward-bell **out of service, will not return to service this quarter**; **district lock positions: unchanged**; Museum action: none. Its consequences: the **ward-bell repeater line is dead**, so the pressed-brass corridor service bell in the unlisted corridor goes silent from that morning, with no fault and nobody coming. It is still plumbing, still a repeater, still not a god, and it is now a repeater with nothing to repeat.
- **Director's Standing Conditions — Movement and Access of Schedule 12 Carried Objects**, four clauses, issued 10:00 on 15 October, signed W. Ward. They are the Movement 2 spine and **Batch 0003 must observe clause four exactly** or the tour of 13 November becomes an unshielded room.
- **Custody Disc 41** — a small red anodised tag, `1961/LW/44 — in temporary custody, west range, ground — W. WARD — 16:20`, on the cage. A red disc; Anja Ward's initials in ballpoint. The vocabulary is the *temporary custody disc* of Batch 0001, already in the continuity record.
- **STANDING INSTRUCTION 14 (2024) — Objects in Transit: Suspended Quarantine**, Monday 19 October, four pages, three signatures, clauses one to seven. **Clause six is the one that undoes him**: no document, image, record or copy of Museum material may be lodged in any Museum file, Board file or departmental file the Museum has not itself requested, without the Director's written authority. **Clause seven is Mara's**, added Sunday 18 October at 15:55 in the margin, initialled and dated: the Museum will not destroy any record of an individual employee held by the municipal payroll bureau without first obtaining the bureau's own account in writing.
- **The goods-out docket** of 20 October: **PLACED BY TRANSFER: 20:00, Sat 16 Oct**, initialled **D.O.**, disposition line **NOT ON HOLD**, place line *cage to lorry, w/c 20th*, and a witness line reading **Present at removal: name, grade, time, signature** — with no *in whose hand* and no *of his own words*. **Unsigned on 20 October.** The date is in the past and the form has not been destroyed.
- **The proposed disposition lapses at the close of Thursday 29 October, on the page.** The Director's condition 3, issued 15 October, says a proposed disposition not executed within ten working days lapses and the object returns to its last certified condition and its last certified place. The proposal was posted at about 10:00 on **Thursday 15 October** under a plastic wallet on a corridor wall. Ten working days off the fifteenth is the **twenty-ninth**, and the twenty-ninth is a Thursday. **Stated aloud by Anja Ward on 20 October and to nobody else, on the reasoning that she is not going to be the woman who says a thing like that out loud in a building before it has happened.** Consequences: **on Friday 30 October the cage is a piece of furniture in a corridor**; Hallowgate Foyston, which has a man booked to sign for the site on 4 November, has to re-propose, and a re-proposal is a new docket, a new ten days, and a new public-event test; and the good docket of 20 October becomes a **lapsed** one rather than a late one, which is the only piece of good news the volume gets out of an institution and it comes out of a clause a woman wrote in a hurry on a **Thursday** morning at six o'clock. **This is not stated in Chapter 19 and is not in `state/open-threads.md`; it is stated in Chapter 20 and is canon.** The chapter and this entry were corrected together in the Batch 0002 review pass: the prose had the thirtieth as a Wednesday, which is neither the true weekday nor the true tenth working day, and Anja's own sentence contradicted itself inside the quotation.
- **The transfer clerk's witness form** of 19 October, 21:00 — the same four boxes, filled in by Elias with four hundred words, corrected by the transfer clerk on Elias's instruction from *cage 2, west range* to **cage 2, service corridor**, initialled in the margin.
- **The written transfer conditions** of 20 October, four clauses, Anja Ward's: one movement only, 10:00–11:00; witnessed by a person who is not an officer or employee of the Museum and not a member of the public; returned to its last certified condition and re-certified before public access; **the disposition is not decided by this movement**.
- **Ivy Case's letter.** Unaddressed and returned; forwarding sticker expired six weeks before Elias reads it; signed **I. Case**, writing for a father at **4 Sillick Lane** who cannot write, who did the last count at the gate on the Friday morning, and who *says he has never had anything to do with the paper in the tin and will not have it on his record*; the money still going to a dead wife's name since 1994. **Wendel Case is fifty-eight, per canon, and the letter says fifty-eight.** The letter contains a bit of paper with figures on it kept in the tin, and the sentence *we know about the money*, and the sentence *you can stop his money for a week and we can live on a week; you cannot stop it for a year.*
- **The Museum's letting address**: **14 Mill Lane, Low Ward**, the postal address for grade hourly staff, 1998 to 2011. Four weekly payslips a year went there for eleven years and went into the bin behind the loading bay and out of the door **in the Monday post bag**, in blue bales, to a paper merchant, on **Friday** mornings, to a pulp mill. The printed head of the book they were recorded in read *NO PHOTOGRAPHY OF COLLECTIONS MATERIAL, LABELS, OR RECORDS. THE BOOK IS THE RECORD. THE TRAY IS NOT*, and there was no column for a destination.
- **The revised device policy**, 15 October: no tray; one line per device at the front desk in the **day book**; the tray removed to the paper store; the printed head now reads **NO PHOTOGRAPHY OF COLLECTIONS MATERIAL, LABELS, OR RECORDS. THE DAY BOOK IS THE RECORD OF ISSUE AND RETURN.** *Its whole effect is that a telephone in a coat pocket in a gallery is now in a coat pocket in a gallery and the book can never prove it, and the four photographs are weaker on 15 October than they were on 13 October.*
- **The services contract.** The cleaners are **not** on the municipal payroll bureau and not on **M.Q.S. 4**. They are on a cleaning services contract written in **establishment, shift and hours**, and contractor personnel are not employees of the client and are not recorded on the client's staff roll. The weekly contract returns are a printed grid filled in by hand headed **CLEANING — 0**, with hours split by room: Hall of Former Faiths 8h × 3 days, Gallery 2h × 2 days, west range/corridor 1h × 7 days, loading bay 30m × 7 days, Quiet Wing corridor 1h × 7 days. **There is no column in the grid in which a person can be written.**
- **The punch-card abolition notice**, 1 November: the time book and punch cards at the building are abolished; hours are recorded at a terminal by badge; *the payroll bureau is not affected, the bureau continues to administer grade hourly staff, cost centre M.Q.S. 4.* The cleaners are not on the bureau. The abolishing of the book removes the only book that recorded the cleaners, the porters, and the men who came in at nine and went at three.
- **The dead letters.** A manila envelope marked DEAD-LETTERS, with a biro number, standing on the corner of the loading-bay desk. Two hundred and ten processed in Batch 0002. **The running series for Batch 0003 is on the closing beat of every chapter and only ever rises: 234 (Wed 21 Oct) · 240 (Thu 22) · 248 (Fri 23) · 251 (Sun 25) · 253 (Mon 26) · 255 (Tue 27) · 258 (Wed 28) · 261 (Thu 29) · 264 (Fri 30) · 271 (Sat 7 Nov). Saturday 24 October is off-page and the count is not given. 210 is the end of Batch 0002 and is not a contradiction of 234.** The Museum's own 1976 letter to *Mrs D. Sarn* is among them: returned, forwarded twice, pencilled *Deceased — family*, forwarding address **c/o Mrs J. Rusk, Sillick Court, Connaught Wing, 4th floor, please pass on.** Copied out longhand because the printed line forbids photography. The typed classification sheet of 1976 — the page that assigned the papers to the *administrative papers, disposal* series — is **not** among the dead letters, and that is a thing to be looked for.
- **The leaving card** of a grade-two employee, *MALLOY, J. — Grade 2*, *2,041 hrs* and *nil*, filled in in biro on the back of a printed leaving card. *(This entry said "two hundred and forty-one hours in"; Chapters 25 and 33 and line 285 of this file all say 2,041, and the two thousand and forty-one is the right figure. Corrected.)* Not Case, not a candidate, not a thread. It is the only joke in the batch.
- **The consignment note** of 17 October, on a lorry tailgate at the Sillick Mill site: **ATTENDANT: 2 (TWO)**. One man was on the slipway.
- **The booking book.** A green hardback ledger on the security desk, four columns, unaltered in two years: **13 NOV — 11:00 — HALL OF FORMER FAITHS — 38 children, 2 teachers — per term booking — St Anselm's (Hall Lane) — booked 1 Sep — ref. SK/BK/0912.** The term booking is the public event that defers the disposition under Anja Ward's condition, and it is a piece of paper in a ledger on a security desk and it is the most powerful object in the building.

### Deadlines now on the page

- **Saturday 16 October** — the docket's own date, four days in the future when it is read; **Thursday 22 October** — the Board sub-committee, item 5, schedule of contents; **Thursday 29 October** — the proposed disposition lapses at close of business. **Tuesday 20 October** — the docket on the desk, unsigned. **Wednesday 4 November** — Hallowgate Foyston's site clearance, the bailiff appointment, and the date in the Quill & Frayle letter. **Wednesday 28 October** — the hearing in Northglass. **Sunday 1 November** — punch cards abolished by the notice of 19 October, the first badge-terminal day being **Monday 4 November** (1 November is a Sunday; see the calendar note below). **Friday 16 November** — laundry load 340, Low Ward, the out-of-house firm. **Friday 13 November, 11:00** — the school group in the Hall of Former Faiths, 38 children and 2 teachers, under Annexe C and Annexe D, **as booked**; the school brings it forward inside Batch 0003 to a Saturday, and the brought-forward date must fall **after 4 November** or Dael Ordway's deferral argument is worthless. **Thursday 19 November, 11:00** — the material-change inspection, QA/CR/1187, with a member of the public present, on the request form Elias lodged at the front desk on Monday 19 October. The Registry's material-change slot was empty on Monday and is not empty now. **The Registry rota is four assessors and the job list is a paper on a wall in Northglass; Ivo has a copy of the bulletin.**

### Object states

The Sillick bell is **answering** throughout Batch 0002 and **says nothing at all in any of the ten chapters**. It was caged on 12 October, returned to plinth 34 on 20 October, and never sounded. **It is never `demanding` and never `hollowed`.** The **repair staple is a repair of a crack and the crack is still visible** — it is named on the page in Chapters 12 and 20, and Chapter 20 states that a Registry assessor is going to certify it in four weeks as *a mechanical repair, not an act of veneration*, and that she will be right. The god is still unnamed. **The only other object read in this batch is the fitter's lamp, 1961/LW/51, and it is a lamp: its last meaningful act is being a light, so what it shows is the room it last lit, and it cannot show a face, and it repeats a sentence it was in the room for.** That is the volume's proof that an echo is perspective-bound: it can show a person accurately while making the surrounding situation false, and it can repeat a lie because an owner repeated it.

### Elias's costs, as dramatised in Batch 0002

- **The Chapter 10 headache breaks at 21:50 on Monday 12 October**, forty-eight hours after it began at 21:41 on **Saturday 10 October** (it had been a solid weight behind his left eye since Sunday morning), and it breaks without warning, and the first thing he feels is not relief but *the specific vertigo of a man who has been leaning on something and has just had it taken away*.
- The second reading, 22:10–22:25 on 12 October: a four-second gap at the answer, hot iron in the nose for about an hour, both palms hot, **the rope gone from the right hand**, no card worth the name, no witness, no one to say the word, a barred door, and a bound of **fifteen minutes where the promise was eleven**.
- **There is no second notebook entry and no second memory cost.** The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is not restated, not tidied, not stabilised and not referred to as a cost in any of the ten chapters. The first major displacement remains reserved for Volume 03, and the night Tomas left remains reserved for Movement 4.

### The two withholdings, both now on the page

1. **Chapter 10.** The **first** of Mara's conditions is intact to the letter and was defeated by a correct procedure. The **second** he broke by silence and then by a lie to her face about the notebook. *He tells her no untruth whatever about the reading itself.* Unchanged by Batch 0002 and not restaged.
2. **Chapter 16, and it is an escalation, not a first offence.** He knows the true count and that the pay run listed fewer than were on the gate, and in a street in front of a library door, with eleven people behind the glass and two of them with bad chests, he tells **Hattie Bramm the location, the row, the plinth, the date and the case**, and he does it with **four seconds in which to say *I can't tell you that*** — a true answer, available, and not taken. He is not protecting himself. He did it because the private channel keeps closing because he keeps closing it, and because on Wednesday he had given four photographs to the post and told Doreen Sarn the truth and had watched the only copy in the world sit on a table with nobody to give it to. **He refuses to say a name in that room**, which is a restraint and not a second breach, and **Chapter 18 is where he names the mechanism out loud and Mara tells him what it is going to cost.**

### The record of what is provable and what is only held by one man

- **Provable, in two institutions' files:** a card in a drawer, hand-cut, with a maker's line erased thoroughly and a second line erased badly; an accession number on it that is not in the form of a Museum reference; a photograph of the same erasure by a Registry assessor with a scale rule; and a label on a box carrying **PF 118**.
- **Provable, in one drawer, in a room he is barred from, and copied into his notebook:** a 1974 carbon receipt listing a bundle of wage sheets tied with tape and marked *pay office*, taken out of a mill on its last working day by a man whose name and street the Museum has on its own posting sheets, received from a firm of solicitors who have not moved in fifty years, and **never opened**.
- **Provable, in a file, on a Sunday, in a registrar's own hand:** the name **Wendel Case**, given to a member of the Board and to an auditor and to nowhere else.
- **Held by one man, in a pocket notebook, and by nobody else:** **ninety-four**, and the fact that the pay run listed fewer than were on the gate. **Held by one man, and refused to himself:** a second number's existence, or its non-existence. Held by one man: the only account of the corridor's twenty minutes. Held by one woman, in a flat in Ferry Bank, and not surrendered and not to be: a hardback cash book with a rubber band round it, the last physical copy of the shortfall sheet she made for herself.
- **Held by nobody at all, and this is the batch's last engine:** a night cleaner's account. The form's box wants his own words and a countersignature and it is blank and it will stay blank, and the only person in Morrowgate who could build a form that holds him has said she will build it, and will not let him write a word of it.

### Other corrections, by class

- **The clock of the second reading.** It ran 22:55 → 22:00 → 22:56 and its bound was *thirty-one hours*, which is the batch's own motif number corrupted into a duration. It is now: headache breaks **21:50**, into the Gallery **22:00**, four minutes in the room at **22:04**, reading **22:10–22:25**, out at **23:00**, and the bound is **fifteen minutes where the promise was eleven**, which is what Chapters 10, 12 and 18 all say it is. The headache began at 21:41 on **Saturday 10 October**, not on the Saturday before last.
- **The statement.** It was dated *Thursday 6 October* and *Saturday 7 October*, and put the second set of photographs in the Gallery of Unnamed Hands at 22:40, before the reading it claims to follow. It is now **Thursday 8 October** at about 02:05 (four angles on the plinth 34 card, from the label room) and **Monday 12 October at about ten at night** (four exposures of the Gallery's own drawer 6B, open, in place), and the fault report is **Saturday 10 October** at about 07:30. **The protocol form, the day-book reading habit and the fault-report claim that Chapters 18 and 19 inherit are all the tenth**, because the reading with the cleaner was the sixth bell event, on Saturday the tenth at 21:07.
- **The photographs.** Four of the card, four of the drawer: eight in all, and only four were ever sealed into anything. Chapter 18 said eight were deposited. It is four, and the other four are still on his telephone, and Chapter 15's handover to Mara now says so.
- **The moral turn.** Chapter 16 has him refuse, in dialogue, to say a number or a name, and then three paragraphs later narrate that he had said a number in the same street four hours ago, and that the number was ninety-four. **The refusal is the beat and it stands; the narration was wrong and has been rewritten.** The number is not in any room. **Anything in Batch 0003 that assumes ninety-four was said in a street is wrong.**
- **The chain of custody.** One envelope could not go to the Board Secretary and to an insurer's policy address. There are now two, posted a day apart, and the claims address is found where it should be found: printed at the foot of the fault report form he falsified, in the post room, in a box marked *CLAIMS — POST ROOM COPY*. The Board's copy is inside clause six and is worth nothing; the insurer's copy is not inside anything.
- **Addresses.** **Wendel Case is at 4 Sillick Lane** — the daughter's letter and Hattie Bramm agree, and the letter is a document. **Cliff Sowerby is at 14 Sillick Lane**, and so is the flat Hattie Bramm is sitting in, which is the connection between them. The chapter that said 14 for Case was wrong. **The Museum's letting address is 14 Mill Lane** and is a different 14 entirely.
- **Ages.** Doreen Sarn is **seventy-eight** throughout, in the sentence that summarises her too. Wendel Case is **fifty-eight**, per his daughter's letter, and is therefore *in his fifties* to Hattie Bramm and to the room.
- **Ages and dates that are right and were nearly "fixed" by mistake.** The present year is **2024**, fixed by *Standing Instruction 14 (2024)*. So: 1974 to 2024 is **fifty years** (the sealed box, the unlit lamp) and 1976 to 2024 is **forty-eight years** (Quill & Frayle's file) and 1988 to 2024 is **thirty-six years** (the count of nine) and 2015 to 2024 is **nine years** (Hattie Bramm's letters). All four are correct as drafted and must not be touched.
- **The "thirty-one years" motif — read this before using it again.** It is the volume's refrain for the age of the claim and it appears twenty-one times across both batches and in the series outline. **It does not compute against 1974.** It is kept where it describes the claim, the closure, the chase, the museum's word, the lamp's memory or the standpipe. **It must never again be attached to a document the book has dated**: in the repair pass it was removed from the 1976 letter (forty-eight years), from the two shillings and the press (fifty years), and from Doreen Sarn's copy of the shortfall sheet (fifty years), because those instances collided with the book's own arithmetic inside a single scene. A writer who needs that number should reach for *thirty-one years* about the claim and for nothing else.
- **Meta language, three instances removed** and a fourth found: *this book was full of women with one finger up* (Ch 18), *the last time in the volume that she did* (Ch 20), *the first completely straight answer anybody in this story has given me* (Ch 20), and *the only document in this volume that anybody can be proud of* (Ch 18, which the first review pass missed).
- **Chapter 11 was titled *The Card-Cutter Alcove*** and contained neither a card cutter nor an alcove. It is now **What It Was Kept From**, after the bronze plate over the door.
- **Day counters.** All the "N days" counters in Chapters 12–20 now run from one anchor — **elapsed days since 07:00 on Sunday 11 October**, so Monday 12th is one day, Tuesday 13th two, Wednesday 14th three, Thursday 15th four, Friday 16th five, Saturday 17th six, Sunday 18th seven, Monday 19th eight, Tuesday 20th nine. Chapter 14's own line about the forwarding address taking *three days* to turn into a place is the anchor, and it was right before anything else was.
- **Two duplicated paragraphs in Chapter 20** — the Director's *I am going to spend the morning on the telephone to a woman in Northglass with a statutory duty and a satchel* was spoken twice, eleven lines apart, with different endings. One of them is gone and the surviving speech has been joined to its second half.
- **The cage moved on Monday the nineteenth at about 21:00**, not on Tuesday morning at six, because that is what Chapter 19 says four times and what the witness form is timed at. Chapter 20's *I want the cage out of the corridor by nine* has become *off the middle of that floor before eleven*, which is also what the systems index says it is doing there.

## Immediate starting state for Batch 0003

Top of Wednesday 21 October 2024. Elias Venn, 32, suspended from night duty since 07:00 on Sunday 11 October, unpaid, in the building on a 07:00–15:00 and 18:00–21:00 rhythm, permitted on the ground floor only, holding a pocket notebook, an unspent name, and a review on the fourth Monday. The bell is back on plinth 34, sealed, re-anchored, classified **Annexe D**, permanent, displayed, and unable to be removed from public view by a museum acting alone. The good docket is unsigned on a desk; the bad docket has never been signed. The Sillick box is in a cage in a corridor off the west range with a red disc and a padlock, and the site it was going to is bare. There are **two** registered-post envelopes out of this building: the four photographs of the erased card and a 400-word statement addressed to the **Board Secretary** (handed to the duty manager 13 Oct 18:24, out in the Monday bag on the morning of **Monday 19 October**), and a second identical set with a second typed copy of the statement addressed to **the claims manager, Vail & Thwaite, Northglass**, posted at the branch office on Sillick Lane on the evening of **Tuesday 13 October** and delivered on Monday 19th. **The first is inside the building and is inside Standing Instruction 14 clause six. The second is in Northglass and is not, and that is the whole of what Chapter 19 is about.** A registry assessor has photographed the erased name and stopped at the word *partial*. A seventy-four-year-old man is in hospital with an arm, a temperature, a head, and a police file. A claims manager in Northglass has a second page and a hearing on the twenty-eighth and has asked him not to be in the room. A dead man's post is in a pulp mill. A school group of thirty-eight is booked for the Hall of Former Faiths at eleven o'clock on **Friday the thirteenth of November** and the Registrar has certified the room in one sentence. A material-change inspection is on the nineteenth of November at eleven with a member of the public present. A form for a night cleaner is being built by a woman who will not let him near it. And a woman of seventy-eight has told him, in a chair by a lit gas fire, that he had better not be the only one who can do what he does, and he has nine weeks.
