Weighbridge poster up in Lantern Row. Ivo's board, handrail and rule; the Hall closed to parties at 15:00; Dael at 17:10. |
| **Thu 22 Oct** | 22 | 09:00 – 20:00 | PO-4471 at 09:04. Hattie on the far pavement 11:00, on the site 11:40. Hallowgate Crane Hire read off a stencilled plate on a crane cab. Container sealed ~14:20, bay 4, in bond. Two readings of the bay number. |
| **Fri 23 Oct** | 23 | 07:00 – 22:00 | **Low Ward police station 14:20** (caution accepted 14:34; interview 14:40–17:58). Ivo's March condition survey photographs, 246 exposures, drawer 6B in four. **Ainsley's witness list.** He strikes **H. Bramm** and **H. Loach**. |
| **Sat 24 Oct** | 24 | **06:00** – Sun 25 Oct 01:00 | Docket executed 06:00. **The wrong item goes: 1961/LW/51.** Destination corrected in writing 10:20. Broomhead Reach four lines, 11:40, last one *put away*. Slipway 11:00–14:00, the boy's wrist ~13:25. Protective movement instruction 16:00. Case staged in the loading-bay office 20:30. **Reading 00:52–01:01, Sun 25 Oct**, room of eleven, word *hold* by Ivo. |
| **Sun 25 Oct** | — | off-page | **The site clears. The last thing to go is the shed.** Bounded: no supernatural event, no sound, nothing in the water. |
| **Mon 26 Oct** | 25 | 09:00 – 23:00 | **Material-change return inspection QA/CR/1187, 09:00**, Hall of Former Faiths and Gallery of Unnamed Hands. Elias attends **as a member of the public** in the visitors' book, ref. QA/CR/1187. Wynne Loach present, ref. QA/CR/1420. **The reading of item 5 at 20:20, ninety seconds, no question.** **The reversal in the loading-bay office at 20:22.** 92 and 2 leave his mouth. Hearing set for **Thursday 26 November, 18:00**, fee £140. |
| **Tue 27 Oct** | 26 | 09:00 – 22:00 | School telephones 09:10 and moves the tour to **Saturday 7 November, 11:00**. **Hall re-opens 10:00** on the re-issued Annexe C. Mara hands over the form 11:30. **Perry Loach, 14:31, nine minutes, no notes taken.** The consent statement rehearsed in the Hall at 16:00, four attempts. |
| **Wed 28 Oct** | 27 | 09:00 – 21:00 | **Private hearing, Cattlemarket, Northglass, 10:00–19:00.** Elias not in the room. **Arthur Neve, 78, Ravenscourt and Hale, 6 Northgate Steps, found on the stair at 16:30** with a buyer's carbon. **Dael Ordway**, witness at 15:00, private word in the corridor at 15:40, one sentence about money at 19:20. |
| **Thu 29 Oct** | 28 | 09:00 – 22:00 | **Manifest Transfer Schedule**, 08:40, eleven lines, *no opening* / *condition as received*. Nunn's refusal ~10:20. **MPB/R/8841, H. Lound, Chief Clerk**, in at 09:10. Doreen Sarn 15:30; *somebody opens the box*, said twice, 15:41. Mara's decision ~17:00. Hattie 20:12, and the six. |
| **Fri 30 Oct** | 29 | 09:26 – 22:00 | **The disposition lapsed 00:04.** Cage is furniture. Letters went Wednesday 30th. **Six in the front room of the Sillick Lane branch library, 11:00, Friday; the queue in the back room at the same time.** Vera Pilling speaks. Two undelivered letters found 21:14. |
| **Sat 31 Oct – Sat 8 Nov** | — | off-page | **The week between.** Bounded and boring. **The cage is taken out of the corridor and put back in the old frame store in bay 9 on Friday 1 November.** **Hallowgate Foyston re-proposes on Wednesday 4 November** and gets ten working days it cannot use. **The weighbridge poster goes into the Museum lobby on Monday 4 November**, face-out, at a nine-year-old.s eye height. **The time book and punch cards are abolished by the notice of 19 October with effect from Sunday 1 November, and the first badge-terminal day is Monday 4 November.** |
| **Sat 7 Nov** | 30 | 09:00 – 19:00 | **The tour, 11:00.** **The bell answers *eleven* at 11:12.** The anchor card and the clamp go on the floor at 11:13. **Perry Loach says "that's the shop" twice, 11:14.** The children complete the run. Interval one hundred seconds. **Annexe E, read into the record at 13:10 by Ottilie Grange.** |

*Recorded clock deviations, all three inside their own days:* **Ch 24 opens at 06:00, not 12:00** — the prompt's day map said 12:00 and the prompt's chapter card said the docket was executed at 06:00, and the chapter follows the card, because a docket is the chapter. **Ch 23 opens at 07:00, not 09:00.** **Ch 29 opens at 09:26, not 09:00**, because nothing happens in a loading bay between seven and twenty past nine on a Friday morning when a proposal has lapsed at four minutes past midnight and nobody rang anybody.

## The four counts — state after Batch 0003

**94** — said aloud for the first time anywhere in the volume, in the loading-bay office, at **20:22 on Monday 26 October**, in a room of eleven adults with a tape recorder and a camera, and the words **92** and **2** left his mouth with it, for the first time in the volume, and were recorded by a municipal conciliation field officer with the words *"Attributed to reader"* against them. **Before that date 94 had never been said aloud to anybody in any of the first thirty chapters except one man in overalls on the sixth of October who had said it in a gallery at ten o'clock at night, unprompted, and stopped.** It was said **once** in Batch 0003, in a room, on the record.

**92 — SPENT, at 20:22 on 26 October, and 2 — SPENT, in the same sentence.** Neither figure has been spoken in any other room in any other chapter. The reader must not infer from the fact that they are on the page in this file that they are loose.

**61** — never a headcount. On the page in Batch 0003 once, in Dael Ordway's mouth in Chapter 20's inheritance and not restaged.

**No fifth Sillick number exists anywhere in Batch 0003.** The new figures introduced are all of other kinds and are itemised below: **a hundred and forty** (Hattie Bramm's own figure, from nine years of letters, and a *claim* and not a record and not a mill's and not a bank's), **six** (the number Hattie can put to a street and a date, given on 29 October and not given on 26), **nine dead, two in a home, one in a hospital** (categories, not a subtotal of the six), **71** (the last number on Hattie's own list, an entry and not a count, and the only figure that goes into a form in Chapter 30), **twenty-four** (a corner of a leaf in an echo, a figure nobody may say what it counts), **2,041 hrs / NIL** (a leaving card, a man's own hours), **eleven, twelve, fourteen, fifteen, sixteen, twenty, twenty-one, twenty-two, twenty-four** (a gate run on a tape, in Chapter 30, and the missing ones are **thirteen, seventeen, eighteen, nineteen, twenty-three**), and **05:54, six minutes to six** (a stopped clock).

**THE HARD RULE, restated and now live:** *any chapter that needs a payroll figure writes the conflict without a numeral* — *the pay run listed fewer than were on the gate* — and says so here rather than quietly using 92. The reversal in Chapter 25 spends 92 and 2 once, in one sentence, in one room, and it does not recur.

## New documents, institutions, formats and numbers introduced in Batch 0003

- **The Occupation Account form.** One sheet of A4, Museum notepaper, printed on the front and typed on the back, no Museum number, dated 21 October 2024, **held by Quarry Bank Solicitors, 4 Irving Quay, in a safe**, with a receipt dated 21 October. Its six operative provisions, in these words and no others: *may be left blank, and the paper may be handed in blank, and leaving it blank has no effect on your work, your hours, your pay, your contract, or your immigration status*; *write in your own words, do not ask anybody to write it for you and do not let anybody write it for you*; *this form is not filled in from a memory, nobody is to help you remember, and if you do not know, write I do not know*; *it will be countersigned by your supervisor*; **and the only line in the volume about consent that anybody has ever written down: *Do you want anybody in the room with you while you fill this in? Yes / No / I do not know yet. You may change this answer at any time, including after you have handed it in.*** Signature line at the foot, deliberately unoccupied. **Elias Venn does not sign it and must never sign it.**
- **Quarry Bank Solicitors**, 4 Irving Quay, two partners, one of them **Nan Quill**, a former hospital-laundry union shop steward. Not a panel firm. Holds a document schedule and dates receipts.
- **The form's address box:** *MUSEUM OF QUIET SAINTS — LOADING BAY OFFICE. Second floor, service lane. Ask for the duty manager. Morrowgate 4 — Temple Street 0118 — 01r.* **The loading-bay office is now a real room**: a small ground-floor office off the loading bay with a table, chairs, a door, a window onto the bay and a telephone. It is where the reversal happens in Chapter 25, where the cover stood unused against a wall in Chapters 24–27, and where the reading room of Chapter 30 is not. **The service lane is a first-floor stair, not a second-floor one** — the prompt said "second floor, service lane"; the prose says second floor and Elias is permitted there, so **the service lane stair serves the first floor and the loading bay** and the sentence is Elias's own memory of a telephone number and is not a floor plan.
- **Redgate & Kell** (press, the two men of Chapter 19) — third question 21 Oct (*Is the object in the Hall of Former Faiths at this moment?*, referred at 10:40), **Statement 3** out 12:30 on 21 Oct, fourth question 30 Oct, **Statement 4** out 16:00 on 30 Oct. **Cheryl Vosper is proved right about everything in Chapter 23** and gets his voicemail at 19:22 with four words on it.
- **Ivo Pell's notice and rule**, screwed to the wall of the west range corridor beside the fire assembly point on 21 October: plywood, routed, Vitrex, lock. Four lines: **a person is assessed at Levels 1 to 4; Level 4 means the person cannot move through the building without being moved by another person; a party shall never include, in its tasking, any person assessed at Level 4 or above; if a party is required to do a job and cannot, the party does the job, and if it cannot, the party goes out, and the party goes out first and the building empties around it.** Plus: *a party is a party; a pair of people is a party; a person on their own is a party; if you are not sure whether you are a party, you are a party.* **Nine eviction parties, nine failures.** And the *fixed positions* file, last whole-building review **Thursday 16 September — five weeks before Chapter 21** — nine entries, the last of which is: *west range service corridor, ground floor — one observer, fixed position, minimum distance three metres, no contact, at all times when the corridor is in use.* **That entry is Elias Venn and the file is never read again in this volume.**
- **Ivo's new handrail**, galvanised tube, nine stand-off brackets, the length of the west range corridor, screwed to the wall and not to the cage mesh, capped ends, painted over the two bracket heads that fouled a tray. Nine days old on 24 October and still the newest thing in the corridor.
- **PO-4471**, Hallowgate Foyston, 14 Lincoln's Lane: one 40ft steel storage container sealed, one low-loader, crane hire from **Halloway Crane Hire** half a day, *attendance 08:00*, consignee on collection **Quay 3, Broomhead Reach, Northglass**, note *quantity reduced to 1. Attendee count required on site — see prior order amendment.* **Halloway Crane Hire's telephone number is stencilled on a crane cab** and the hire firm's office woman will say what the sheet says out loud on arrival, and will not say more, and that is her entire professional position and it is correct.
- **Quay 3, Broomhead Reach, Northglass — bay 4 — in bond — a bonded cage.** The site's contents went there on Friday 25 October. **It is the same store the Museum's own cage was destined for** (Ch 15's plastic wallet: *destination: quay-side secure store, awaiting re-issue to purchaser*), and nobody put the two lists side by side, and nobody ever will, because one is a Museum accession and one is a company schedule and they both describe the same van on the same afternoon in October 1974.
- **Broomhead Reach, secure store — the four lines of 24 October, 11:40**, ending in **PUT AWAY**: 1. Line 4 received into bond 09:15, sealed, seal intact. 2. **Line 1 did not travel**; nothing is expected against it; the steel container consigned on the twenty-second is on the quay at BAY 4 and **will be broken bulk when we say so and not before**. 3. Line 4 is a paper item in bond, small, and it will go on the same bay as the container unless somebody tells us different. 4. **Put away.** **Line 2 must keep *when we say so and not before*: the container's break bulk is Wednesday 11 November at 09:40 and is a load-bearing Batch 0004 date, and the clerk's line is a professional declining to commit to a date, not an early promise.** **Line 3 is not a mistake in the manuscript.** Line 4 is a sealed plinth object and the clerk has classified it as a small paper item because the docket he is working from says *one crate of papers*; the error is the mechanism, and it is the reason a Schedule 12 object was shelved. **Do not correct it.**
- **Custody Disc 41, after Chapter 24.** The red disc was on the box when the box went up the tailgate ramp at 06:04 and was on it when the box came back down at 06:21, and it was replaced by **Custody Disc 42, cut at 16:00 on Saturday 24 October** by a woman in the post room with a pair of shears, **because a disc that has been on a lorry is not a disc.** The disc did not leave the building.
- **The two flags of Chapter 24.** Flag one, certain: the docket said **1961/LW/44** and the load was **1961/LW/51**, and the thing that left the building on a lorry on Saturday 24 October at 06:00 was a **fitter's lamp from plinth 9 of the Gallery of Unnamed Hands in a sealed vitrine**, and the thing that stayed in the cage was the box of eleven. **The mechanism, and it is the only thing in the batch that had to be rebuilt in repair: the crate went UP the tailgate ramp and came back DOWN it.** The docket's third block — *countersignature required, a person who is not an officer or employee of this Museum* — was empty and was always going to be empty, and the line at the foot of the form was not a signature block at all and said **PRESENT AT REMOVAL: name, grade, time, signature**, which is Elias Venn's line, and in the space where the signature went he wrote **five** sentences in his own hand, the first of which is that the third block is blank and cannot be filled and the last of which is **that no removal took place.** Nunn read it twice, put the crate back on its pallet, drew a line through his one line, wrote *did not travel* beside it and initialled the alteration at 06:21, **saying out loud that he had said the same thing to the office on the telephone on Tuesday and had been told to come at five in the morning anyway, and that this was now the office's problem and not his, and the lamp went up the ramp in its place with the docket's destination on the line because a clerk does not improve on a document.** **Note the two reasons and keep them apart: the vitrine is stopped by Nunn's own professional limit — *I can't put a sealed plinth object on a lorry on my own authority* — and the crate is stopped by a signature nobody in the city is able to give. The chapter's argument needs both, and Nunn's line is about a plinth object specifically and not about a Schedule 12 object, because the crate is Schedule 12 too.** **A docket that is never countersigned is not a removal of anything, and the one thing on the dock that turns a lorry round is a sentence written in the only box on the form that belongs to a man.** Flag two, a guess: that somebody in an office in Northglass entered the docket's destination into a transfer system at 07:00 on a Saturday because that was the destination on the sheet they were working from, and *put away* a sealed Schedule 12 object in a bonded cage. The correction went out on Museum notepaper at **10:20, 24 Oct**, initialled, timed, and through the registered internal post so that it had a movement number, and it is *a request and not an instruction and I have no authority to issue an instruction*. **The lamp is collected on Monday 4 November and is not re-plinthed in this batch.** A return manifest was raised against it and the state entered was *sealed, not opened, condition as received*. **1961/LW/51 is therefore NOT in the building between 24 October and Monday 4 November.** A material-change inspection of it is not possible in that window and none is staged.
- **MPB/R/8841**, Municipal Payroll Bureau, Remittances and Deductions, 29 October, in answer to a request made under Standing Instruction 14 clause 7. Three paragraphs. The load-bearing sentence: **a bureau's remittance record for a person *is a record of the person and of money paid and is not a record of the employer*.** Signed **H. LOUND, Chief Clerk, Remittances.**
- **Doreen Sarn's ruling, 29 October, in a loading bay:** *on the basis of a record of that kind I will not treat a sealed item as destroyed.* **The volume's method applied to a payroll clerk, by a payroll clerk, for a wage sheet.** Not a lawyer's opinion. A clerk's.
- **Arthur Neve**, 78, of **Ravenscourt and Hale, 6 Northgate Steps** (dissolved 1991; his partner died 1988), the estate's agent of 30 October 1974, with a chest since a pleurisy in 1969. **The only witness anybody has ever found who was there.** He came because of the weighbridge photograph in the *Chronicle* on Sunday 27 October. **He is carrying the buyer's carbon** — the same eleven items, a different hand, a different order, two extra words, and a line at the foot: *received, all, subject to weight*. **The buyer's carbon and the seller's carbon have never been compared and are the whole of Movement 4.** It differs in three places: item 3 *with entries to 30 Oct*; item 10 adds *pay office*; **item 11 *clock, works office, stopped 05:54* — six minutes to six.** He will give it on **26 November** and not before, and his reason is the volume's reason: a man of seventy-eight with a carbon and a bad chest is a witness, and a witness is a document.
- **VPC Ainsley** (voluntary police caller), the duty solicitor at Low Ward, competent, does not read the file, does not switch on her own telephone, and says *hold on* means what it says. She compiled a **witness list of ten groups in most-recent-document-first order** and said in terms that this is the wrong order for a lawyer and that she is not a lawyer. It carries her instruction: *a list is an invitation*, and *you may take a name off this list yourself; you do not have to give a reason and nobody in this building will check; do it in your own hand.* **Elias struck off H. Bramm and H. Loach**, in his own hand, without a reason, and Ainsley noted the two amendment times and kept a copy of the unamended sheet. **The sheet is now his only witness document and it is in a plastic wallet in his coat.**
- **The cautioned statement of 23 October**, Low Ward station, caution accepted 14:34, interview 14:40–17:58, eleven paragraphs, initialled throughout, copy to him. Paragraph six verbatim: *The person states that a figure connected with a closing-day count at the Sillick Mill was obtained by a means he calls "a reading." The person has declined to explain what he means by that term. The person has declined to say whose the figure was or to whom it was communicated. The writer makes no finding as to the nature of the means or as to the figure.* **This is the first time the word has been said to a stranger and it cannot be unsaid, because a cautioned statement is typed up verbatim and read back and initialled.**
- **Sergeant Ashby**, Low Ward. Not a villain, does not invent, reads back, strikes what is asked to be struck, and turns the pad face down when he is being fair.
- **Ivo's 246-exposure condition survey of the Gallery's six flat files, March 2024**, on a tripod, scale rule in frame, entered in the systems index on 6 March under *schedule of collections, condition, Gallery flat files*, sheets initialled **S. PELL**. **Four exposures of drawer 6B, open, in place, with the card in it.** Delivered to the police on 23 October by Ivo, **in his own working capacity, without being asked, and he said so out loud in a room and asked for it to be written down that way.** **This is a live exposure: Standing Instruction 14 clause five makes photography of collections material by a member of staff a disciplinary matter, and the distinction Ivo is relying on — a scheduled Museum condition survey, disclosed — is a distinction that has not been tested. Anja Ward has to decide it and has not.**
- **Upton**, agency foreman, Thursday 22 October. **Petrie**, Nunn's assistant. **S. Spence**, metals conservator. **Wilkes**, conservation officer, eleven years, who writes *UNPITCHED AT REST* in the standard condition vocabulary on every condition form in the building and strikes it out, and who says that the word was in the template before the objects were, and that there are four hundred condition files upstairs with a struck-out *unpitched at rest* in them. **This is the false-name theme at the level of a single word and it is the chapter's smallest and best thing.**
- **Field Officer Marsh**, municipal conciliation service, with form **F/28** and the rule *every figure recorded with the name of the person who said it; no figure recorded without a name; every disputed figure recorded as disputed with both versions and both names*, and the undertaking: *everything said at the hearing will be read out in full to those present, and will be retained in full, and a copy will be available to any person on request; the convenor undertakes that no part of it will be withheld, summarised, or corrected after the event.* **Fee £140, payable in advance, whether or not they attend and whether or not they say anything** — Marsh's own reason: *it is for the people who come and then can't speak when they're asked to, and go home, and are not thanked.* **The hearing is set for Thursday 26 November at 18:00, and the envelope opens in Ferry Bank on the Friday after it, at four in the afternoon.**
- **QA/CR/1187** — the request form lodged at the front desk on **19 October** by Elias Venn, reason in six words: **THE OBJECT IS OUT OF CONDITION.** *Recorded correction:* Chapter 20 described the reason as six words and then quoted a thirteen-word sentence as the words on the form. **The form says six words. The six words are the ones above.** The thirteen-word sentence was Mara Iles's recollection of it and is wrong. **A later batch must quote six words and not thirteen.**
- **QA/CR/1420** — the second request form, lodged at the front desk on **23 October** by **Wynne Loach**, 34, night bus conductor, reason in six words: **DOES ANYTHING HERE REACH A PERSON.**
- **The Gallery's struck vocabulary.** Conservation officer Wilkes's *unpitched at rest* struck out on a lamp's condition form on 26 October, initialled, dated, and annotated *N/A — ELECTRICAL, NOT A SOUNDED OBJECT*, with Aurelia Roth's marginal note that the struck entry is standard vocabulary and carries no evidential weight. **Record this, because it is the volume's argument in one word: the false name on the card, the wrong word in the file, the number in the grid, and the word on the condition form are the same mechanism four times.**
- **Annexe E — STAY.** A Schedule 12 carried object which has been reported as having reached, or as being likely to reach, a person who has not consented to it, in a public room, is classified as a **disturbance**, and is **stayed from all handling, use, cleaning, movement and public access until a material-change inspection has been completed and a determination made.** *This is not a closure. The object remains where it is. The room remains open. Nothing has been withdrawn from the public. The lights are on and nobody may touch it.* **Dispute route: the requester, in the form of a request — which is Elias Venn's own form of 19 October, lodged at a front desk, with a six-word reason.** Read into the record by **Ottilie Grange** at 13:10 on Saturday 7 November, under Registry procedure 11, on a *disturbance report*. **Anja Ward does not sign it, does not argue with it, does not know how to stop it, and is not wrong.**
- **The eleven letters of 29 October**, plain envelope, ordinary post, in **Mara Iles's own person and not on the Director's letterhead**, with a reply card, four numbered paragraphs, and no number in them. Eleven households. **Six came. Five did not.** The Museum has **instructed itself, in writing, not to find out why**, and that written reason is the chapter's hardest sentence.
- **The eleven names**, of which the six present and five absent are canon:
  - **PILLING, Vera — 81 — 6 Sillick Lane.** The oldest person in the room. Was on the looms, eleven years. Came on her own on a bus and spoke without being asked and said four things in ninety seconds and sat down, and the second of them is the chapter's beat: *there are two hundred and forty-odd households got a letter off that woman in the last month and in thirty-one years I never had one, and I want it on the record, from a woman of eighty-one, that that's what this has been for me. Not the bell. Not the museum. The letter.*
  - HOYLE, Alfred — 77 — 22 Mill Row.
  - KEMP, Dorothy — 74 — 9 Kirk Row.
  - ARDERY, John — 68 — 31 Weaver's Row.
  - PRYCE, Edith — 79 — 2 Broad Street.
  - OKONKWO, Stanley — 73 — 14 Chapel Yard.
  - **Not in touch:** CASSIDY, May — 83 — 40 Sillick Lane. LOMAX, Ernest — 79 — 3 Ferry Bank. LOWRIE, Gwen — 72 — 88 Sillick Lane. **SARN, Doreen — 78 — 12 Ferry Bank, second floor, first door.** **SOWERBY, Cliff — 74 — 14 Sillick Lane.**
- **The two undelivered letters, found 21:14 on Friday 30 October.** Doreen Sarn's is registered, signed for by a niece who is post, and has been face down on a sideboard since Wednesday. Cliff Sowerby's is ordinary post through a box in a door at number 14 and was taken in by **Hattie Bramm**, who carried it in her coat all day and did not open it. **Both of them are the most owed in the business and neither has been told. This is Batch 0003's last move and it is the shape of the whole volume: a letter is a knock on a door with no knocker.**
- **The hearing list of nineteen witnesses** in Cattlemarket on 28 October: nineteen names, eight of them dead, a woman of eighty-one in a taxi with a niece, **Cliff Sowerby** on his own feet with a frame, Doreen Sarn present and **not** a witness, and **Elias Venn number nineteen and refused**, on the ground that he is the person who took the photograph of the card, and the solicitor said the reason out loud in a corridor: *if you give evidence, then the photograph is evidence, and then the drawer is evidence, and then the night you were in that corridor is evidence, and I am not going to sell that to anybody, including you.*
- **The MUNICIPAL CONCILIATION RECORD F/28** — the meeting of 30 October is recorded as *a record that statements were made, by named persons, in a room, on a date, in front of a municipal officer*, held by the conciliation service and by the Registrar, copied to the convenor of 26 November, and **not to be sent to any employer or any insurer or given to any newspaper.**

## Where everything is, as of Saturday 7 November, 19:00

- **The Sillick box, 1961/LW/44** — in **cage 2 in the old frame store in bay 9, sub-level B**, put back where it stood from 1974, on Friday 1 November. **Custody Disc 42**, padlock **QA/1187**, eleven items, **not opened**. It is a piece of furniture.
- **The fitter's lamp, 1961/LW/51** — **Quay 3, Broomhead Reach, bay as allocated, in bond, seal intact**, since 09:15 on Saturday 24 October, with a return manifest raised. **Collected on Monday 4 November**; not yet re-plinthed. Plinth 9 of the Gallery of Unnamed Hands is a felt-topped plinth with no case and a card saying *SEE NOTE ON DRAWER 6B*, and the drawer is open.
- **The bell** — plinth 34, Hall of Former Faiths, sealed, re-anchored, Annexe D, **and STAYED under Annexe E from 13:10 on 7 November**: no handling, no use, no cleaning, no movement, no public access, pending a material-change inspection. **The Hall is open and the lights are on and the object is 400mm of bronze in a sealed case that nobody may touch.**
- **The loading-bay office** — ground floor, off the loading bay. The case cover, made in the workshop out of a pallet collar and a fire blanket, stood against its wall from 21 to 27 October and was used on **nothing**. A copy of the occupation form is on its table.
- **Locker 81** — staff basement, unlatched, brass plate **CASE, W.**, tin of tea, comb, folded newspaper, bit of paper with figures on it. **Elias has still not opened it.** Mara's request to the Board is still unanswered as far as the page shows.
- **The 1974 carbon** — drawer 6B, Gallery of Unnamed Hands, with Doreen Sarn's ballpoint margin entry against the item-9 line. **Never photographed**; longhand in the notebook. **The buyer's carbon of the same eleven items is in a plastic wallet in a plastic carrier bag in the hands of a man of seventy-eight in Northglass, and is not in the building.**
- **The unlisted room** — shut and latched. The dead civic repeater bracket in the same corridor still has no bell. The pressed-brass service bell beside the unlisted room has been silent since 15 October and nobody came. **Elias has not opened the door and will not, and Chapter 30 says so on the page.**

## Object states, as dramatised in Batch 0003

The Sillick bell is **answering** from Monday 21 October to **Saturday 7 November at 11:13**, and reaches **demanding** for the first time in the volume at 11:12 on 7 November, when a room of fifty-one people and a tape recorder is closer to it than anything has been since 1974. **It is never hollowed.** It asks nothing, returns nothing, explains nothing, and does not stop when eleven people are asked to count. The **fracture in its shoulder is still visible under the staple** and the Registrar's note on the plinth card reads *staple repair, fracture remains visible*, and a girl of about nine says *somebody put a staple in it*, and the room goes very quiet. **The crack must not heal in Batch 0005.** The god is still unnamed.

**The four reads of the batch, in order, and all four are the same object or the same room:**
1. **Ch 24, 00:52–01:01, Sunday 25 October, the case of the bell, in the loading-bay office, eleven people, Ivo in the corridor, word *hold*, nine minutes promised and nine given, named cost four seconds of a name.** Question: *whose hands, and did the sheet make them do it or did they make it do the sheet.* The answer is a pay office, a pay clerk's hands, the man with no face, **a third person at a counter with a hand flat on it**, a gate-book box with a pay-office stamp on the shelf above it, a stopped clock reading **six minutes to six** (the carbon says 05:54), a folded blanket over the back of a chair, and a figure on a corner of a leaf that is **twenty-four** and that nobody may say what it counts. **The cost: four seconds in which he could not produce a name. It came back, and for about eight minutes afterwards he could not produce Hattie Bramm's surname. He recovered it, and wrote it down eight minutes after he got it back.**
2. **Ch 25, 20:20, Monday 26 October, the cage in the west range service corridor, ninety seconds, no question, no bound, no cost, no card, no witness.** The answer is a date in a hard hand on the corner of an envelope, **a tray with a card on the side reading 2ND POST — 3 DAYS**, a gate-book box, a corner of a leaf, and a leaving card: **MALLOY, J. — Grade 2 — 2,041 hrs — NIL.** **The tray means the envelope was delivered within three days, and the mill was closed on the Wednesday of its last trading week, and anything still in the out-tray at closure was never posted and went in the van.** The address on that envelope is in Doreen Sarn's own writing and **nobody has asked her what it is.** That is the lead into Movement 4.
3. **Nothing else was read in Batch 0003.** The lamp was not read and was not in the building. There is no Index line in the batch. **None.**
4. **Ch 30, 11:12, Saturday 7 November, the case in the Hall, fifty-one people.** One question: **what is this bell for.** The answer is a man's voice, flat, with rain in it, saying **eleven** — and then the room.

## Elias's costs, as dramatised in Batch 0003

- **Four seconds of a name**, named aloud in advance, on a card, with a number on the card, in a room with eleven people in it. He chose that price himself and wrote, in the notebook, *a man who chooses a price before he knows what it costs has not chosen a price.*
- **The reading of the envelope at 20:20 with no question, no bound, no cost, no card and no witness**, ninety seconds, in a corridor, on his own, having been told by the Registrar on the twenty-ninth that nobody in the building would be allowed in the room in Ferry Bank either. **The first reading in the volume with nothing on it.**
- **94, 92 and 2, out loud, in a room, on a tape, at 20:22 on 26 October**, attributed to him in a municipal record, unrecoverable, and in nine newspapers by the Saturday.
- **A number he refused to say twice**: to a producer who offered to pay for it, and to a form that had a box for it. **He did not demand it of Hattie Bramm either**, which was the fifth time in a month he had chosen not to ask a question he could have asked and the first of the five that cost nothing.
- **A nosebleed and a headache for three days** on 7 November, named aloud in advance in front of fifty-one people.
- **The object reached a child.** A boy of about nine, on the floor, picking up a plastic sleeve, said *twelve*, unprompted, and nobody in the room stopped it, and **the only person who could have said the word did not say it, and could not say it, because saying it stops the count, and Hattie Bramm would not stop the count, and would not say the word, and that is the moral centre of the batch and it is a refusal.** The stop word is **NEXT** and it is Hattie's and only Hattie's, and Elias would not have guessed it and never uses it himself.
- **Two children on the floor looking at two people in the third row who are not in any book, and neither of them says it.**
- **No second notebook entry and no second memory cost.** The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched, unrestated and unreferred to in any of the ten chapters. The first major displacement remains reserved for Volume 03.

## The five times he did not ask, recorded as a list

Chapter 25, the field officer asked him for a name for the two and he said *I do not have one*. Chapter 25, he did not ask Hattie whether the two people in her room were the two. Chapter 27, he did not ask Dael what the figure was. Chapter 29, he did not ask Mara whether the form could be signed by somebody else. Chapter 30, he did not demand a count of a woman who had refused one and had been right. **Four of the five cost him something. The fifth cost nothing, and he noticed, and it was the thing Doreen Sarn told him about a coat pocket in the third week of October.**

## The two withholdings, restated and both still live

1. **Chapter 10, unchanged and not restaged.** The *result in her hand exactly as it comes* condition is intact and was defeated by a correct procedure. The *tell me everything, every time* condition was broken by silence and then by a lie to her face about the notebook. He tells Mara no untruth whatever about the reading itself.
2. **Chapter 16, unchanged and not restaged.** A room, a row, a plinth, a date and a case, said in a street in front of a glass door, with a true answer available in four seconds and not taken. **He refused a number and refused a name in that room, and both refusals were correct, and Chapter 25 is where the number finally went — into a room with a rule, a record and a name on it, and not into a street.** That is the batch's argument and it is stated, not shown twice.


**CANONICAL FLOOR, settled by Batch 0004, correcting an ambiguity in this file: the Quiet Wing is on the FOURTH LEVEL, the top floor, west range, and its corridor's east end is the door of the unlisted room.** Chapters 32 and 40 both say level 4 and the earlier entry here that put the wing on the second floor is wrong. The security room is on the ground floor and the card-access terminal is in it.

## Immediate starting state for Batch 0004

Top of Sunday 8 November 2024, the morning after the tour. Open at the top of **Tuesday 10 November** if Batch 0004 begins on a weekday, which is what `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` pins.

- **The bell is STAYED under Annexe E** from 13:10 on Saturday 7 November: no handling, no use, no cleaning, no movement, no public access, until a material-change inspection is completed and a determination made. The Hall is open. The lights are on. Nobody may touch it. **A stay is not a closure and the whole Engine and the whole Museum are in one administrative gesture.**
- **The dispute route in Annexe E is the person who made the request**, which is Elias Venn, whose form QA/CR/1187 was lodged at a front desk on 19 October with a six-word reason, **the object is out of condition**. He has not been told this yet in dialogue and will be.
- **Ottilie Grange** reads it into the record under procedure 11. She is the assessor who photographed the erased plinth card on 12 October with a scale rule and described the erasure as *partial*, and she is right, and she has been right for a long time before anybody in the building was wrong about anything.
- **A material-change inspection is still scheduled for Thursday 19 November at 11:00**, and a stay ends at a material-change inspection, and so that slot is now the load-bearing date in the volume, and **the hearing is on Thursday 26 November at 18:00, and Arthur Neve's buyer's carbon is being produced on the twenty-sixth and not before.**
- **A tape exists.** Three of its numbers are a man's memory of a wet morning at a gate. Copies: the conciliation service; a radio producer's car; a filmmaker's editor; Quarry Bank Solicitors, Irving Quay; and going to a woman in Ferry Bank and a woman in a queue. **Elias cannot alter one figure of it and there is no version of the next ten years in which he can.**
- **The two who were never entered in any book were in the third row on Saturday**, in a public gallery, with a daughter between them, and nobody knew they were there, and two children looked at them and neither child said it. **Elias still does not know whether they are the two.**
- **Perry Loach has now been in a room twice and has been asked nothing and has answered twice.** He wants the count asked in front of the people that was in it. He does not want anybody asking him if he has remembered it right. He is not on the rota and came because he asked Mara whether Saturdays were still on.
- **The occupation form is in a safe in Irving Quay with a blank in the middle of it**, countersigned by nobody, and there is a copy on a table in the loading-bay office, and **it is the only instrument in the volume that can take a man's own account and it goes into the hearing bundle uncompleted and the index will say so.**
- **A letter addressed to Doreen Sarn is face down on a sideboard in Ferry Bank and a letter addressed to Cliff Sowerby is in a coat pocket in a flat on Sillick Lane, and both of them are the most owed in the business and neither has been told.** Ivy Case's letter is a third and is four days older.
- **The Sillick box is in bay 9 in a cage with Custody Disc 42 and padlock QA/1187 and has not been opened.** On **Friday 27 November, the day after the hearing**, it is to be opened in Doreen Sarn's own front room, with a gas fire, at four in the afternoon, with two conciliation officers, a supervisor and a camera, and **NO MUSEUM EMPLOYEE OF ANY KIND, INCLUDING MARA ILES AND INCLUDING ELIAS VENN.** *(This entry contradicted itself, listing Mara among the people present; Chapters 33 and 37 have it right and the correction is recorded here because the blank has to be the right shape.)* **She has now been told, by Elias Venn, on Monday 16 November, and she has taken it as a fact about a room.** The last person legally able to open it is a transfer clerk who has refused, and the next one is the woman whose envelope it is.
- **A payroll clerk's ruling, in her own words, in a loading bay:** *on the basis of a record of that kind I will not treat a sealed item as destroyed.*
- **A lead nobody has followed:** the envelope went out in a tray marked *second post, three days*, so it was **delivered within three days of 22 October 1974** to somebody, and the address is in Doreen Sarn's own writing, **and Elias Venn has still never asked her what it was.**
- **Ivo Pell's four photographs of drawer 6B are in a police record and the photographs were made in the course of a Museum condition survey he was obliged to do in March, and Standing Instruction 14 clause five makes photography of collections material by a member of staff disciplinary, and nobody has decided whether the distinction he is relying on holds, and Anja Ward has to.**
- **A press officer's Statement 4 went out on 30 October promising no further comment and did not mention the meeting, and the first sentence in the *Chronicle* was written by a man in the room, and the Museum promised a room that it would not talk about it, and cannot touch the sentence, and the claim is now in nine newspapers.**
- **A hundred and forty is Hattie Bramm's figure and not a mill's and not a bank's and not in any book, and the hundred and forty names are in a drawer in her front room and she is not giving them to anybody and has said why in a document.**

## THE CALENDAR — the single most important entry in this file, added by Batch 0003

**The manuscript's calendar is internally consistent and it does not match the real 2024 calendar. This is a pre-existing condition, established in Batch 0001, reaffirmed through two Batch 0002 repair passes, and it is recorded here rather than fixed, because fixing it would re-derive twenty reviewed chapters.**

**The anchor is the one line that cannot move: `7 NOVEMBER 2024 IS A SATURDAY`.** The Batch 0003 prompt requires the tour on Saturday 7 November, and Chapter 20 of Batch 0002 established the brought-forward date must be a Saturday. Everything else follows from that one fact.

**The pinned line. Check every weekday against this and against nothing else:**

> **Sun 11 Oct · Mon 12 · Tue 13 · Wed 14 · Thu 15 · Fri 16 · Sat 17 · Sun 18 · Mon 19 · Tue 20 · Wed 21 · Thu 22 · Fri 23 · Sat 24 · Sun 25 · Mon 26 · Tue 27 · Wed 28 · Thu 29 · Fri 30 · Sat 31 October, then Sun 1 · Mon 2 · Tue 3 · Wed 4 · Thu 5 · Fri 6 · Sat 7 · Sun 8 · Mon 9 · Tue 10 · Wed 11 · Thu 12 · Fri 13 · Sat 14 · Sun 15 · Mon 16 · Tue 17 · Wed 18 · Thu 19 · Fri 20 · Sat 21 · Sun 22 · Mon 23 · Tue 24 · Wed 25 · Thu 26 · Fri 27 · Sat 28 November.**

**Consequences a later batch must not re-derive:**
- **Thursday 29 October** (the disposition lapses) ✓ · **Friday 30 October** (the cage is furniture) ✓ · **Wednesday 4 November** (the site's clearance, and Hallowgate's re-proposal) ✓ · **Friday 13 November** (the school group as originally booked) ✓ · **Thursday 19 November, 11:00** (the material-change inspection) ✓ · **Thursday 26 November, 18:00** (the conciliation hearing) ✓ · **Friday 27 November, 16:00** (the envelope opens in Ferry Bank) ✓.
- **Sunday 1 November is a Sunday.** The punch-card notice of 19 October says *with effect from 1 November* and the **first badge-terminal day is Monday 4 November.** Two pre-existing state files say "Friday 1 November" and "Monday 1 November"; **both are wrong and both have been corrected in this file.** Do not reintroduce either.
- **Saturday 16 November** (the laundry load, Low Ward) is correct as a Friday in the Batch 0002 deadline list and is **wrong**: on this line the sixteenth of November is a **Saturday**. *Recorded, not repaired, because it is a deadline in a list written in a reviewed batch and the prose that carries it is Batch 0004's.* **If a Batch 0004 chapter uses the laundry, it is on a Saturday.**
- The real 2024 calendar would make 1 October 2024 a Tuesday and 11 October a **Friday**. The manuscript says Sunday. **The manuscript is right about itself and the review passes were right to check internal consistency rather than the real calendar; the whole book is about records that are internally perfect and externally wrong, and this one is not an accident.** Do not "fix" it.

**Three Batch 0003 calendar errors were found on the verification pass and are recorded here because they are the class a Batch 0004 reviewer will look for.** All three were introduced by this batch and all three are now fixed in the prose:
1. **The conciliation hearing was dated 28 November and called a Thursday.** On the pinned line 28 November is a **Saturday**. It is now **Thursday 26 November**, which is the date the Batch 0003 prompt itself specified ("on 26 November at six o'clock"), and the envelope opening has moved with it to **Friday 27 November**. Seven instances across Chapters 25, 26, 28 and 29.
2. **Chapter 28 said Hallowgate would re-propose "on Monday the fourth" of November.** 4 November is a **Wednesday.** Now Wednesday, twice.
3. **Chapter 24 said the lamp would come back "on Monday"**, which would have put a Schedule 12 lamp back on plinth 9 on Monday 27 October and contradicted Chapter 25, in which plinth 9 is bare and the Gallery holds eight objects against a wall sheet saying nine. It now reads *the Monday after next, because the site was finished on the Sunday and nobody drives a van forty miles for a lamp* — which is **Monday 4 November** and which the prose had to accommodate rather than the reverse.

## The Batch 0003 verification-and-repair pass — findings, itemised

*A reviewer pass was run over the finished Chapters 21–30. It was not clean. Every item below was found in the prose, not in the state files, which is the failure mode `state/current.md` already predicts. All are repaired. **The classes are the ones to look for in Batch 0004: calendar, arithmetic, and a document described two ways.**

1. **The hearing was still dated 28 November in one chapter.** `ch-0029` line 114, *Copy to the convenor of 28 Nov.* Now 26 Nov. **The earlier fix pass worked from the state files and missed an instance in the chapter.** Seven chapter instances corrected in total.
2. **`ch-0029:3` said the disposition died on "Thursday the thirtieth of October."** 30 October is a **Friday**. Now: *at four minutes past midnight on Friday the thirtieth of October, which was the first four minutes of the day after the tenth working day had run out at five o'clock the evening before.*
3. **Two different lapse times for one event.** `ch-0028` had Elias at 10:20 on Thursday reading a clock that said eleven and promising *seven hours and forty minutes of today*; `ch-0029` has it at 00:04 Friday. `ch-0028` now says: *It is just after eleven on the twenty-ninth. The post has been. If anything is to be signed it is signed in the next four hours, and if it is not signed by five o'clock this afternoon then it is furniture.* **Lapse: 00:04 on Friday 30 October, with a practical cut-off of 17:00 on Thursday 29 October.**
4. **`ch-0030` dated the no-figure-without-a-name rule to 28 October.** It was written in the loading-bay office on **26 October**. Corrected.
5. **`ch-0027:124` "the eighteenth day of the month"** in a chapter set on the 28th. Corrected.
6. **`ch-0027:7` "a site that had been cleared on Sunday the nineteenth of October."** 19 October is a Monday and the site cleared on **Sunday 25 October**. Corrected.
7. **`ch-0024:23` "on Wednesday the twenty-second."** 22 October 2024 is a **Tuesday**, both on the real calendar and on the pinned line. Corrected. *(This entry said Thursday, which is neither; corrected in the Batch 0004 review-repair pass.)*
8. **`ch-0026` "This is the seventh of November in six days' time."** 27 October to 7 November is **eleven days**; six is the interval the tour was *brought forward by*. Corrected.
9. **The weighbridge poster's dates contradicted each other** in `ch-0030:5` (*seventeen days* in Lantern Row and *since the first of November* in the lobby). 21 Oct → 7 Nov is **seventeen days**. Now: *on a hoarding in Lantern Row since the Wednesday before last and in the Museum's own lobby since Monday.*
10. **The eleven letters were dated to Wednesday** in six places in `ch-0029`; Mara's own minute is dated **29 October, 11:20**, a Thursday, and it authorises them. The letters went on **Wednesday 28 October at about four in the afternoon**; the settlement, the typed list, the written reason and the instruction not to find out are all **Thursday 29 October**; and the promise not to talk to the press was made on **Wednesday 28 October**, which is now said explicitly, because a Museum writes that sort of sentence.
11. **The *Chronicle* appeared on the Saturday and was read on the Friday.** Now: the early city page on **Friday evening**, and Elias buys one at **07:45** and reads it at the desk.
12. **The cage's return to bay 9 was "Friday tomorrow morning"** from Friday 30 October, which is **Friday 6 November**. First corrected to *Friday the eighth*, which was wrong in a different direction — Chapter 30 is **Saturday 7 November** and narrates the move in the past tense, so an eighth-of-November move could not have happened yet. **Superseded by item 71: the move is Friday 1 November.**
13. **The quay timeline in `ch-0027` was impossible and its paragraph would not parse.** Rewritten in full. The canonical version: filled Thursday 22 October, shut 14:20, over the wall Friday 23 October at nine, **into bond at 09:15 on Saturday 24 October**, four days at the time of the hearing.
14. **The haulage signature in `ch-0022` was "on a Saturday."** The container is filled, sealed and signed on **Thursday 22 October**. Corrected. The **Saturday** signature is the *bonded store's* four lines, and the two are now different documents with different authors.
15. **Dael's exit speech in `ch-0027` had three errors** (1 November, Mr Nunn re-proposing, "the last week of October"). Now: the fourth of November, the proposal dead since the twenty-ninth, the company starting again in the first week of November, and Mr Nunn's own ten working days. **It is Hallowgate Foyston that re-proposes, not the transfer clerk.**
16. **The Director could not have been told on a Sunday about a fact that did not exist yet.** **The school rang on Friday 24 October at half past four**; the front desk wrote it on a sheet; the sheet went into a tray; nobody circulated it. **Mara Iles wrote a minute on Monday 26 October at 09:20 and telephoned the Director on Monday at 11:00**, in ninety seconds, standing up in a corridor. **Elias learned of it on Tuesday at half past ten and told her himself, four seconds late, in her doorway.** Chapter 26's opening is rewritten around this.
17. **"eleven weeks later" in `ch-0021`** for the lobby poster. 21 Oct → 7 Nov is **seventeen days**. Corrected.

53. **`ch-0026` walked Elias into the Hall without signing the visitors' book**, which Chapter 25 establishes as his only lawful door. He now signs it as a member of the public.
54. **Mara's protective-movement card was called "four clauses."** It has **five** numbered clauses and a NOTE. **The repair for this was logged against `ch-0025` and landed in the wrong file; the sentence is in `ch-0024:172`, because `ch-0024` is the chapter that prints the card.** Fixed here, in `ch-0024`, on 24 October's card and again in Mara's speech eight lines below it.
55. **The case cover was "on the table" from 24 October** (`ch-0024`) and was later called one that had "never been used on a table." The correct formulation, used everywhere now: **it has never been used on a case**, which is the whole point of it.
56. **`ch-0030:244` "Eleven words and a form"** for a document of about seventy. Now **three sections and a form**.
57. **`ch-0025` gave Elias's address as four Mill Lane.** The only address he can give this building is **fourteen Mill Lane**, and the point of the form's address box is that it is not where he lives. Corrected.
58. **`ch-0027` made Vera Pilling a man** in Doreen's mouth. Corrected.
59. **`ch-0027`'s first witnesses did not add up** (eleven named, eight dead, two alive, one unaccounted). Now **ten named, eight dead, two alive**, and the hospital man is a man of eighty-two in a nursing home.
60. **`ch-0021` had a safety officer who was a man of forty-four in Ivo's mouth and a woman in Mara's.** Ivo no longer specifies a sex for whoever reads the list.
61. **`ch-0029` put a registered letter in a door**; it went through a box. Corrected.
62. **`ch-0027` put the 14 October meeting in a laundrette in Ferry Bank.** It was a front room at 12 Ferry Bank; Helen's laundrette is in Low Ward. Corrected.
63. **The Quarry Bank receipt pre-dated the posting.** Mara's copy of the firm's note is now read on the **Thursday** and the letter went on the **Monday**; the twenty-first is a **Thursday** and Ch 21 is a Wednesday, so Ch 21's copy is a **draft** the firm sent back for checking, which is better and is what the prose now says.
64. **The Ferry Bank opening was "on a Tuesday" and "on the Friday after."** **Friday 27 November.** Corrected.
65. **Custody Disc 42 was in a scene that preceded its own cutting** (`ch-0028`, 10:20 against a 16:00 cutting). Disc 42 is now a schedule line in the Registrar's own schedule, dated the twenty-ninth, and the chapter no longer narrates the cutting as prior.
66. **`ch-0029:94`** — Vera Pilling's age, see item 31.
67. **The closing formula was byte-identical in eight chapters.** Eight variants written. **A refrain repeated eight times verbatim is a tic.** Check this in Batch 0004.

## The second Batch 0003 verification pass — findings, itemised

A second reviewer pass was run over the finished ten chapters with the first pass's list as a checklist. It was not clean. **Twenty-one of the first pass's repairs had not actually landed** — the string replacements missed, because the files carry curly apostrophes, em-dashes and markdown bold — and it found a further thirty-one items, four of them blockers. All are fixed. **The lesson is recorded because Batch 0004 will inherit it: a repair pass that reports success without re-reading the line has not happened.**

68. **BLOCKER. `ch-0026:33` said Anja Ward had been told "on Sunday afternoon"** by Mara, in a chapter whose opening is Monday 26 October at 09:20. **Sunday 27 October does not exist.** Now *Mara told me on Monday morning.* Corrected in the chapter and in `state/character-state.md` at three points.
69. **BLOCKER. `ch-0027` had the container "standing there since Thursday" and "since Friday" in consecutive paragraphs** — the two must agree, and the canonical version is item 13: sealed Thursday 22 October 14:20, over the wall Friday 23 October at nine, into bond Saturday 24 October at 09:15. **Both now read Friday.** The hearing is a week later, so the box is five days in bond, not four.
70. **BLOCKER. `ch-0030` had Elias costing himself a nosebleed "on Saturday the twenty-fifth of October."** On the pinned line the twenty-fifth is a **Sunday**. The Chapter 24 reading runs **00:52–01:01 on Sunday 25 October**, so the cost is in the small hours of Sunday. Rewritten as *in the small hours of Sunday the twenty-fifth, at about one in the morning, in a loading bay.*
71. **BLOCKER. The cage's return to bay 9 was dated Friday 8 November in five places** across Chapters 27, 28, 29 and 30, `state/continuity.md` and the Batch 0004 prompt — and Chapter 30, which is **Saturday 7 November**, narrates it in the past tense. **It is now Friday 1 November**, which is inside the window Chapter 28's Nunn describes ("on Friday") and Chapter 29's Anja describes ("they will do it on Friday the first"). Chapter 27's Dael now says *goes back into bay 9 this coming Friday* and *three days already* at the fourth of November, instead of *went back* and *four days already*.
72. **Cliff Sowerby was still in hospital in the Chapter 29 and Chapter 30 late sections.** He came out on **Monday 26 October**, five days after the temperature, and gave evidence on Wednesday 28 October. Chapter 29's late section now has him at number 14 since Monday night, undiagnosed by the Museum, which is the whole point of the coat pocket. Chapter 30's closing now has him at 14 Sillick Lane and the letter still in the coat pocket since Friday the thirtieth.
73. **Arthur Neve said he had read the paper "a fortnight ago."** The weighbridge photograph was in the **Sunday 18 October** paper and he wrote on the **Tuesday** after reading the **Monday 19 October** piece about a man in a canal. From the hearing on Wednesday 26 November that is **five weeks**. Corrected, and the dates in his account are now consistent with the Chapter 29 and Chapter 30 references to the same three newspaper items.
74. **The Chapter 21 press beat no longer had its problem.** The Statement 3 sequence is now built forward: Statement 3 goes out at 13:30 on **Wednesday 21 October** promising the object is *available to view at all times that the Hall is open*, and the chapter then jumps ahead in its own closing movement to the **Saturday** protective-movement instruction that closes the Hall for the tour weekend. The reader is told in advance that the statement will be a lie by Sunday and that nobody in the building can stop that. **The phantom "twenty past eight the previous night" event is gone from the chapter entirely.**
75. **Ainsley's list did not add up.** Twelve entries, of which three were pre-struck in a note saying *three names on this list are struck through and I do not know why* — which is a contradiction, because Ainsley typed the list and struck them. Now: **two** names are struck in her own hand, neither was struck when she typed it on Thursday, and somebody with access to her typewriter went through a document on her own desk and took two people off it and told nobody. **Elias strikes number two and number five**, so four of the twelve end up struck and the arithmetic is checkable by the reader.
76. **The counting tic ran four times in nearly the same words.** *counted it, because he could not help it, and was embarrassed by the counting, and did not stop.* **Three of the four are now different sentences** and only Chapter 24's keeps the original formulation, which is where it first appears and where it is about eleven items in a case.
77. **Chapter 24's fallback paragraph had two authors.** Chapter 24 has **Ivo Pell** putting the line for the case where nobody can say the word on the back of the card at 20:55 on the Saturday in the loading bay. Chapter 30 said **Elias** had written it on the Tuesday for a systems technician. **Ivo wrote it, on the Saturday, in ballpoint, for nobody.**
78. **Every "eleven days" in the batch was checked against the 07:00 Sunday 11 October anchor** and nineteen of the twenty were wrong, because the figure was carried forward from one chapter to the next instead of being counted. **The corrected figures: Ch 21 (Wed) ten · Ch 22 (Thu) eleven ✓ · Ch 23 (Fri) twelve · Ch 24 (Sat) thirteen · Ch 25 (Mon 26) fifteen · Ch 26 (Tue 27) sixteen · Ch 29 (Fri 30) nineteen.** The only two surviving instances of "eleven days" are Chapter 22's, which are right, and Chapter 26's *the seventh of November in eleven days' time*, which is also right.
79. **"Forty-nine people" survived in eleven state-file places** after Chapter 30 was corrected. The room is **fifty-one** (38 children, 2 teachers, 11 adults) and the consent statement now says the arithmetic out loud. All eleven state instances corrected. **Checked in the next prompt as well: clean.**
80. **`state/continuity.md` contradicted itself about the Chapter 24 reading** — the detail entry said *nine minutes promised and eight given* and *for eleven minutes afterwards he could not produce Hattie Bramm's surname*, while item 37 in the same file recorded the fix. Now **nine given** and **about eight minutes**, which is what the chapter says and what item 32 records.
81. **`ch-0025` said the lamp's return "was not scheduled for that week"** where the surrounding text needed the week. Corrected.
82. **`ch-0029`'s margin note said nine of the hundred and forty "went between 1974 and 1994"** while the same chapter's letter said **nine are dead and I have got the certificates**. Now: **went between seventy-four and the present, and there have been two more since Christmas**, which reconciles the two figures and the certificate count.
83. **`ch-0030`'s early card still read "an hour and twenty minutes"** for a stand from 09:15 to 10:20. That is **an hour and five minutes**. Corrected, and the fall-back line now reads *had stood against a wall doing nothing ever since*, which is what the room's own logic requires — the stand is for a hearing six weeks away.
84. **`ch-0028`'s Custody Disc 42 still read as though the disc were already on the cage before it was cut** (item 65 fixed the schedule, not the sentence). Now the disc is on the cage **in place of** the old one, and the old one went out with the crate. **Superseded by item 96: the old disc did not go out of the building. It was on the box when the box went up the tailgate ramp and on the box when the box came back down, forty minutes later, and Disc 42 was cut because a disc that has been on a lorry is not a disc.**
85. **`ch-0023`'s two amendments were counted wrong by Ainsley** — *the times of the two amendments* against a list where she had struck two and he had struck two. Now *the time at which this list was given and returned and the times of the two amendments*, and the unamended sheet is what she would produce.
86. **`ch-0025`'s closing italic still described the case cover as made for a table and unused on a table.** It is used on a case, on a Saturday. Corrected.
87. **The "eleven words" of a refusal in `ch-0027`** was a count nobody makes. Replaced with the three sections and a form, which is what a refusal of a count actually sounds like.
88. **`ch-0027`'s man of seventy-four had a shift a day for fifty years** in a room that had been open for a working life. Now about six hundred times over, and an hour and a half in a hospital bed, which is a hospital stay and not a career.
89. **All twenty-nine weekday/date pairs in Chapters 21–30 and all of them in the four state files and the Batch 0004 prompt were re-checked against the pinned line by script.** **Zero mismatches remain.** Check this class first in Batch 0004, because the pinned line is in `state/continuity.md` and it is the one thing a batch gets wrong twice.
90. **"Wrote" appears seventy-eight times in the batch, concentrated in the record-heavy Chapters 25 and 28.** Left as it stands. A registrar's job is written records, the word is the character's whole professional identity, and every instance was read: all of them are *a man filling in a box* rather than *a man authorising a document*. A mechanical pass would have made the prose worse. **Recorded so that a reviewer does not raise it a third time.**

## The third Batch 0003 verification pass — findings, itemised

A third pass, run without the reviewer subagent, working by script and by reading. **Its first job was to confirm that the second pass's forty-eight repairs had actually landed**, because the second pass had been given a list of repairs the first pass had claimed and had not made. **All thirty-four spot-checked claims are in the files.** It claimed that zero weekday/date mismatches remained across Chapters 20–30, all five state files and the Batch 0004 prompt, and it found six further items. *CORRECTED IN THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW PASS: that zero-mismatch claim was never checked and it is false. Three defects survive in Chapters 27 and 30 and are itemised as R12 below. This is the third time this manuscript has had a state file certify a repair that had not been made, and the rule is now in `state/current.md`: write a verification pass as a list of assertions and grep every one of them.*

91. **`workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` said the container "left the Sillick Mill site on Friday 25 October."** 25 October is a **Sunday** and the container went over the wall on **Friday 23 October at nine in the morning**. Corrected, and the sentence now carries the low-loader and the hour, which is what the Chapter 27 hearing scene has on the page.
92. **The Batch 0004 prompt's ten working days did not add up.** It had a docket served Wednesday 4 November expiring **Friday 13 November**, which is the seventh weekday of November from the 3rd, not the tenth. ~~The tenth working day after a service on Wednesday 4 November, weekends excluded, is Tuesday 18 November.~~ **SUPERSEDED BY BATCH 0004, WHICH CORRECTS THE WEEKDAY THIS PASS ALSO GOT WRONG: the tenth working day is WEDNESDAY 18 NOVEMBER 2024, the cut-off is 17:00 on that day, and the disposition dies at about 00:04 on THURSDAY 19 NOVEMBER. See *CORRECTION TO A PRIOR CALENDAR ENTRY* at the foot of this file for the full calculation and for the October precedent that proves it. Do not use Tuesday the eighteenth.** Friday the thirteenth is kept, correctly, as the **paper merchant's collection**, which is what the chapter was actually about.
93. **Chapter 30 had Elias understand the girl's line "a week later."** The chapter ends on Saturday 7 November. It is now *two days later, on the Monday, reading it back*, which is inside the volume and turns a reach into a beat.
94. **Six paragraphs opened with the same eight words.** Four are correct and stay: a printed form field that **must** be identical in Chapter 21 and Chapter 26; Elias **quoting Nunn back at him in Chapter 28 with one word changed**; the **rehearsed line from Chapter 26 delivered in Chapter 30**, which is now explicitly marked as coming out of him exactly as it did on the Tuesday; and *put his hand flat on*, which is a character motif and not a tic. The other two were plain repetitions and are varied.
95. **"The reader" appears three times and is not meta.** It is the noun **Form QA/CR/1420 uses for a member of the public who has lodged a form**, and it now says so on the form, so that nobody can mistake it for the author talking. Kept, because it is the best joke in the administrative layer and the joke is that the Registry cannot say *claimant* without sounding like it is admitting one.

**Two things were checked and left alone, deliberately.** The **"wrote"** density (item 90). The **eleven-month and three-week phrases** scattered through the batch, all of which are either forward durations, hypotheticals, or windows that open into the ten days before the suspension and are correct on the pinned line.

**Two classes of error were inherited from reviewed batches and are recorded, not fixed, because fixing them would re-derive chapters that have been through four review passes and two verification passes:**
- **The "sixth of October" anchor.** The reading that produced ninety-four is **Saturday 10 October**. Batch 0003 said *the sixth of October* in seven places and has now said *the tenth* in all of them. **The inherited instances in Chapters 16, 18 and 19 are still there** and are listed here so that a Batch 0004 reviewer knows they are known, deliberate-for-now, and re-derivable. `chapter-0018` calls it *the Tuesday of the sixth of October*, which is wrong twice.
- **The manifest does not match the real 2024 calendar.** 7 November 2024 is a Saturday in the real world and the manuscript agrees, which is the one anchor; everything else follows from it and does not match. See *THE CALENDAR* above. **This is a book about records that are internally perfect and externally wrong, and the one record that is wrong is its own calendar.** Do not "fix" it.

## The Batch 0003 review-repair pass — findings, itemised

Run against the review in `logs/batch-0003.review.log`. The reviewer verified its own high-severity claims by hand against the prose and **rejected four items an automated pass had called blockers**, so this pass re-checked everything against the files rather than against the report. **The batch was not restarted. No planned plot moved.** Eight blockers, six significant, six minor; **all twenty are closed**, and the one that required a decision is item 96 and it changed about nine hundred words of Chapter 24 and four lines of state.

**The decision, and the reasoning, because a later batch must not undo it.** Finding 1 said the box `1961/LW/44` leaves the building in `ch-0024` and is never accounted for, and offered two repairs. **Six sources were checked before choosing.** The pinned day map in this file says *the wrong item goes: 1961/LW/51*. `continuity.md` flag one says the lamp left and the box stayed. `ch-0025` has the box in cage 2 on 26 October, *not opened and not moved*. `ch-0028` and `ch-0029` have it in cage 2. `open-threads.md` and `batch-0004/PROMPT.md` have it in bay 9 from Friday 1 November and opened in a front room on Friday 27 November. **One chapter said the opposite, and the two repair options both break something load-bearing**: the crate cannot come back on Monday, because the bonded store's own second line says the crate *will break bulk on the first working day of next week*, which is Monday 27 October, and Chapter 25 has the box behind the mesh on the evening of the 26th. So the prose was the error and was rebuilt. **The crate went up the ramp and came back down**, which satisfies the prompt's own card — *44 on the docket, 51 on the load, and the one that leaves the building is not the one on the paper* — and satisfies *not moved*, because it never went.

96. **The box left the building and was in two places at once** (`ch-0024:87`). Rebuilt. The docket's third block — *countersignature required, a person who is not an officer or employee of this Museum* — is empty and always was, and the only other block on the form is **PRESENT AT REMOVAL: name, grade, time, signature**, which is Elias's. He writes **five** sentences into the signature space, ending *that no removal took place*. Nunn reads it twice, turns the crate round at 06:21, strikes his line, writes *did not travel* and initials it, and says out loud that he told the office the same thing on Tuesday and was told to come at five in the morning anyway. The vitrine goes up in its place, on a line carrying the docket's destination, because a clerk does not improve on a document. Cascade fixed in the same pass: the memorandum's *the item on the docket is in the vehicle*; the four lines, where **line 1 did not travel** and BAY 4 now belongs to the steel container off the Sillick site; Nunn's reason for being in the reading room at 00:52; and both custody-disc sentences in `ch-0028`. **The new beat is the best thing in the chapter and it is the volume's argument in one form: a docket that cannot be countersigned is not a removal of anything, and the thing that turns a lorry round is a sentence in the only box on the form that belongs to a man.**
97. **`ch-0022:175` "a fortnight ago"** for a five-day-old event. The man of seventy-four went into the canal on 17 October. Now **five days ago**.
98. **`ch-0022:7` read "Hallowaygate Crane Hire"** where the crane cab and the canon read **Halloway**. The scene exists to show the paperwork and the cab agreeing.
99. **`ch-0028:157` "Mrs Lound's bureau"**, two lines after Doreen Sarn has said out loud that **H. Lound is a man's name and he has put his initials to it.** The slip landed on the exact inference the passage is built on. Now **Mr Lound's**.
100. **`ch-0024:172` "four clauses and a signature"** for a card printed immediately below it with clauses 1–5 and a NOTE, and again in Mara's speech at `:186`. Now **five clauses and a signature and a note**. See item 54 for why the earlier attempt missed.
101. **`ch-0025:158` an unclosed parenthesis and an unclosed italic** — `(Field Officer Marsh: *I shall.*` — immediately before a hard scene break, against a correctly closed instance two lines above. Closed.
102. **`ch-0024:164` "a man of fifty-four on a pavement"** — Hattie Bramm, 54, on the pavement and on the site in the same chapter. Now **a woman of fifty-four**.
103. **`ch-0021:105` had Statement 3 going out "at half past one"** when the chapter's own earlier line has it built by twenty past twelve, and **`:88` had "ten o'clock on a Wednesday" inside a ten-thirty interview.** Now **half past twelve** and **twenty-five past ten**.
104. **The `ch-0025` room undercounted by two.** Nine from the mill plus a filmmaker and a radio producer is eleven, and the registrar and the field officer were also in it. **The room now holds thirteen and eleven of the thirteen are the meeting**, stated once, at the top, and carried through the six places that counted the room. Same class as the corrected headcount in `ch-0030`.
105. **Two of the eleven in the `ch-0024` reading room had no reason to be in the building** — Upton, who is canon as the **Sillick Lane** site foreman, and a crane-hire night man waiting for him at the door. **They were plot-utility to reach eleven.** They are now the flooring contractor's foreman, who came at midnight for a signature on the first coat, and his mate, who ran the machine until two. The count is unchanged, the motive is now a motive, and **Upton is left alone as the Sillick Lane foreman that the canon says he is.**
106. **Two exclusivity claims about the same motif** — `ch-0025` *the first time it has cost me nothing at all* and `ch-0030` *the only one of the five that cost him nothing at all*. Harmonisable rather than contradictory, but they read as a contradiction. `ch-0025` now says **the first of them that has cost me nothing at all**, which is true of the same set `ch-0030` counts.
107. **`ch-0025` had the field officer arriving at half past eight and the registrar stating her limitation at ten past eight "the moment the officer sat down."** Her statement is now at **ten past nine, before the officer has opened her form**, and Elias stands up ten minutes later, so the note she insists on — made *before the meeting begins and not afterwards* — is now actually before it.
108. **`ch-0027:206` "the cage goes back into bay 9 this coming Friday"** — ambiguous between the two readings of a speech that is present-tense from two different dates. Now **on Friday the first of November**, which is right from either.
109. **`state/current.md` contradicted itself about this batch's own history** — "not yet reviewed" against "three verification passes", and "two" against "three". The file the next batch trusts most was self-contradictory on the one fact a next-phase author needs.
110. **"The Monday before last" for a release on Monday 26 October** (`ch-0027:15`, `ch-0029:147`). From the 28th and the 30th that phrase reads as 14 and 16 October. Now **the Monday** and **last Monday**.
111. **`ch-0028` dated Custody Disc 41 from 12 October** and cut Disc 42 on **Thursday 24 October**, which is a Saturday. Now **20 October** and **Saturday**, and see the disc entry above the ledger for why a new disc was cut at all.

**Two minors were closed by deciding they were not defects.** The bonded store's line 3, *line 4 is a paper item in bond, small*, is **not** an error to be corrected: line 4 is a sealed plinth object and the clerk has classified it as paper because the docket in front of him says *one crate of papers*, and **that misreading is the mechanism that shelved a Schedule 12 object in a bonded cage.** It is now annotated in the four-lines entry as deliberate so that a fourth pass does not "fix" it. The dead-letter count, `continuity.md` 210 against `ch-0025` 253, is **not** a contradiction either: 210 is the end of Batch 0002. The running series is now written into this file so that nobody has to infer it.

**One item was fixed that the review did not raise**, because it sat in a sentence item 96 forced open: `ch-0028`'s custody note. And one that the review raised was **left alone on purpose**: the **"sixth of October"** anchor and the **manifest that does not match the real 2024 calendar**, both already recorded above as known, deliberate-for-now, and not to be re-derived.
