## CORRECTION TO A PRIOR CALENDAR ENTRY — the tenth working day, and the date of the Board

**`state/continuity.md` item 92 and `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` both dated the tenth working day of the Hallowgate re-proposal as *Tuesday 18 November*. That was wrong on the weekday and it was wrong twice over, and Chapters 36 and 40 are now right and the earlier entry is superseded.**

**The canonical arithmetic, and it is the same arithmetic that produced the October disposition, clause for clause:**

- Service: **Wednesday 4 November 2024** (Hallowgate Foyston re-proposed; a new docket, a new number; signed and in time).
- Ten working days, weekends out, **the day of service not counted, the next working day is day one.**
- **Thu 5 (1) · Fri 6 (2) · Mon 9 (3) · Tue 10 (4) · Wed 11 (5) · Thu 12 (6) · Fri 13 (7) · Mon 16 (8) · Tue 17 (9) · Wed 18 (10).**
- **The tenth working day is Wednesday 18 November 2024.** The practical cut-off is **17:00 on Wednesday 18 November**, because the office shuts at five.
- The disposition **does not die at five o'clock. It dies in the small hours of the next working day**, at **about 00:04 on Thursday 19 November**, and Nunn wrote *did not travel. No removal took place.* on it, which is what he wrote on the last one, in the same hand, with the same pen, because it is the same form and he is the same man.
- **The parallel October calculation is exact and is the precedent:** conditions issued **Thursday 15 October**; Fri 16 (1) · Mon 19 (2) · Tue 20 (3) · Wed 21 (4) · Thu 22 (5) · Fri 23 (6) · Mon 26 (7) · Tue 27 (8) · Wed 28 (9) · **Thu 29 (10)**; cut-off 17:00 Thursday 29 October; dead at **00:04 on Friday 30 October**. That is what Chapters 25, 28 and 29 say and it is right.
- **Consequence, and it is the Batch 0004 batch's best coincidence and nobody arranged it:** the **Board's schedule of contents sub-group sat on Wednesday 18 November, from 11:00 to about 18:00** — that is, on the last day on which anything at all could be executed, with an agenda item that said *the physical material is unavailable since 11 September* because a proposed disposition was before the standing instruction, and no box on the paper for *the reason the last box stopped being a live possibility.* **The material-change inspection was the next morning, Thursday 19 November at 11:00.** Elias put all three dates side by side on the back of a clipboard in a loading bay on Sunday the fifteenth and wrote that it was the first useful thing he had done all month.

**The Board itself is on TUESDAY 24 NOVEMBER 2024**, with the pack going up on Friday the twentieth. **The Director's written review of Elias Venn is on WEDNESDAY 25 NOVEMBER at 09:25** — the day before the hearing. Neither of those is the nineteenth, and the noticeboard notice that says *fourth Monday* is not corrected, because a document is corrected and a person is not.

## The stay, the determination, and the only dated instrument left in the business

**As of the close of Chapter 40, Thursday 19 November 2024:**

- The **material-change inspection** was held at 11:00 in the Hall of Former Faiths, in a public gallery, with a member of the public present, ref **QA/CR/1187**, and the member of the public was the requester, Elias Venn, who signed the visitors' book at **10:26**.
- Present at the case: **Aurelia Roth** (Schedule 12 assessor, led), **Ottilie Grange** (second assessor), **M. Iles** (registrar, not signing as an officer), **S. Spence** (metals conservator), **A. Ward** (Director), **I. Pell** (systems, at the north door), and Elias Venn. **Neither assessor has seen a piece of the case's evidence and neither asked to.**
- The object was **not opened**. A material-change inspection is not handling and a determination is not a lifting.
- **Determination: no material change in the condition or the classification of the object.** Seal intact; no handling, use, cleaning, movement or public access since 7 November; Annexe D stands; Annexe E stands.
- **The plain reading of Annexe E would lift the stay on a determination of no material change, and Aurelia Roth refused it**, in front of the room, in these terms: *if a stay lifted on a determination of no material change, then every stayed object in this city would lift the first time an inspector looked at it and found it stable. The clause would be a clause that says stay until somebody confirms that you are staying. I am not going to improve that clause. The Registry does not write the schedule; the schedule is about two hundred years old; and every one of the four hundred copies of it in this city has a box 4(d) on it that somebody put there in about 1840 because they needed somewhere to put the true things that would not fit in the other boxes.*
- **Box 4(c) is empty. Box 4(d) carries:** that on 7 November 2024 the object was reported as having reached a person who had not consented, in a public room, with fifty-one people present of whom a number were children; that the certified condition is unchanged; that **the Registry has no threshold in the schedule for the number or the age of the people in the room**; and that the stay continues.
- **The stay is not lifted and the object is not lifted. The Hall remains open, the lights are on, nobody may touch it, the school term continues, and the Hall certificate of 7 November stands.**
- **THE DETERMINATION GOES ON THE PUBLIC REGISTER ON FRIDAY 20 NOVEMBER 2024**, at no charge, with a registration number, open to any person, and **the route to it is Elias Venn's, because Annexe E's dispute route is the requester and he is the requester.** Roth said so to him in the room. This is the third time his own form has been the mechanism, and the first time it has put something in a public document.
- **THE ONLY DATED INSTRUMENT LEFT IN THE BUSINESS, and it was given to him in a public gallery, out loud, in front of six people, and Roth refused to send it:** a requester may put **a further question to the Registry, in the form of a request**, and the office will list it, and if it is listed it will be decided **on a stated day with a stated assessor in the room**. **The form is the same form he used in October. There is a box on it for a period. The period is twenty-eight days from the date of determination, and the date of determination is 19 November 2024, so the last day is THURSDAY 17 DECEMBER 2024.** What the question is is his and not hers; she refused to help him frame it, *because the moment I help you with it, it is the Registry that asked whether an object reaches a person, and it never will be.* She wrote the date on the back of her own copy in pencil and will not send it, and said that if he does not use it she will regard that as the correct outcome.
- **Roth also separated the two routes, so that Chapter 30 and Chapter 40 do not contradict each other and so that a later batch does not.** An **appeal** is a hearing on the disturbance, goes before a panel, takes about eleven weeks, and a panel that finds nothing ends it. A **material-change inspection** is not a hearing, has no panel, takes forty minutes, and answers one question. They are different instruments with different tests, they do not cancel each other out, and a determination has no effect on an appeal. **No appeal has been lodged and none is in prospect.**

## The origin — the work authorisation, and what it does and does not say

**Found Thursday 19 November 2024 at about 16:40, in the security room, in a red lever-arch file labelled CARD ACCESS — 2006 REORGANISATION / QUIET WING, which sits on the shelf under the card-access terminal and which is the only restricted file in this building a suspended night guard is permitted to open.**

The outer document is unchanged and unchanged since 2006:

> **QUIET WING — CARD ACCESS REORGANISATION. Instruction 06/QW/11. 11 October 2006.**

with a typed name at the foot that is on no list of people who have ever worked here, and a stapled-in buff envelope marked **ATTACHMENTS — DO NOT SEPARATE**. The corner of the envelope had lifted. Inside are two documents.

> **WORK AUTHORISATION — 4417/06.** *(Protective works. Contract 4417.)*
> **Location:** Quiet Wing corridor, level 4, west range. **Item:** east end — door and lobby.
> **Scope:** make good, seal, and re-hang. Fit secondary latch to corridor side. **Leave the primary latch engaged.**
> **Also:** re-site the ward repeater bracket at half-landing. Existing bracket to remain in position and not to be removed.
> **Date of authorisation:** 2 November 2006. **Authorised by:** *T. VENN.*
> **Countersigned:** ☐ **not countersigned. Job complete:** ☐ **not completed.**
> and, in a second pen, at a later date: **Closed 3 Nov 06. Bracket not re-sited. Lamp not returned. Left as found. R.M.J.**

and a folded plan of the corridor with a door drawn on it and, in pencil, in a third hand: **already latched when I came.**

**What this establishes, and it is the whole of it:**

- A work authorisation for protective works on contract 4417, for the **east end of the Quiet Wing corridor** — the door and lobby of the unlisted room — **dated 2 November 2006**, three weeks **after** the instruction and after the Casket, **signed T. VENN, not countersigned, not completed.**
- **T. VENN is on no list of anybody who has ever worked at this Museum, and there is no T. VENN on the bureau and none in a payroll run in this city.** *(An earlier version of this entry and of Chapter 40 also said that Elias Venn is on no list of anybody who has ever worked here. That is false: he is in the payroll system on M.Q.S. 4 and he is the ninth entry in the fixed-positions file of Thursday 16 September — see the entry above. The false claim is removed from the prose and from here.)* The one T. Venn in the world he can reach has been dead for eighteen years. **The recognition that the two letters are his own is real and is on the page, and Chapter 40 now says plainly that it gets him nothing: a man who has found his own initials on a form has not found the hand that wrote them.**
- **RECORDED CHANGE, and the reason for it.** `state/continuity.md:21` and `state/open-threads.md:25` promised that *Tomas's initials are in the attachments and are found in Movement 4.* **Chapter 40 finds T. VENN and R.M.J. and does not name either of them, and does not say that T. VENN is Tomas.** **The prose says only that there is one T. Venn in the world he can reach, that the man has been dead for eighteen years, and that the two letters at the front of the signature are his own. An earlier draft of Chapter 40 went one step further and said that there is one person alive in this city who could sign a document like that and she is the Director and her surname is Ward. That was a non-sequitur — A. Ward is not T. VENN, she was not in this building in 2006 in any capacity the file records, and Anja Ward has run the building for nineteen years — and it has been cut. Chapter 40 now ends that movement on the fact that two letters are the cheapest thing a document can be made of, which is the volume's own argument and does not spend anything.** **This is deliberate and it preserves the Volume 01 reveal discipline** (*the origin of Elias's ability beyond 'he spoke a name during the Casket, and a fragment fused to his memory'*). **A later batch may identify T. VENN. No batch before the one the outline names may.**
- **RECORDED CHANGE, second.** `state/current.md:45` deferred the origin of the ability to Volume 12. **Chapter 40 states it, in the volume's own words and inside the discipline:** *the ability is not a gift. It is a room he was in, in the last week of October 2006, at fourteen, and something in it fused to him.* **It goes no further than that, and the document proves nothing about the ability at all.** The state files have now been told.
- **R.M.J.** is a third set of initials in the file. Nobody can say who they are. **Anja Ward's standing assumption for eighteen years is that the name on the instruction's face is a contractor's signatory, and she has never read the attachment because nobody has ever asked her to.** Open, and a live lead, and the first one anybody has been inside in eighteen years.
- **THE FOURTEEN-YEAR-OLD ON THE SITE IN CHAPTER 24 IS NOT ELIAS VENN AND MUST NEVER BE READ AS ELIAS VENN.** The accident was **Saturday 24 October 2024, about 13:25, on a site that was being cleared**, four miles away, with a college tutor, a site induction record and a foreman called Upton; the boy got a fracture of the radial styloid; nobody is to blame. **Chapter 40 says so in the prose and separates the two explicitly, and says it is a different fourteen-year-old on a different site in a different decade and has nothing to do with a bell.** **Elias Venn was fourteen in October 2006, in this building, in the Casket. Two different fourteen-year-olds. The confusion is a trap and the chapter walks round it in four sentences.**
- The unlisted room is **still shut and latched** and has not been opened and is not going to be opened in this volume. The authorisation does not change the door.

## The notebook page — the first displacement, and it costs nothing

**There is no second notebook entry and there is no second memory cost. The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched, unrestated, unreferred to and unstabilised, and the first major memory-displacement cost remains reserved for Volume 03.**

**What happened on Thursday 19 November at about 20:20, in a flat in Low Ward above a laundrette that shuts at ten:** Elias Venn took out the notebook to write down what a piece of paper in a buff envelope said, because that is the only instrument he has ever had. He turned to the fourth page from the end, which he had not written in since Monday the sixteenth.

**The bay check of Monday 16 November, 11:20** is on that page in his own hand, in the ink and the nib pressure and the lean he could not improve on in his life, and it is the sentence he wrote standing up in a loading bay with a clipboard on his knee and then copied into the notebook, which is what he does.

**Beneath it, in a rounder and lighter hand, with less pressure and a slant the other way, the same sentence, word for word, dated *Mon 16 Nov, 11:20*.**

Nothing is wrong with either. Nothing is missing from either and nothing is extra. Both halves are complete and neither half refers to the other. A clean page with one sentence on it in his own hand does not account for the same sentence in another hand on the same page on the same day. **He cannot tell which of the two he wrote first, he is not going to be told which, and he does not find it frightening, and he should.**

**The method is sensory and not amnesic, and a later batch must not turn it into a cost.** There is no headache, no nosebleed, no name gone, no time lost, no blank, and no new entry. It is the same sentence twice on one page and it is a page, and a page is the only object in this volume that has ever got the better of him.

## New documents, institutions, formats, people and numbers introduced in Batch 0004

- **The fitter's lamp's return.** A bonded-goods return form, one sheet, with a **CONDITION ON RETURN** line, a **SEAL** line and a **DISPOSITION** line reading *re-plinth (Annexe D object, sealed) / stores / other, circle one*. Line four was struck and initialled; the blank at the foot carries **RE-PLINTH DEFERRED. Item is a Schedule 12 carried object. It is not to be re-plinthed while a Registry determination is open. Stores. E. Venn (receiving), 07:16.** **The lamp is in cupboard 14, off the old frame store corridor on the ground floor, on a piece of hardboard, on its side, under a key-board entry and a signature in a book every time it moves.** Signed out 07:37, signed back 07:41. **It is not re-plinthed and it does not go back on plinth 9 in this volume.**
- **Cupboard 14** and **key board 14**, ground floor, off the old frame store corridor. The board has a time column and nobody reads the column.
- **The Registry maintenance notice QA/N/xx.** One sheet, Registry typeface, dated 19 November is *not* on it and it carries no countersignatures. *Action required: None.* *Countersigned: [ ] Registry assessor (Schedule 12) [ ] Museum officer (not the Director).* **NO ACTION REQUIRED — TICK BOX NOT TO BE COMPLETED BY THE ADDRESSEE.** *Issued under maintenance procedure 4(b). A maintenance notice records a recommendation. It is not an order. It confers no authority to move any object.* **It cannot be executed. Annexe D forbids removal from public view by a museum acting alone and Annexe E is a stay from all movement, and both documents were produced by this building, nineteen days apart, by two competent people in Northglass who had not read each other.**
- **The systems index line of Tuesday 10 November**, under *schedule of collections, location, Gallery of Unnamed Hands*, as first written and as amended: **1961/LW/51 (fitter's lamp) — location amended 10 Nov 2024. Was: Gallery of Unnamed Hands, plinth 9. Now: stores, cupboard 14, ground floor, key board 14. Seal intact on receipt. Not on display. Not to be re-plinthed pending Registry determination.** — amended to **relocated** and **by order of the receiving officer**, initialled with the date and the time. The index keeps amendments, which is the entire reason the file is worth anything. **The index is on a screen in the systems office, which is a room anybody can walk into, and the system rotates through every room in the building, and the index has about nine hundred lines and two hundred and forty-seven items with a location in it.**
- **The Gallery of Unnamed Hands door counter.** A little mechanical wheel beside the door, reset by a systems technician on a Tuesday. **Five hundred and eighty-eight at 09:00 on Saturday 7 November; four hundred and ninety-one at 10:30 on Wednesday 4 November; one hundred and twenty-six at 10:30 on Wednesday 11 November.** The Museum does about two thousand people a day and a Saturday is about nine hundred.
- **The archive corridor count of 6 minutes, twice, on 10 November and 11 November.** Eleven things on the Wednesday of the 4th (plant, lift twice, fire door once, schools' letters once, a trolley, a man's voice from the quiet room) and **four on the first run on the 11th, five on the second.** The word *or* in front of the figure is the honest part of the page.
- **Miss Arkwright**, bonded goods clerk, about thirty, six years in the sheds, licensed, on a headset. Competent, courteous and completely blameless, and the volume's argument in a person. Her rules, all correct: a pencil is not a hold; a hold is a hold on her system with her initials and a time and a reference; she will not hold a book in bond for a pencil; anything under a proper hold is held seventy-two hours and then referred upwards; a claim has to be in writing to the consignor within seven days of the collection and wants an accession number and a description; she has never once known what was in a bay in her life and will not start on a Friday. **Her one line that is the volume: *you have to say that one to somebody, and everybody in that chain has about nine hundred loads in their head and the one you've got in yours is not the one they've got in theirs.***
- **Mowbray**, the man with the board and the marker on the quay. **Renwick**, about fifty, the driver, eleven thousand of those a year, has never opened one, will not start, and gives a man a docket number because it takes four days to come out of that office and it comes out with the month on it.
- **The delivery sheet.** One page, printed, carbon-copied, four parts. *1 x steel container, ex Sillick Mill site, Low Ward. Cont. seal [number]. **Site contents — general, as per schedule.** Weight on break bulk. Balance consigned: paper merchant, collection booked. Received into bond 24 Oct 09:15. Bay 4.* **In the fourth box of the second section, headed HELD, a pencil tick and the three letters HOLD, in an office manager's pencil, October.** **A pencil is not a hold. That is the batch's whole mechanism.**
- **The four lots, and the four figures.** Lot one, six sacks. Lot two, two strapped pallets of books. Lot three, bundled paper. Lot four, a trolley of oddments, a tin, a bundle of keys, two empty wooden trays, a bicycle, a hard hat. **Bay scale, 09:58 Thursday 12 November: 482 kg gross in four lots, read off a dial by a boy of about twenty and written in a box with nothing above it. Yard weighbridge, 10:31 Friday 13 November: 494 kg, tared, four skips, up on the bay figure because the paper was damp from three days in a bay in November, and the merchant's clerk will not think twice about it and is right not to.** **Neither figure is a count of anything, and Elias makes himself do the sum he is not going to do so that he never does it by accident.** *482* and *494* are kilograms. **They are not a Sillick number, and no batch may make them one.**
- **The claim period: seven days from the collection. The collection was Friday 13 November 2024. The claim must be in writing to the consignor by FRIDAY 20 NOVEMBER 2024.** The consignor on the sheet is the company that filled the container; its site office is on a site that was cleared on Sunday 25 October and the number on the sheet is a mobile number, answered on Friday the thirteenth at 08:44 by a facilities contractor who is not the company and has a pen for a fence.
- **Lot two, the books, goes on the merchant's fortnightly collection on TUESDAY 25 NOVEMBER 2024** — same firm, same yard, same weighbridge. **The bound wage book for October 1974, item four, the one with the pencil, is on that pallet.** It survived the Friday by a tail lift, a wheel stop and a man's four seconds.
- **The item identified on Wednesday 12 November, at about ten to ten.** A bound book, **two hundred and ninety-one pages**, a leather spine, a printed label on the front reading **WAGES, PAY OFFICE** in somebody's typing and, under it, a month and a year: **1974, October.** In a polythene sleeve with a tie; the strap was rotten and Miss Arkwright put a new one on, because a rotten strap is the one thing in a bay you are not allowed to let go of.
- **What was lost on Friday 13 November, and it is unrecoverable:** the **pay office's own posted wage sheets for the last eight weeks of trading in 1974**, loose, in the pay clerk's hand, the only corroboration in this city of what was paid on the final run. **Doreen Sarn's envelope contains a *copy* she made at her own kitchen table in November 1974. The mill's own book was the primary record. The primary record went into a skip in Northglass at eight o'clock on a Friday morning and was at a pulp mill by Tuesday.** **The only surviving record of the final pay run is now a copy in a sealed envelope that a woman will not open for anybody, made at her own table, in her own hand, in November 1974.**
- **The address, which is the cheapest live lead in the volume and it took seven weeks to ask for. It is QUILL & FRAYLE, Solicitors, 14 LINCOLN'S LANE, IRVING QUAY.** Doreen Sarn gave the firm and the street at about ten past ten on the afternoon of Thursday 12 November 1974's fifty-year anniversary date, from a **brass plate**; she did not remember the number and **Elias had had the number since the sixteenth of October**, off the 1974 carbon in drawer 6B, which says **RECEIVED FROM: QUILL & FRAYLE, Solicitors, 14 Lincoln's Lane, Irving Quay**, dated 7 November 1974. **She put the envelope in the out-tray on **Tuesday 22 October 1974** (Monday 21 October 1974 was a real-world Monday and the twenty-second was therefore a Tuesday; Chapter 35 said Wednesday and is corrected), at about ten past eleven, in her own hand, with a fourpenny stamp, into the mill's own out-tray, which had a card on the side of it reading 2ND POST — 3 DAYS, because the mill took its own post down to the sorting office on Wednesday afternoons.** It arrived **within three days, on the Thursday or the Friday, and the mill closed on the Wednesday of its last trading week, so anything still in the out-tray at closure was never posted and went in the van.** **A pay clerk wrote to a firm of solicitors in October 1974; that firm sold this Museum the mill's office on 7 November 1974, sixteen days later; the receipt has said RECEIVED FROM in a box file on level two for fifty years; and nobody in this building had ever asked who had written to them.**
- **Quill & Frayle**, solicitors, **14 Lincoln's Lane, Irving Quay**, established 1871, unmoved, and the reason is the **fourth clause of a deed of covenant of 1889** that no paper, print or document of any kind may be stored at any height above four feet from the floor of the strong room. Open on Saturdays because property men come in on Saturdays. **THIS IS A DIFFERENT FIRM FROM QUARRY BANK SOLICITORS, 4 IRVING QUAY, which has two partners, one of them Nan Quill, and which holds the occupation form in a safe. The two firms are a few hundred yards off the same quay and are on different streets and have nothing to do with each other. Do not let a reader merge them.**
- **Emyr Rhys**, fifty-eight, thirty-one years at Quill & Frayle, the person who deals with what comes in: the post, the deeds, the boxes, the old files. **Thirty-one years of what comes in.** He knows which wall a document is in. He is correct, and he is decent, and being right is what costs a wage sheet its life. He will not hand over the clearance schedule and will not explain why twice, and the reason is that it is a client's document and a partner may give it or not, and the partner has said no twice for a reason that is a reason.
- **Beverley Marsden**, sixty-two, senior transcript clerk, twenty-six years, a reading frame on a chain, and a printed pro forma with two columns, eleven ruled lines and a box at the foot. She reads documents aloud as they are and not as they might be, and if anybody has improved a document in this firm in fifty weeks she wants to stop it. **She is the neutral party in the comparison and the only one in the room who is.**
- **Arthur Neve, 78**, came at five past ten on Saturday 14 November, with a frame and a plastic carrier bag, having said no twice, and had been up since half past six. **He keeps the carbon. He states it before anybody starts: it is my document until the twenty-sixth of November, and on the twenty-sixth it is the conciliation service's document, in a room, with a field officer in it.** His hand stays on the corner of it for two hours. He reads out his own side where he can. The carbon goes back into the plastic wallet into the carrier bag onto his knees and leaves with him, up six steps, to a bus, **because a doctor in 1971 told him not to sit in a small warm car with a man who is going to talk, and the doctor was right about that too.** **What exists in two places after the Saturday is a comparison, not a document.**
- **The two carbons, and the three differences and one figure.** The **seller's carbon** is in drawer 6B of the Gallery of Unnamed Hands with Doreen Sarn's ballpoint margin entry against the item-9 line, and it is the Museum's own copy of the 1974 receipt. The **buyer's carbon** is Arthur Neve's, in his own plastic wallet, in his own carrier bag. **The order differs (the seller wrote them in shop order, the buyer in cart order) and Mrs Marsden neutralises the order by reading in numbered order against the pro forma. The three substantive differences and the one figure are:**
  1. **Item 3** — seller: *gate book (leaves 1–96 present).* Buyer: *gate book, with entries to 30 Oct.*
  2. **Item 10** — seller: *bundle of wage sheets, tied with tape.* Buyer: *bundle of wage sheets, tied with tape, **marked pay office**.* (Two words, and it is a rubber stamp on the outside of a bundle and it is not a description.)
  3. **Item 11** — seller: *the works-office clock, stopped.* Buyer: *clock, works office, **stopped 05:54**.*
  - **And on the buyer's foot only: *received, all, subject to weight.***
  - **The two documents are 5–4 and 5–3 of the same eleven items and they are otherwise identical line for line.**
- **Nobody accuses anybody, and the difference is a fact about two clerks in 1974.** *It is a clerical error or it is a decision and there is nothing on either piece of paper that says which, and anybody who writes leaves 97 to 100 removed in a document will be asserting a thing they cannot support in a room with a man of seventy-eight in it who was at the gate.* **And the counter-argument, which is better and which nobody had thought of until a transcript clerk said it out loud: there is a pay-office stamp in that building in a tin and there is a date on the die, and if the die is dated before the thirtieth of October then the stamp could not have been on the bundle on the thirtieth and it went on afterwards, in a store, by a person, and nobody wrote it down then because nobody thought it was worth writing down.**
- **Mrs Marsden's certified comparison, in full at the foot:** *Three differences in wording, and one figure. I do not certify that either document is correct. I certify that this is a true comparison of the two documents as they stand, and that both documents were in the room at the time of the comparison.* One copy to Mara Iles, one to Arthur Neve, **and neither copy is of the carbon.**
- **Quill & Frayle's destruction schedule**, the firm's own and disclosable, 1971 to the present, about four hundred pages, in date order, in the strong room under the pavement, on a shelf at four feet because of the covenant. Every line has a reference. **The line, and it is the answer to the cheapest lead in the volume:**
  > **INCOMING — w/c 21 X 74 — 1 env. personal delivery, office of origin, per second post — 1 — DESTROYED 1979, per this schedule, batch 34/74.**
  **It is a destruction schedule, so it says how many and when, and everything else about an envelope is thrown away with the envelope. There is no address, no sender, no subject and no contents line, and there never will be.** In 1974 a firm this size received forty-one items of post a week on average; that week is high; there is exactly one line in it that is not a bill and not a bank and not a firm on another matter; and it says *personal*.
- **The cross-reference**, written in ink by Emyr Rhys, initialled and dated, pinned to the same page in the same binder, on the transmittal, which is not a hole in a schedule but an ordinary cross-reference that about two hundred of them are signed to every year and nobody ever fills the box in:
  > **CROSS-REFERENCE, 14 Nov 2024. Item at this line identified 2024 as personal correspondence of D. SARN, written 22 X 1974, in her own hand, delivered to this office within three days. Contents not examined by this firm at any time. Identification does not evidence contents. Schedule unaltered. E. RHYS, 31 yrs.**
  - **RECORDED CORRECTION: the earlier Chapter 35 text called this *a sheet of large paper* and *a Saturday*. It is a cross-reference on a transmittal, and it is the only thing in the volume so far that names the addressee of the envelope in a document that will still be there in a hundred years.**
- **The fee ledger, 29 X 74**, a book and not destroyed, kept for a hundred years after a Registrar of Companies, with a date and a folio and a job number and an amount of **eleven guineas**, and four clauses:
  > **To attend site, Low Ward; take delivery of mill office papers, and —**
  - ***Take no instructions.*** Three long clauses and a fourth of two words, and it means: a man goes to a place; a van comes; and a firm writes down, in ink, in eleven guineas' worth, that nobody told them anything to do and that they did exactly what they were asked, which was to stand in a yard in the rain and write down what came off a cart.
  - **Elias Venn does not write it down. Mara Iles writes it on a sheet of Museum paper and types it into a Museum schedule at 21:30 that night, in a building he is not allowed on the upper floors of, and it takes an accession number and gets a location, and the location is a drawer.** The typed version is a Museum record and **an eleven-week hearing index will carry the phrase *take no instructions* in a Museum file, and nobody in this building will be able to explain where a fifty-year-old fee ledger came from.**
- **The Registry rota.** Four assessors. **Two are Aurelia Roth and Ottilie Grange and two are not named in this batch and have never been spoken to.** A slot cannot be booked against a named assessor; a slot is booked and the rota decides. **Ivo Pell has a photocopy of the job list because it comes through on the weekly systems bulletin and the bulletin is a document and the document is a series and the series is in a drawer.** He booked the Gallery re-certification for **Tuesday 24 November at 14:00** on Friday 20 October... **no: on Friday 8 November at 20:10**, from the bulletin, without being asked, and it came back **ASSESSOR (ROTA) — NOT ROTH, NOT GRANGE.** *A rota is the most frightening document in this building and there are about forty of them and I read all forty of them on Monday.*
- **The DECISION under Standing Instruction 14 clause five, dated 17 November 2024 and expressly not back-dated** (the Director refuses to date a decision she did not take, and says so). Ivo's four exposures of drawer 6B, made 6 March 2024 in the course of a scheduled condition survey for the insurance schedule, entered in the systems index on 6 March under *schedule of collections, condition, Gallery flat files*, sheets initialled S. PELL, four of 246 delivered to Low Ward station on 23 October by him, in his own working capacity, unasked. **Finding: the survey was scheduled, was in the index, and would have been made in the same month by the same person whether or not the drawer had subsequently been photographed and delivered. Clause five is not complied with — not because of a distinction of professional status, and if a member of staff photographs collections material in the course of a duty, the duty is the reason and the status of the photographer is not a defence to it in any other case. NO DISCIPLINARY ACTION.** **Consequent condition from 1 December 2024: every photographic exposure of collections material, labels or records made by a member of staff in the course of any survey, condition check or schedule compilation shall be countersigned at the point of making by a supervisor, with the reason and the schedule reference. The post is unchanged. It is not a demotion. It is the removal of a man's ability to be alone in a room with a camera, and I am aware of that and I am doing it and I would like it recorded that I am aware of it.** Re-notified on the west range corridor board on Wednesday 18 November at 08:30, over the top of Ivo's own assistance notice, in a plastic sleeve, by nobody to anybody.
- **The Director's card of 20 October**, in her own hand, read out to him on 17 November before she put it in a drawer dated the seventeenth. Carried here in full because it is the volume's assessment of its own protagonist and no later batch may soften it: *E. Venn, 32. Suspended from night duty 07:00, Sun 11 Oct 2024, on work order 4412-S, a door 7 corridor fault he did not close. No further incident attributed to the same cause in five weeks and none likely. He has been the mechanism twice: once for a Registry stay on a Schedule 12 object by his own request of 19 Oct, and once, possibly, for a disposition by his own conduct. He reports honestly and late. He is the man in the room now and not the man with the fact, and the man in the room is not safer. A person who causes the events he investigates should not be the investigator. I do not have a mechanism to say that in a document and it is the only reservation I hold.* **A. WARD, 20 Oct 2024.**
- **The DISCLOSURE FORM**, one sheet of Museum notepaper, printed on the front, from a print run of four hundred that goes out with a pack of compliment slips and a pen. **It has no Museum number and no clause, and that is the point: a request does not need a number and a request does not need a clause.** Eight numbered boxes: *1 the document · 2 date sent · 3 held by · 4 reference on the envelope, if any · 5 why it has not previously been disclosed to this Museum · 6 action requested of the Director (request the document into a Museum file, under the date of this request / other) · 7 date of request · 8 signature, Director.* **Box 5, in Mara Iles's hand: *Because I was not asked.*** **Box 6: *I am asking for the document to be brought into a Museum file under the date of this request, so that it is no longer an unsolicited document in a file the Museum did not request, which is what clause six makes it, and which is why it is worth nothing.*** **The Director did not sign it on the spot and gave a date: Friday, or a written reason on Friday.**
- **The BOARD SUB-GROUP minute of Wednesday 18 November 2024**, Collections and Interpretation, three trustees and the Director and the Board Secretary and the registrar, 11:00 to about 18:00, forty pages, item order one two three **five** four, typed and initialled and brought down to the front desk at 18:30 by the Board Secretary. **Item five took two hours and ten minutes and item four took one hour and forty minutes, and item five was on the paper because a papers request landed on the agenda in the middle of October while item four was still being drafted, and nobody gave an explanation.**
  - The standing minute note of 9 October 2024 on the paper: *the physical material is UNAVAILABLE SINCE 11 SEPT 2024. A proposed disposition is before the standing instruction. Review deferred at minuted request.* **It is the only reason item five is on the agenda and it stopped being true at 17:00 that afternoon.**
  - **5.4** The Registrar read the name. *Case, Wendel — grade 2 porter, 1994 to 2012 — as it appears at entry 1961 / LW / 52 (b), and as it appears on a card in the Registrar's historic staff file which is in part illegible and in part amended.*
  - **5.5** It was entered by herself on 29 October 2024 in consequence of the Manifest Transfer Schedule, drawn up from a 1974 carbon and not from the box's label, *because a receiving party who signs for a gate book cannot afterwards say the gate book was nobody's.*
  - **5.6** *I wrote the statement of 19 October 2024 in the conservation office on the Saturday before it was lodged, in the ordinary way, in answer to a request that had been received at a front desk, and I would do it again, and I said so unasked.*
  - **5.8 RESOLVED:** that the Museum write to the Municipal Payroll Bureau, Remittances and Deductions, **in the form set out at appendix**, for such confirmation as that office is able to give; and that the Director report the position to the Board at its next meeting. **The Board is on TUESDAY 24 NOVEMBER 2024.**
  - **THE COST OF THE EXPOSURE, and it is exact:** the bureau's form has two questions, *did you pay this person* and *was this person employed by anybody*, and **a bureau's remittance record for a person is a record of the person and of money paid and is not a record of the employer**, so **H. Lound is going to have to complete the same form twice and answer the second half of it with nothing and sign it, and the nothing goes in a Museum file with the Museum's request number on it, and in about eleven years somebody will read that form and think the Museum could not be bothered to ask the second question.** He answered half of it once already, on 29 October, in about forty seconds, and he has never heard of the building.
  - **4.4** the hall certificate of 7 November stands, condition four is not engaged, no further certification is required before the end of the term. **4.5**, at the Director's request and not debated: **the Annexe E stay is a stricter instrument than the Director's condition four, and it has been in force since 7 November, and the Museum's own file is the only place in which that is written down.**
- **Mrs Angela Vessey**, sixty-four, trustee since 2019, chairs the sub-group, has a right of access to Board papers, and reads headings. **She is not a villain and she has not done a wrong thing in this chapter and she read the Schedule 12 heading out loud in a room.**
- **THE SCHEDULE 12 HEADING, which is the only place in the volume where the thing this Museum keeps is named without the word being used, and it is printed on every Schedule 12 classification certificate in this city since about 1810:**
  > **QUIET ACCORD — SCHEDULE 12. CARRIED PATTERNS.**
  > *Objects and records carrying a set form held in common use.*
  > **ANNEXE D (PERMANENT). A PATTERN REQUIRING CONTINUED OBSERVANCE. TO BE DISPLAYED.**
  - **Nine of them are displayed across three galleries in this building, on open plinths, with a rope line, and the rope line is the whole of the provision and has been since the building opened. About two hundred more are in store and are not displayed.** Four per cent of the collection.
  - **The mechanism, in the Director's answer to *who is observing it?*: *Whoever is in the room. That is the mechanism. It has been the mechanism for two hundred years in this city and it is the reason the buildings are free, and it is not a metaphor.** The people who come in on a wet Saturday and stand in front of a case and look at something for ninety seconds are doing it, and they do not know they are doing it, and if they stopped, it would stop.*
  - **And why nobody in that room said the word in an hour and forty minutes, in the Director's own words: *because the word would be accurate, and a minute is a public document, and the moment it is in a public document it becomes a thing this Museum has said about itself in the past tense.***
- **The work order** on the work authorisation is **4417/06** and the protective floor works contract is **4417**. Elias's own work order is **4412-S**. They are different numbers and must not be confused.
- **The new dead-letters series for Batch 0004, which continues the running count and is monotonic: 276** (Tue 10 Nov, Ch 31) · **281** (Wed 11 Nov, Ch 32) · **294** (Fri 13 Nov, Ch 34) · **301** (Sun 15 Nov, Ch 36) · **305** (Mon 16 Nov, Ch 37) · **309** (Tue 17 Nov, Ch 38) · **313** (Wed 18 Nov, Ch 39) · **319** (Thu 19 Nov, Ch 40, and the count has stopped meaning anything about four hundred of the time). **Chapters 33 and 35 carry no count.** Chapter 30 gave 271 for Saturday 7 November, which remains canonical. **Running series for the record: 210 (end B2) · 234 · 240 · 248 · 251 · 253 · 255 · 271 · 276 · 281 · 294 · 301 · 305 · 309 · 313 · 319.**
- **The 1974 weekday of 21 October is a Monday and the manuscript says so** (Ch 35, the week of the destruction-schedule line). **1974 is not on the pinned calendar and this is a real-world weekday, not a manuscript invention. Do not "correct" it.**

## Where everything is, as of Thursday 19 November 2024, 23:00

- **The bell** — plinth 34, Hall of Former Faiths, sealed, re-anchored, earth lead, green telltale, **staple repair, fracture remains visible, and the crack is open and is not going to heal.** Annexe D, and **STAYED under Annexe E since 13:10 on 7 November 2024**: no handling, no use, no cleaning, no movement, no public access. **Inspected 19 November at 11:00. Determined: no material change. The stay stands and is not lifted and the object is not lifted. The determination goes on the public register on 20 November. The Hall is open and the lights are on and nobody may touch it.**
- **1961/LW/44** — **cage 2 in the old frame store in bay 9, sub-level B**, Custody Disc 42, padlock **QA/1187**, eleven items, not opened, a grey polythene bag over the front rail against the dust. **Since 1 November it is furniture, and since 00:04 on 19 November there is no proposal before the standing instruction that could move it, and the company that wanted it out has sent a letter with no service date on it, and Nunn has written to them in terms that the letter is not a proposal and that they must serve a date and take ten working days, and they can serve it in December and he will be here in December.** **The cage opens on Friday 27 November 2024 at 16:00 in Doreen Sarn's own front room**, with two conciliation officers, a supervisor and a camera, and **no Museum employee in the room, and Elias Venn in particular.**
- **1961/LW/51, the fitter's lamp** — **cupboard 14, off the old frame store corridor, ground floor, on a piece of hardboard, on its side, seal intact, not re-plinthed**, on a key-board entry with times, signed in and out. **It is the correct place for it and it is out of the world.** The plinth card in the Gallery says *1961/LW/51 — FITTER'S LAMP. On loan from a bonded store, returned 10 November 2024. In stores pending a determination. See note on drawer 6B.* **The Gallery holds eight objects against an inventory sheet dated 1988 that says nine.**
- **Drawer 6B** — **closed at 17:12 on Sunday 15 November by Elias Venn, in front of four people, with his initials and the time in the day book: *DRAWER 6B, Gallery of Unnamed Hands, closed. Contents undisturbed. No item removed. Reason: drawer was open in a public room.*** It was opened again on the morning of **Saturday 14 November at 08:20 by Mara Iles, with a key, in a public gallery, with nine people in it**, for the comparison at Irving Quay, and **put back by her before she left, with the slip still in with it: *1961/LW/44 — item 3 of 11, drawer 6B, lifted 14 Nov 2024, 08:20, M. ILES.*** **It is a single sheet of thin grey carbon, folded once across, eleven typed lines and a ballpoint margin entry in a hand that leans.** It has never been photographed.
- **Bay 4, Quay 3, Broomhead Reach, in bond** — an empty steel container with a broken-bulk sheet on it, a hold in a form, and **a pallet of two strapped stacks of books, and on it, in a polythene sleeve, the mill's own bound wage book for October 1974, item four, two hundred and ninety-one pages, with a new strap**, going to the paper merchant's **fortnightly collection on Tuesday 25 November.** It is not in the Museum, it is not with anybody, and it is the only copy of the primary record in existence. **Quill & Frayle's four clauses — two of them long, the third of them one word and a dash, and the fourth two words — are on a page in a Museum file, typed by Mara Iles out of a fee ledger, and will be on the index for the hearing on 26 November.** *(This entry said *H. Lound's four clauses*. H. Lound is the Municipal Payroll Bureau's Chief Clerk, Remittances, and has nothing to do with Quill & Frayle's fee ledger of 29 X 74. Corrected.)*
- **The 1974 carbon** — the seller's copy, drawer 6B, Museum carbon paper, and **a certified comparison of it with Arthur Neve's, one copy in Mara Iles's wallet and one in Arthur Neve's plastic carrier bag.** **The buyer's carbon itself is in Northglass, in a plastic wallet, in a plastic carrier bag, on Arthur Neve's knees and then in his hands, and goes to the hearing on 26 November and not before.**
- **The pressed-brass service bell** beside the unlisted room — **silent since 15 October 2024's repeater line died *(this entry said 2026; corrected)*, and it rang once on Wednesday 11 November 2024 at 11:52, half a tone flat, of something three floors away, and nobody came, and Elias Venn is the only person in the building who heard it, and he has not told anybody.** **The corridor outside the unlisted room is quieter than it has been in eighteen years and the quiet is not the wire.** He has not proved any of this and he must not say it out loud, and the only dated instrument it gives him is the same twenty-eight days everybody else has.
- **The unlisted room** — **shut and latched, not opened, and it is going to be shut and latched when the year has gone.** The work authorisation in the attachments of the 2006 instruction is dated **2 November 2006** and the pencil on the plan says **already latched when I came**, and nobody has ever opened either document in this volume before Chapter 40.
- **Locker 81** — unopened, brass plate **CASE, W.**, tin of tea, comb, folded newspaper, a bit of paper with figures on it. Mara's request to the Board is still unanswered on the page.
- **Helen Venn's letter** — on the shelf above the boiler in the laundrette in Low Ward, in a white envelope with his father's name on it in her mother's hand, the paper gone soft along a fold that has learned to hold itself. **He said out loud on 16 November, in the doorway, that he was going to open it, and he did not, and his mother has told him twice that not doing it is the growth and that the reason is the same reason with a better coat on.** The registered letter from the Museum that was face down on Doreen Sarn's sideboard since 29 October is **in a coat pocket, unopened, and will be opened by her at that table on the Friday after the box, which is Sunday 6 December 2024 — the Friday is the fourth, and the prose in Chapters 47 and 48 has the sixth of December as a Sunday**, and nobody knows what is in it, including him. *(This entry said 'Friday the 28th or the 29th'. On the pinned line 28 November is a Saturday and 29 November is a Sunday, and the box opens on Friday 27 November at 16:00. The date is now the Friday after it, which is what `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt have said all along.)***

## Object states, as dramatised in Batch 0004

**The Sillick bell is stayed and unchanged. It is not answered, not demanding, not hollowed, not asked anything, and it is not touched. It rang once, on its own, in a corridor, in an empty building, in the form of a press on a plunger that nobody pressed — or in no form at all — and the man who heard it is the man who is going to have to explain what a bell is.** **The god is still unnamed. The crack is still open under the staple.**

**THE FOUR READS OF THE BATCH, and the answer is that there are none.** There is no reading of the case in Chapter 40 and no reach and no object answering anything. **What happened in Chapter 40 is that the object was not touched, was not asked, and was determined to be unchanged, and that the only reading in the chapter is a sentence arriving twice on a page.**

**The one uncontrolled event of the batch, and it is not an object:** the pressed-brass service bell at 11:52 on Wednesday 11 November. **It is a fuse, and nobody may say so in front of anybody, and the number of people behind it is every person in a public gallery and a library queue and a corridor and a laundrette, and the man who discovered it is the man who has to speak on the twenty-sixth.** He has three figures and cannot prove any of them and the word *or* in front of them is the honest part of the page.

## Elias's costs, as dramatised in Batch 0004

- **A correct decision that cost the case a thing it cannot get back** — the cupboard, and the open drawer, and four photographs in a police file.
- **A notice he could not execute, and a building that went quiet, and a bell he heard at 11:52 and told nobody about for four days.** Not a memory cost. A *concealment* cost, and the first time he has concealed a fact from the building in order to protect the building.
- **A number he has had since 25 October and could not account for, disclosed in a room as a disclosure-shaped fact that is not a lie and is worse than one.**
- **A document.** The pay office's own posted wage sheets for the last eight weeks of 1974, gone at 08:40 on Friday 13 November, for ever, and the person they are lost to is a driver called Renwick who signs about eleven thousand a year and has never opened one and was never told and cannot be told anything now, because telling him would be a thing done to a man to relieve a man.
- **A line in a notebook that says TO WHOM IS THIS LOST, and no form in the world that will take it.**
- **A letter not opened, on a promise that is real, in a laundrette in Low Ward, on a Monday night.**
- **A name in a public minute he is not allowed to be in the room for.**
- **A determination he caused, on a public register, naming a gallery and a date and a room with children in it.**
- **A work authorisation in a buff envelope with T. VENN on it, and two sentences on one page of a notebook, and he is not frightened, and he should be.**

**There is no second notebook entry and no second memory cost. The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched.**

## The seven times he did not ask, extended

Chapter 25, the field officer asked him for a name for the two and he said *I do not have one*. Chapter 25, he did not ask Hattie whether the two people in her room were the two. Chapter 27, he did not ask Dael what the figure was. Chapter 29, he did not ask Mara whether the form could be signed by somebody else. Chapter 30, he did not demand a count of a woman who had refused one. **Chapter 33, he did not ask Doreen Sarn for the address until Thursday 12 November, five weeks and one day after the sixth of October, and he had to ask her twice and he came in with the wrong sentence.** **Chapter 36, he did not go and find Hattie Bramm after she had given the sentence to Ivo Pell, and he wrote down why and then crossed most of it out.** **Seven. The sixth cost him the only sentence in the case he did not have. The seventh cost him nothing and he noticed, which is now a pattern rather than a coincidence.**

## The two withholdings, restated and both still live, and Batch 0004's additions

1. **Chapter 10, unchanged and not restaged.** The *result in her hand exactly as it comes* condition is intact and was defeated by a correct procedure. The *tell me everything, every time* condition was broken by silence and then by a lie to her face about the notebook. He tells Mara no untruth whatever about the reading itself. **In Chapter 40 he lies to nobody at all and discloses more than he has ever disclosed, and it is still not the notebook, and the notebook is a hundred and fifteen pages and item 7 of his own inventory.**
2. **Chapter 16, unchanged and not restaged.**
3. **NEW IN BATCH 0004 — the disclosure-shaped truth.** In Chapter 35 he is asked, by a transcript clerk, whether anybody in the room holds any other document relating to the eleven items. **He says no, and the room accepts it, and it is a falsehood, and Mrs Marsden writes *none produced* in a box on her pro forma and initials it.** What he does instead is put a *different* true disclosure in the room ten minutes later: that he has been in a room with an object from that consignment, has put his hand on it, has asked it one question out loud, that it gave him a number, that he cannot tell them the number and could not if he wanted to, and that the whole of it is in paragraph six of a cautioned statement in a police file, and he asks for it to be minuted that he said it in a room rather than waited to be asked in one. **It is the first time he has lied to a stranger about a document and it cost nothing and was not found, and that is the horror of it, and it is a third kind of withholding: not a lie to Mara, not a refusal in a street, but a false *no* to a question asked in an ordinary way by a competent person doing her job.**
4. **NEW IN BATCH 0004 — the concealment.** The bell at 11:52 on 11 November, told to nobody for four days. **It is not a lie to anybody. It is a fact held back from six people who would have acted on it inside a day.**

## The six Register clauses for Annexe D, in this order and in this exact wording, unchanged

* A plain bell with no maker's mark, no devotional content, and a mechanical repair is not a Quiet Saint. It is not required to be worked on, it should not be worked on, and the Accord asks that it be left where it is.

**And the printed heading that goes with them, which the Board read on 18 November and which is now on the page for the first time in the volume:**

> **QUIET ACCORD — SCHEDULE 12. CARRIED PATTERNS. Objects and records carrying a set form held in common use. ANNEXE D (PERMANENT). A PATTERN REQUIRING CONTINUED OBSERVANCE. TO BE DISPLAYED.**

# Fixed by Batch 0005 — new canon taken from finished prose (Chapters 41–50)

**Volume 01 is complete as drafted. Chapters 41–50 are Movement 5, "The Memorial", and they are the last batch of the volume.** Everything in this section outranks the Batch 0005 prompt and the Volume 01 outline where the two differ, and the deviations are itemised at the foot.

## The week-five day map, AS DRAFTED

**The pinned line stands and is unchanged, and it is now carried through December:** `Sun 15 · Mon 16 · Tue 17 · Wed 18 · Thu 19 · Fri 20 · Sat 21 · Sun 22 · Mon 23 · Tue 24 · Wed 25 · Thu 26 · Fri 27 · Sat 28 November`, and therefore **`Sun 29 · Mon 30 Nov · Tue 1 · Wed 2 · Thu 3 · Fri 4 · Sat 5 · Sun 6 · Mon 7 · Tue 8 · Wed 9 · Thu 10 · Fri 11 · Sat 12 · Sun 13 · … · Thu 17 December`.** **1 DECEMBER 2024 IS A TUESDAY. This is the ruling and it is load-bearing for Volume 02: the Batch 0005 prompt's own day map put 30 November at a Monday and 12 December at a Friday, which cannot both be true of the same week, and the prose had inherited the wrong one. Every December date in Chapters 47–50 has been re-derived from the pinned line.**
