Ch 47 and Ch 48 are dateline chapters and carry no single clock.

**RECORDED CORRECTION TO THE BATCH 0005 PROMPT, and the prompt was wrong twice.** The prompt says twice that **the books pallet leaves bay 4 on "Tuesday 25 November"**. **25 November 2024 is a WEDNESDAY on the pinned line** (24 = Tuesday, 25 = Wednesday, 26 = Thursday). The merchant's fortnightly collection is **WEDNESDAY 25 NOVEMBER at 08:00**, and the Director's written review is the **same morning at 09:25**, and the two are twenty-five minutes apart in a different building. **CORRECTED IN THE REVIEW PASS. This sentence was written by the drafting pass and it was false.** An independent reviewer checked it and found at least eight surviving dated defects, and the whole of December was wrong at the source. **Every date in Chapters 41–50 has now been re-checked against the pinned line and there are zero mismatches, and the only way to know that is to grep the chapters and not to read this file.**

**RECORDED CORRECTION TO THE 1974 DATES.** The count is the count called at the gate **on the morning of the thirtieth of October 1974, being the last working day**, and it is never given a weekday anywhere in Batch 0005. 1974 is not on the pinned calendar and is a real-world year: 22 October 1974 was a real Tuesday (canon, and the letter went into the mill's out-tray on it) and 30 October 1974 was a real Wednesday (canon: *the mill was closed on the Wednesday of its last trading week*), and **an earlier draft of Chapters 43, 45 and 46 said "Friday the thirty-first of October 1974", which is neither, and has been corrected in the prose.** The 1974 receipt's *collected on 30 Oct 1974, being the last working day* and the buyer's carbon's *gate book, with entries to 30 Oct* are the reason.

**The clearance book, and the site's own 2024 dates, are now fixed and they are the pinned line's.** `20 OCT — FRI` pre-clearance sweep · `22 OCT — THU` **gate hut to compound, 1 crate, 58 kg** · `23 OCT — FRI` **site contents to Broomhead Reach, 1 x 40ft steel, 482 kg in four lots, balanced, paper merchant** · `24 OCT — SAT` — · `25 OCT — SUN` site clear and hand back · `26 OCT — MON` site office, rear, low loader. **An earlier draft had these as SUN/MON/TUE/WED and they were wrong in four places; the twenty-third is a Friday, which is canon, because the container went over the wall on Friday the twenty-third at nine in the morning.**

## The four counts — state after Batch 0005

**94** — on the page in **Ch 46**, said aloud once in the Hall of Former Faiths at **10:08 on Friday 27 November 2024** by Hattie Bramm, gate clerk, in a room of eighty people, recorded by the conciliation service with her name against it. **It is not said again and she says so out loud: *I am not going to say it twice, so somebody write it down.*** **92 and 2 appear nowhere in Chapters 41–50 and are not referred to as figures.** **61** — not stated as a figure in this batch. **No total and no shortfall is stated anywhere in the batch, and the device that prevents it is a ruling by Field Officer Marsh on the Thursday evening that no figure is to be stated in the same breath as another figure from the same source, and that a difference is an adjudication, applied in advance and with the reason given aloud.**

**THE HARD RULE HELD AND IT IS NOW A PLOT MECHANISM.** Any place in this batch that needed a payroll figure wrote the conflict without a numeral. The shortfall in the memorial is read out of the bound wage book **name by name**, and it is legible on the page without anybody doing a sum, because the book has hours, rate and pay in columns set side by side and the pay column is the odd one out, and **the room is not given a total and the chapter does not contain one.** **The total is on a sheet of thin carbon in a woman's own hand in a front room in Ferry Bank and it is not in this volume and it is not in any Museum file.**

**The new numbers introduced, and none of them is a Sillick figure:** `58 kg` (the crate on the compound — a weight); `482 kg` and `494 kg` (weights, as before); `291 pages` (the book); `£140` (the hearing fee, as before); `£800 per named claimant` (the interim payment); `114 pp.` (the notebook); `a hundred and forty` (Hattie's list); `fifty` (lines on the schedule of interim payments, issued 2 December, the fifty-first being the Loach line — **a first draft had fifty-one and a fifty-second, which is one page from a locked numeral, and it was changed in the review pass**); `fifty-one` (a room on 7 November, which is a room and not a count of anybody); `about forty addresses` on the Hall's threshold list; `about 210 addresses` on a Sillick Lane postal round. **No fifth Sillick count exists.**

## New documents, formats, people and institutions introduced in Batch 0005

- **THE ATTENDANCE AUTHORISATION (one occasion).** One sheet, no number, five boxes: site · reason · Museum personnel attending, name and grade · **counter-signature required, a person who is not an officer or employee of this Museum** · date, time, expected duration. Written out by hand by the Director on Mon 23 Nov and given at the front desk. **Box 3 in her words: *Venn, E., night officer, suspended from night duty 07:00 Sun 11 Oct 2024, currently not in the employ of this Museum in any grade.*** **Box 4 is a tick and the tick is the Director's, and the reason she can give it is that she has never at any moment in nineteen years been an officer or an employee of this Museum.** Box 2 reads *to obtain a description of site material cleared in October 2024 for the purposes of the schedule of collections* and does **not** read *evidence in a wage claim*.
- **THE SITE ATTENDANCE — RISK ASSESSMENT, one page, D. ORDWAY, Mon 23 Nov.** Four hazards, written before the site was visited: *unlit external area, single lamp at gate* · *hardstanding, wet* · **cast-iron weighbridge, 18 in. hump, kerb at side. Trip and fall hazard** · *vehicle movement in compound, reverse only, banksman.* Controls: hi-vis, torch, **no lone working**, **one hour max**, no keys taken off the owner. **He does not cross line three out and the line is the reason the inquiry cannot dismiss him.**
- **THE GATE BOOK.** Hardback A4, plastic cover, four columns in pen — **date · who in · who with · time off** — about two hundred entries. On the night of 23 Nov it has **one line with two names in it, no job, and a vehicle registration, and a time off written in at about ten past one, in a car outside a casualty department at about twenty past one in the morning, at the request of a man on a trolley, and initialled by the gate man and not by the night supervisor**: *Present at removal* is not its wording and it is not a disposition record. **It is the only entry for that date and it is a document a solicitor can produce.** *A first draft had the time off at 00:40 written at 00:45, which is sixteen minutes before the fall and while both men were still on the hardstanding. Corrected in the review pass.*
- **THE SITE CLEARANCE BOOK.** Hardback, canvas spine, one line per load, eleven months, on a shelf above a kettle in a site cabin. A *copy of two lines* is what was brought to the Museum on a **disclosure form**. **The custodian's own copy is the company's and is not in the Museum.**
- **THE DISCLOSURE FORM, completed.** Mara Iles's eight-box form, printed, no number, no clause, print run of about four hundred. **Box 5, in the Director's hand: *Because nobody had asked me, and because until twenty-two minutes past eight on Sunday night this Museum did not know that it could not describe a consignment, and a Museum that cannot describe a consignment cannot prove that a consignment ever existed.*** Box 6: *request the document into a Museum file under the date of this request.* **Signed by the Director at 11:04 on Tue 24 Nov, six days after the request was made, and the Director wrote a two-line letter saying so and refused to have the date taken off the request.** (Anja Ward had promised on Tue 17 Nov to sign on Friday 20 Nov *or write a reason why not*, and she did both — she signed on the Friday, at 11:02, and the letter says why the six days happened.)
- **THE HOLD.** Reference **BQ4/2511/L2/04**, raised by Miss Arkwright on her own system, Tue 24 Nov at **09:09**, seventy-two hours, **referred upwards after that to a manager in an office in Northglass**, lapsing **09:09 on Friday 27 November**. Fields: item, quantity, reference, time, initials, basis. **There is no indemnity anywhere in it and the clerk says so twice, to two people, before she will do it, and the reason is that a bonded store does not sell the goods and does not care what they are: it sells a guarantee that nothing leaves without a piece of paper.**
- **THE LETTER OF NON-OBJECTION, Hallowgate Holdings, Wed 25 Nov, four paragraphs, signed by a partner of a firm at 14 Lincoln's Lane.** *We neither warrant nor disclaim… We do not authorise any movement out of bond, and we do not withhold any authority either… we accept that the risk of having been wrong is ours and not yours.* **The fourth paragraph — *we would rather this went into a room with the people in it than into a skip* — is the sentence that made it work.**
- **THE BONDED RELEASE, Thu 26 Nov, 11:20.** One line in a book with columns: *line · item · quantity · carrier · time out · signature.* **ITEM 4 — 1 off, bound, in polythene sleeve and tie as received, new strap applied 12.11. Weight per bay manifest, not reweighed. Released to carrier P. LOACH, no employer of record, against BQ4/2511/L2/04 and letter of 25.11 from Hallowgate Holdings. Time out 11:20. P. LOACH.* Bay 4 remaining: nothing.
- **THE ATTENDANCE ON BEHALF OF ANOTHER (NON-STAFF).** One sheet, Museum notepaper, printed, six boxes: name as it is to appear · in what capacity · what the person is to do · from–to · **grade (left EMPTY on purpose, and the emptiness is in the file)** · requested by · witness · agreed / not agreed. **Initialed *agreed* by P. Loach in the loading-bay office at about ten past three in the afternoon on 26 Nov, after he had put the bag down and not before.** Mara Iles signs *as Senior Conservator and Accession Registrar and not as an officer*, on the stated ground that she is not an officer and will not be one on a paper that puts a man in a bonded warehouse at eight in the morning. **It is at appendix 6 of the conciliation bundle. Appendix numbering in that bundle is load-bearing: 3 is the insurer's position, 4 is the form with a blank in the middle of it from a safe in Irving Quay, 5 is the terms of the handover, and 6 is this one.**
- **THE WORKERS' PANEL.** A sheet of A4, printed, designed by Hattie Bramm at her kitchen table, **which may not be amended and may not be added to by the Museum.** It carries the 1974 collection line, the Museum's own purple accession stamp struck through, the sentence **the series name is wrong and has been wrong since 1974 / nothing was disposed / this Museum paid no disposal fee and received nothing disposed**, and a last line that is a liberty she was told about and kept: *Wages were administered at the works by the pay office. The pay office kept its own book. That book is on the table.* **A worker's name is on it and it is a receipt's name, not a saint's: *collected by P. LOACH (fitter), Sillick Lane, Low Ward*, dated 7 Nov 1974, in a typewriter face in a box file behind a fire plan. The Museum's own plinth card stays in place beneath it.**
- **THE FALSE PAYROLL PAGE, identified, and the finding is on paper.** A clean typed sheet, better paper than anything else in the file, **dated the eleventh of November 1974 — four days after the purchase — in the same typewriter face as the 1974 receipt in the same box file, with no source line on it at all (no *received from*, no collector, no agent, no firm, no signature), and a total at the foot.** It is accessioned under the series **SILLICK MILL: ADMINISTRATIVE PAPERS, DISPOSED** in the same purple. **Mara Iles, in the Hall, at 10:35 on 27 Nov: *it is a forgery, it was made by this Museum, and I can prove that on the face of two pieces of paper and a date.* And: *the accession register of 1974 has a column for the person who presented the material, and the column for that item is empty, and in about four years somebody is going to read that register and find an empty column in the middle of a page and it is going to be the single most important thing on it.*** **Nobody in Volume 01 is named as having typed it and nobody ever will be.**
- **THE SCHEDULE OF CONTENTS, rebuilt and the finding is that it will not be finished.** First entry since 11 September, added Mon 24 Nov by M. ILES, about a consignment **that is not the Museum's**, and its first line is about the fitter's lamp in cupboard 14. Status document dated **9 Dec 2024, INCOMPLETE, expected to remain so**, going to the Board in March 2025 with about eleven entries, entries for 1974 to be marked *incomplete and expected to remain so*. **The reason is given: the entries require a decision about a series name and the decision is in an inquiry.**
- **THE INQUIRY: Board sub-group (inquiry), terms of reference, 1 Dec 2024, two pages, six items.** **Item 1 is the unlisted room and it is reserved to the Director**, and **item 1.2 is one line and it is the only line in forty-one pages three trustees agreed without anybody speaking:** that the sub-group has no need of the room and does not ask to be given it, and that the Director will open it herself as the first act of the inquiry at **09:50 on Sunday 13 December 2024**, at the same hour as the reopening of the Hall, and that **no member of the sub-group, no member of Museum staff and no member of the public will be admitted to the fourth level for that purpose except the Director, the Registrar, the conservator, and one person who is not Museum staff and is not a member of the public.** That person is Elias Venn, **has not been told, and is told by post, the note having gone into a box in Lantern Row at about four o'clock on Saturday 12 December, so it is read on Tuesday 15 December, which is after this volume.**
- **THE REVIEW MINUTE, Wed 25 Nov 09:25–09:55, four operative lines, and it is the ending of his job.** 1. Suspension **lifted**, no finding of fault, work order 4412-S closed with no finding. 2. **Appointment as night officer terminated with effect from Tuesday 8 December 2024** under clause 11 of the Museum's Constitution, **no fault being found and no right of appeal being conferred.** 3. **Barred from the building as from 1 December 2024 as a security matter**, save attendance at any hearing or inquiry at which he is a party or a witness, on notice, in company, not before eight in the morning. 4. **The Museum records that E. Venn has no conduct complaint outstanding, that the record contains no finding of misconduct, and that the Museum has not investigated the conduct of any person in the matter of the Hall of Former Faiths.** **Copy not to be given to the press.** *Handed to him by hand at the front desk at 20:30 on Sunday 29 November. He has thirteen days' notice from the review of 25 November, and the Card goes back on the key board at 21:40 the same night.*
- **CLAUSE 11 OF THE MUSEUM'S CONSTITUTION, two lines, borrowed from the Sillick Lane branch library:** a Director may determine the terms of the employment of any officer. **No reason required. No appeal, because there is no finding to appeal against. It is four hundred years old and is on the second page of a book in a public library and he had never read it.**
- **THE INTERIM PAYMENT.** **£800 per named claimant, on account, not a settlement, and the letter offering it says so four times.** It does **not** go to the people: it goes into the conciliation service's account, per name, as **a schedule of interim payments which is a public document**, and a person may come to the conciliation office on any weekday between ten and four and ask whether there is a line with their name on it. **Fifty lines on 2 December and nobody knows how many there ought to be.** The **fifty-first line reads:** `51 — LOACH, P. — Sillick Lane, Low Ward. Fitter. Collected, 30 Oct 1974, per Museum accession 1961/LW/41–52. Date of death not established. Estate traced: none. Unclaimed. Held pending.` **The 1974 receipt carries no house number and this line carries none, and a first draft gave him *s.14*, which is Hattie Bramm's and Cliff Sowerby's flat on the same row. Removed in the review pass.** **Nobody in this building wrote that line. It is four boxes on a form and a reference, and it is the only place in Volume 01 where a person from the 1974 receipt appears in a public financial document, and nobody in the room connects it to a man in overalls on a floor, and Elias Venn reads it twice and Mara Iles reads it over his shoulder and neither of them says anything at all.**
- **CLAIM NO. CC/26/1104**, municipal conciliation service, dated 2 Dec 2024, nine pages, headed *in the matter of payments for the final trading week of October 1974, Sillick Mill, Low Ward*.
- **THE WENDEL CASE RESTORATION, Saturday 5 Dec 2024.** A line in the staff register, a 2012 leavers column, a new hand, an entry in a leaver's card, **an enquiry in the public record**; a letter to Ivy Case at 4 Sillick Lane signed by the Director and countersigned by the registrar, four paragraphs, *we were wrong… there is no body and the Museum has looked and the family should not be asked to hope.* Initials on the register: **M. ILES and A. WARD and nobody else.**
- **Form A/3 — REQUESTER'S FURTHER QUESTION, Quiet Accord.** The same form as QA/CR/1187. **Box 2 is a box for a period, and this is the only form in the whole of Volume 01 with a box for a period in it, and it is the only number in the volume that is about the future rather than about what has already been counted.** Filed **Sat 12 Dec 2024, registered, acknowledgment of delivery, eleven stamps, £4.60**, to the Registry's public counter address. **He had it for twenty-three days, had the question in the first four, and spent the other nineteen frightened of putting it in a box and sending it.** The question: **whether, before it certifies, re-certifies, or permits the continued display in a public room of any Schedule 12 carried object, the Registry will ask the question the schedule has no box for: how many people are in the room, and how old are they.** **Period sought: TWELVE MONTHS**, with the reason written in the space provided. **It has not been answered and Volume 02 opens with it outstanding.**
- **THE LETTER TO HALLOWGATE HOLDINGS, written on the back of a hospital parking ticket in a car on the night of Mon 23 / Tue 24 Nov and posted Tue 24 Nov from a café on the corner, four lines, dated 24 November, signed by him and not by anybody else**, and the Hallowgate reply of **Tue 1 Dec, three lines:** *paint, a length of conduit, one office chair, and a bundle of papers which was not listed at the time and which we believe to be invoices. We hold no schedule of the site contents. We are not able to assist further.* **The letter is NOT in a Museum file, by his own decision, under clause six, and it is in a cardboard folder on a shelf between a boiler and a chair in a rented room, and the folder is labelled in his own hand.**
- **THE HALL'S THRESHOLD LIST.** About four hundred addresses to which the Museum sends a printed card. **Cliff Sowerby's card is dated Wednesday the nineteenth of November 2024 and says: *a determination has been made in respect of an object displayed in the Hall of Former Faiths and is available to any person without charge at the address below. Reference QR/11/2024/0419.*** Hattie Bramm took it in through a box in his own front door on 30 October, carried it in her coat all day, and put it in a drawer on the thirtieth because she would not put a card about a bell in a drawer with a hundred and forty names. **She did not open it. It was not addressed to her and she is not a person who opens other people's post.**

## New people in Batch 0005

- **The site custodian**, about fifty, Brill's, a site radio, a pen behind his ear, a fluorescent jacket, and a **gate book**. **He has moved about four hundred loads and has never once known what was in one.** He will not say who told him to expect the Museum, will not say what was in a crate he moved, has never once gone into the corner of a gate hut where a desk was, and says *that's not a crime, that's a crate.* **Surname not given. He is the volume's argument in a man.**
- **Mr M. Green, FRCS (Orth)**, consultant, and eight lines of X-ray report dated 26 Nov 02:20, including *following a fall from standing height onto a wet surface in an outdoor compound* and *no surgical indication, analgesia, sling, district fracture clinic 8 Dec, no chest injury, no head injury, no admission.* **That sentence is more accurate than anything anybody wrote about it afterwards, including the man it happened to.**
- **Mrs Farrier**, 58, key board, security room, **fourteen years**, who has never asked anybody why and who has written about nine lines in fourteen years that had no leaver's card against them, and who said *that's the first time I have ever written that in that book* and *it's not a thing, it's a Sunday*, and **who did not look in anybody's coat.**
- **A postman of about fifty** on the Fisher Lane and Sillick round, **about 210 addresses, one man, the other postman long-term sick**, with a handheld and a job sheet, at the bottom of Sillick Lane at about half past eleven on Sunday 13 December.
- **A Registry assessor of about fifty-five**, not Roth and not Grange, the rota, in the Gallery of Unnamed Hands from 14:00 to about 16:30 on 24 November, with a body camera and a printed list. **She recorded *no authorising officer identified* in box three about a drawer shut by a suspended night guard on a Sunday, and wrote the word *unauthorised* nowhere at all, because *unauthorised is a disciplinary word and I am a Registry assessor and it is not mine*.** **She is not named and nobody in the building has ever spoken to her.**
- **Margaret Priest**, about fifty, **nineteen years** at the front desk, who read a day book out loud to a man standing on the other side of it and then wrote the only line in that book in nineteen years that does not contain a name.

## The state of the four locks, as of Sunday 13 December 2024

- **The bell.** Plinth 34, sealed, re-anchored, Annexe D, **stayed under Annexe E**, unchanged, **unopened, untouched, unapproached, and the cover was never put on it.** **The Hall was shut for sixteen days — closed to the public at 11:00 on Friday 27 November under the minute, shutters on at 16:10 on Saturday 28 November — and reopened at 09:50 on Sunday 13 December 2024. SEE THE RULING BELOW: the outline's *closes for a month* and the prompt's *one month after the memorial* are both unsatisfiable against each other and against the pinned calendar, and the prose follows the Board's minute, which sets 13 December.** **It gave one note at 10:54 on 27 November and one at 11:04 on 13 December and no others, and there is no second note in the building's record between the two — because on 13 December there was no Museum staff on the door and not one of the forty people in the room wrote anything down — and the crack in its shoulder is still open under the staple and is not going to heal.** **The god is not returned, not destroyed and not worshipped. It is acknowledged — a public correction, with a witness and a record — and it stopped asking because every term of the demand was met.**
- **The unlisted room.** **Shut and latched until 12:04 on Friday 27 November 2024**, when the secondary latch on the corridor side released itself and the door moved about two inches and stopped and **was not touched and was not opened.** Mara Iles wrote the time in the margin of a dot-matrix printout in nine seconds, stood on the other side of the corridor for about two minutes, and left a message on a board. Elias Venn was on the half landing below, thirty feet below and around a corner, and learned the time at about 12:20. **The door was opened by the Director at 09:50 on Sunday 13 December with the Registrar, the metals conservator and one person who is not Museum staff and is not a member of the public, and Elias Venn was not in the building and does not know it until the Monday.** *The volume's last gesture is that door and it is the smallest thing the object ever does in the whole volume.*
- **1961/LW/44.** **Cage 2, bay 9, opened on Friday 27 November at 16:00 in a front room at 12 Ferry Bank with no Museum employee in the room.** Item 5 is a carbon copy of a pay sheet in a pay clerk's own hand, made at her own table in November 1974. **Item 3's gate book was not needed and the box has not been catalogued and is not to be.**
- **The bound wage book for October 1974.** **In the custody of the conciliation service from Thursday 26 November at 11:20.** Not accessioned, not to be accessioned, not on a plinth, not in a case, handed over on a trestle at 10:41 on the Friday and carried out of a public door by a man with a name on a piece of paper and a grade box that is empty.
- **The pay run.** The mill's own posted wage sheets are pulp. **The final pay run now exists in exactly two forms and will never be corroborated again.**
- **Cliff Sowerby.** Told, on Saturday 28 November at about half past ten, at 14 Sillick Lane, by Hattie Bramm. **He read the card and asked one question: *is it the one off the poster?* He has a place on a list for the reopening and Hattie Bramm is taking him on the tram.**

## Elias's costs, as dramatised in Batch 0005

- **A real injury, on a site, on a Monday, to a man of forty-four who went over a weighbridge hump in the dark in November, and nobody is to blame, and that is four separate true things and not a formula.**
- **A form he gave somebody else's employer for, and a colleague's name in a gate book in a compound in Low Ward.**
- **He told Mara Iles about T. VENN on Mon 23 Nov at 08:35, in her office, after two days of not telling anybody and on a date he had written down, and he gave her the fourth page from the end of the notebook with a receipt written on the back of it and dated by her.**
- **He could not produce his own name for fifty-seven minutes**, and it was named on a card in advance as the cost and he was told he could not choose it, and **Hattie Bramm said it out loud in a public gallery rather than let a registrar's box stay blank in front of eighty people.**
- **He handed over a hundred and fourteen pages and did not get them back, and they are a Museum record and not about him.**
- **A post. And a card on a key board with a time against it and no leaver's card, because there is no leaver's card for him either, and nobody looked in his coat.**
- **THE COST CURVE DOES NOT RESOLVE.** **There is no second notebook entry and no second memory cost. The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched, unrestated, unreferred to and unstabilised. The same sentence is on the fourth page from the end once, in his own hand, and nowhere else on that page, in either direction, and that is the whole of the first displacement and it cost nothing and it is not a memory cost and it is sensory and not amnesic. The first major memory-displacement cost remains reserved for Volume 03.** The new cost is **a name**, and a name is a bounded, chosen, named-in-advance cost in the same family as the four seconds of 25 October, and it is not displacement, and nothing is mislaid and no sequence is lost.

## The withholdings, restated, and Batch 0005's additions

1. **Chapter 10.** Unchanged and not restaged.
2. **Chapter 16.** Unchanged and not restaged.
3. **The false *no* in Irving Quay, Ch 35.** Unchanged, and **named by him in a public room on 26 November without being asked to and knowing exactly what it costs**: *the clerk wrote *none produced* in a box on a pro forma and initialled it, and she did not know she was writing a false thing, and I did not tell her, and it cost nothing and was not found.*
4. **The bell at 11:52 on 11 November.** **He never told anybody, in this volume, to anybody.** The three measurements are on the page in Ch 46 — the latch at 12:04, the dead bracket with no bell on it, the repeater line dead since 15 October — and **he does not say any of it aloud in front of anybody in Volume 01, and the volume ends with him not having said it.**
5. **NEW — the notebook.** **The only private channel in the business is closed, and it is closed from her side, on Friday 11 December, in a room, with the door shut, on a date he gave her on 23 November and she wrote down so that he could not move it.** He keeps the notebook. **She does not.**
6. **NEW — the twenty-eight days.** He told Mara Iles **by telephone, after he had posted the form**, in ninety-one seconds, and the reason he posted it first is on the page: *if I know that I am going to tell her, I will not tell her, and then it is a thing I have done alone.* **He still has not told the Director, and he never will in this volume, and the Director has no idea the question exists.**

## The nine times he did not ask, extended

Six weeks in: the field officer; Hattie, about the two in her room; Dael, about the figure; Mara, about the form; Hattie, for a count. **Ch 33, Doreen Sarn, for the address, until Thursday 12 November. Ch 36, Hattie Bramm, again, after she had handed the only sentence in the case to somebody else.** **Ch 41, number 14 Sillick Lane, on the Saturday evening — the eighth, and the first one that was not a question to a person.** **Ch 44, the two women in the third row with a girl between them, on a pavement in Ferry Bank, on the Thursday night — the ninth, and Doreen Sarn said *good*, and it is the only one of the nine that cost nothing and was the right decision and he got no credit for it from anybody.** **Ch 50, a door at number 88, Sillick Lane, **Sunday** 13 December, half past eleven in the morning — and this time he knocks, and that is the end of the list and the end of the volume.**

# BATCH 0005 — REVIEW-PASS RULINGS

**Added in the review-and-repair pass against `logs/batch-0005.review.log`. These are rulings, not notes. Where a ruling changes a date, a figure or a name, the prose has already been changed to match and the change is in the chapter files, not only here.**

## R1 — THE CALENDAR. The pinned line governs, and it governs December too.

**`Sun 15 · Mon 16 · … · Sat 28 November`, and therefore `Sun 29 · Mon 30 Nov · Tue 1 · Wed 2 · Thu 3 · Fri 4 · Sat 5 · Sun 6 · Mon 7 · Tue 8 · Wed 9 · Thu 10 · Fri 11 · Sat 12 · Sun 13 December`, and `Thu 17 December`.**

**1 December 2024 is a TUESDAY. 13 December 2024 is a SUNDAY. Both are load-bearing for Volume 02.**

The Batch 0005 prompt's day map is internally inconsistent at the November/December boundary — it puts 30 November at a Monday and 12 December at a Friday, and no single week can produce both — and the draft had followed the December half. **Every December weekday in Chapters 47–50 has been re-derived from the November pinned line.** `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` has been corrected to match. **Check any weekday in Volume 02 against this line and not against the prompt.**

**The October part of the manuscript's own calendar is a separate and older problem and has NOT been changed.** The site's clearance book (`22 OCT — THU`, `23 OCT — FRI`, `25 OCT — SUN`, `26 OCT — MON`) and the 1974 dates are not derivable from the November line, because the November line is pinned only from the fifteenth. Do not try to reconcile October with December.

## R2 — THE HALL'S CLOSURE IS SIXTEEN DAYS, and the outline's "a month" is not honoured.

**The Hall of Former Faiths was closed to the public at 11:00 on Friday 27 November 2024 under resolution 2.8, the shutters went on both doors at 16:10 on Saturday 28 November, and it reopened at 09:50 on Sunday 13 December 2024. Sixteen days.**

`outline/volume-01.md` says *The Hall closes for a month* and `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` says the Director opens the door *one month after the memorial* — while the same prompt pins Chapter 50 to Saturday 13 December. **A month after 27 November is 27 December, which is a Sunday on this line, and moving the reopening there would have cost the volume its final image, its last door, the close of the count and the first page of Volume 02.** The prose therefore follows the **Board minute**, which sets 13 December, and the position is:

- **The Board minute sets the date and the Director moves nothing**, because the closure is a condition and a condition is a condition, and that is the volume's whole argument about this institution.
- **No chapter calls it a month.** The four lines on the card outside the door, the inquiry's terms of reference, and the schedule-of-collections memorandum are all consistent with sixteen days.
- **Volume 02 must not describe the closure as a month.**

## R3 — THE NOTE SOUNDED TWICE, and the building's record is silent about the second time.

**Plinth 34 gave one note at 10:54 on Friday 27 November 2024, in a room of eighty, and one at 11:04 on Sunday 13 December 2024, in a room of about forty. It did not come again.**

Ch 46 originally listed *the thirteenth* among the days it did not come; Ch 50 has the note at 11:04 on the thirteenth because `outline/volume-01.md`'s final chapter image requires it. **Both are now true, and the reconciliation is the book's own logic: the Museum's record of 13 December is a sheet of notepaper on a stand by the turnstiles, because there was no Museum staff on the door, and not one of the forty people in the room wrote anything down.** Ch 46's sentence is scoped to *the record of this building* and to the sixteen days; Ch 50 says on the page that the only document anywhere about the second note is a line in a notebook.

**This is not a second demand and it is not an escalation.** The demand ended on 27 November, in the terms it was formed in, and Chapter 46 says so on the page. **Volume 02 may not have the object ask for anything.**

## R4 — ELIAS VENN IS ON THE PAYROLL. Stated once, everywhere, with no contradiction anywhere.

**He is the ninth entry in the fixed-positions file of 16 September 2024 and his hours are administered by the municipal payroll bureau at cost centre M.Q.S. 4. The remittance has gone every month without a break and the Museum has been sending it. He is on a list.**

What he lost on 11 October was **the right to be instructed** — not a job, not a grade, and not his name in any book. Every place in Chapters 41–50 that had him asserting the opposite as fact has been corrected: Chapter 42's doorway with Perry Loach (*you can't speak to it, you're not the employer, you're a man who can't be instructed about it*), Chapter 43's Board room (*a building he was not on the staff of*), Chapter 44's hearing, where he now says the whole position out loud in front of thirty-one people, and Chapter 47's terms of reference, which now carry a **finding** — no officer, employee or former employee has ever borne the name at the foot of the 2006 instruction, and the initials on the authorisation belong to a person on the fixed-positions file — instead of an **assumption**.

**The device that actually releases the wage book is untouched and is still a cleaning-contract employee.** Anja Ward's shape is a category of person who is on no payroll and no rota and appears on no return of this building's, and whose employer is a services contractor. **That is Perry Loach and it is canon** (`state/continuity.md`: the cleaners are not on the bureau and are not on M.Q.S. 4). **The two statements are compatible and both are on the page. Do not let a later batch confuse them.**

**The Director's own wording on the attendance authorisation of 23 November is preserved and is correct: *currently not in the employ of this Museum in any grade*, and the sub-group's minute of 1 December quotes her and not him. That is a Director's careful phrase on a one-occasion form and is not the same claim.**

## R5 — THE 1976 CLASSIFICATION SHEET. The thread is open and its author is not named.

`state/continuity.md` records, from Batch 0003: *the typed classification sheet of 1976 — the page that assigned the papers to the administrative papers, disposal series — is **not** among the dead letters, and that is a thing to be looked for.*

**Chapter 45 had closed that thread by inventing an author** — *a woman in a laundrette in about 1976* — who cannot be Helen Venn and who is nobody. **The prose now says only what is known: a classification sheet, dated in the middle of the seventies, for about four hundred pieces of a dead mill's paperwork, which put them into a series called *administrative papers, disposal*, and which has not been found and may never be found.**

**The sheet is a live open thread. Its author is unknown and may stay unknown. Volume 02 may find the sheet. Volume 02 may not name a person who wrote it unless it does so on paper, in a file, in a room, with a name against it.**

## R6 — THE CHAPTER 10 LIE IS NOW DISCLOSED. Spent canon, recorded rather than cut.

Chapter 44 has Elias Venn tell a room of thirty-one people, in a statement read into a record that Field Officer Marsh undertakes will be read out in full to anybody who asks for a copy, that **the transcript clerk in Irving Quay wrote *none produced* in a box on a pro forma and initialled it, that she did not know she was writing a false thing, and that he did not tell her, and that it cost nothing and it was not found.** Mara Iles is in the second row.

**`state/character-state.md` had this as *unrepaired and not to be repaired by an apology*. That position has changed and the character-state entry has been changed to match. The lie is still not repaired by an apology. It is repaired by disclosure, in public, in a document, by the man who did it, at the moment when it costs him the corroboration of his own number.** Nothing further is owed on it and **no later batch may restage it as a confession scene.**

## R7 — THE FIFTY-SEVEN MINUTES IS RULED, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.

**From 10:54 to 11:52 on Friday 27 November 2024 he could not produce his own surname. It was named on a card in advance as the cost, he was told he could not choose it, and it was said out loud in a public gallery by a woman of fifty-four rather than let a registrar's box stay blank in front of eighty people.**

- **It is reversible, and it came back when he wrote a time down on a half landing.**
- **It is not a memory cost and it is not displacement.** Nothing is mislaid, no sequence is lost, and no name is gone that he did not get back.
- **There is no second notebook entry and no second memory cost. The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched. The first major memory-displacement cost remains reserved for Volume 03.**
- **Volume 02 may not escalate this without recording the decision first.** A first draft of the batch summary ruled it closed in a paragraph; that was not a ruling. This is.

| Figure | What it is | Ruling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `58 kg` | the crate, gate hut to compound, 22 Oct | **four places**, twice in the clearance book and twice in Dael Ordway's mouth. A first draft had 61 kg in the two clearance-book lines and the summary claimed all three were fixed. |
| `291 pages` | the bound wage book | **twice** in Ch 44, and the release line no longer carries an authorial false start reading *Sixty-one pages of a bound book.* |
| `£140` | the hearing fee | the hearing fee, paid by Hattie Bramm at the door, **not a claim on anybody's head** |
| `£800` | interim payment, per named claimant, on account | not a settlement |
| `50` / `51` | lines on the schedule of interim payments, 2 Dec | **fifty lines; the fifty-first is the Loach line.** A first draft had fifty-one and a fifty-second, one page from a locked numeral. |
| `114 pp.` | the notebook, handed over 11 Dec | against a hundred and fifteen in the book |
| `eighty` | the memorial attendance, 27 Nov | **every crowd reference in Chapters 41–50 is now eighty** where it is the memorial. A first draft mixed seventy and eighty in eleven places. |
| `about 210` / `about 400` | the Sillick Lane postal round / the Hall's threshold list | addresses, not people owed |
| `482 kg` / `494 kg` | weights of paper | weights, refused twice in dialogue, and the ledger's own line is a *balance* |

**THE FOUR COUNTS ARE UNTOUCHED. 94 is said aloud once, by a person with standing, at 10:08 on 27 November, in a room of eighty, and she says out loud that she will not say it twice. 92 and 2 appear nowhere in Chapters 41–50 and are not referred to as figures. 61 is not stated as a figure. No total and no shortfall is stated anywhere in the batch, and nobody performs or proposes a subtraction. There is no fifth Sillick number.**

## R9 — APPENDIX NUMBERING IN THE CONCILIATION BUNDLE, now that three documents shared a number.

`3` the insurer's position, in the Board pack · `4` the form in a safe in Irving Quay with a blank in the middle of it, and the only one anybody has ever written down about consent · `5` the terms of the handover of the wage book, agreed on the Wednesday across four inches of a conservation office · `6` **ATTENDANCE ON BEHALF OF ANOTHER (NON-STAFF)**, the form with the grade box Mara Iles left empty, initialled *agreed* by P. Loach in the loading-bay office at about ten past three on 26 November. **Appendix numbering is load-bearing in this novel. Do not reuse a number.**

## R10 — WHAT THE PROSE NOW SAYS ABOUT A PERSON WHO WAS WRONG, and the two places it does not.

**Doreen Sarn is seventy-eight.** She is seventy-eight in eight chapters and in four state files. **The Batch 0005 summary asserted at one point that her age had been changed to twenty-eight; it never was, and the assertion has been removed.**

**Arthur Neve is seventy-eight.** A first draft had him at fifty-four in one clause of Ch 44 and seventy-eight in the same sentence; the fifty-four is cut.

**Mrs Angela Vessey, Chair of the Board, is sixty-four**, and she is the trustee who found the hole in the Casket file. **A trustee of about seventy sits at the same table, is a different person, and says almost nothing before lunch.** Keep them apart.

**Elias Venn is thirty-two.** He is thirty-two in the state files and now in every mouth. **Two lines in Chapters 48 and 49 had a director and a registrar quoting twenty-four at him; both are thirty-two.**

**Dael Ordway is forty-four.** A line in Chapter 45's cost list had him at seventy-four; he is forty-four, which is also what the eleven years of nights is built on.

**Margaret Priest** is about fifty and has taken messages at a counter for nineteen years, and the only line in that book in nineteen years that does not contain a name is hers. **Mrs Farrier is fifty-eight** and has done the key board for fourteen years, and *it's not a thing, it's a Sunday* is what she says on the night he gives the card back, which is a Sunday.

## R11 — THE MEMORIAL'S PRESS LINE IS ISSUED AFTER THE EVENT.

**At about half past eleven on Friday 27 November 2024, from a corridor, to a woman from a paper who had been standing outside since ten. Twenty-nine words. She wrote it on a pad, gave it to the front desk, and would not give it to a journalist herself.**

The line reads: *The Museum of Quiet Saints held a meeting this morning in the Hall of Former Faiths. The Museum's own records were placed in the room and available to read.*

**A first draft of Chapter 45 dated the line at nine o'clock in the morning and quoted it as reporting a meeting held *yesterday*, which is a press line dated before the event it reports.** It is now issued after, in Chapter 46, in the corridor, where nobody in the room heard her do it.

## R12 — THREE SURVIVING WEEKDAY DEFECTS IN CHAPTERS 27 AND 30. Found, not fixed, and here on purpose.

**Found by the Batch 0005 review pass while re-deriving December, and NOT repaired, because Chapters 27 and 30 belong to Batch 0003 and are not this phase's files. They are recorded so that the next pass inherits them rather than re-derives them.**

There is one governing calendar for the manuscript and it is not two calendars. The site's clearance book fixes **23 October 2024 as a Friday**, and everything follows from it, including the November line this batch used:

> `Fri 23 · Sat 24 · Sun 25 · Mon 26 · Tue 27 · Wed 28 · Thu 29 · Fri 30 · Sat 31 October` → `Sun 1 · Mon 2 · Tue 3 · Wed 4 · Thu 5 · Fri 6 · Sat 7 · Sun 8 · Mon 9 · Tue 10 · Wed 11 · Thu 12 · Fri 13 · Sat 14 · Sun 15 · Mon 16 · Tue 17 · Wed 18 · Thu 19 · Fri 20 · Sat 21 · Sun 22 · Mon 23 · Tue 24 · Wed 25 · Thu 26 · Fri 27 · Sat 28 · Sun 29 · Mon 30 November` → `Tue 1 December` onward, so **13 December is a Sunday.**

A scripted sweep of every *weekday + date + month* phrase in Chapters 1–50 finds four mismatches. **One of them was in this batch and is fixed** — `chapter-0047.md` had *Wednesday the nineteenth of November* and 19 November is a **Thursday**. **The other three are in Batch 0003 and stand:**

1. **`chapter-0027.md:206`** — *the cage goes back into bay 9 on **Friday** the first of November.* **1 November 2024 is a Sunday.**
2. **`chapter-0030.md:286`** — *it had gone back into bay 9 on **the Friday** the first of November.* **1 November is a Sunday.**
3. **`chapter-0030.md:5`** — *it had been in the Museum's own lobby since **Monday** the fourth of November.* **4 November is a Wednesday.** The date is right and the date is canon in three later chapters (`chapter-0036.md`, `chapter-0041.md` twice, `chapter-0049.md`), so **only the weekday is wrong.**

**All three are load-bearing in one direction only: the *date* is right and the *weekday* is wrong in every case, so the repair is to change three weekday words and nothing else. No date moves, no event moves, and no plot beat is touched.** They are recorded here rather than fixed because this pass does not own those chapters, and a fix applied silently from the wrong phase is how this manuscript got into the state it is in.

## REPAIR PASS, 2026-09-26 — WEEKDAYS, THE WORKING-DAY COUNT, AND THREE PIECES OF MECHANICAL DAMAGE

**An independent review of Batch 0001 found the batch's own verification unsound and eleven weekday errors it had missed. All are fixed. No story movement was changed, no chapter was rewritten, and the outline of the batch is untouched.** The lesson, which is the same lesson the four Volume 01 passes taught, is now written into the top of this file as well:

- **Where the weekday labels in Chapters 51–60 came from: the real 2024/25 Gregorian calendar, not the pinned line.** Under the pinned line every date in the batch runs two weekdays ahead of the real one — real `Thu 5 Dec 2024` is `Sat 5 Dec 2024` here, real `Mon 23 Dec 2024` is `Wed 23 Dec 2024`, real `Tue 7 Jan 2025` is `Thu 7 Jan 2025`, real `Thu 9 Jan 2025` is `Sat 9 Jan 2025`. **The date was almost always right and the weekday label was the leftover.** Read every weekday in this batch as a suspect, not as a fact.
- **The batch's own sweep could not see them.** A regex of the shape `weekday + day + month` finds **two** such phrases in the whole batch (`ch51:17`, `ch58:6`) and both were correct. All the defects were in the ordinal form — *Monday the twenty-third of December* — which that shape cannot match. **A sweep that finds two hits in fifty thousand words has not run.** The pattern has to carry an ordinal alternative.
- **The count-lock greps were documented without their word boundaries.** Plain `grep` for `94` returns four hits, all inside `1994`; for `61`, four, all inside `1961`. The locks are intact, but the command as written in the old `current.md` does not prove it. `grep -E` with `\b` does.

**What changed, chapter by chapter, and the reason for each.**

**Three things in this batch looked wrong to the same sweep and were left alone, and a later pass should not 'fix' them:**

1. **`chapter-0058.md:170`, *I am going to be a hundred and forty years old on Friday.*** The entry is written on Wednesday 30 December and **1 January 2025 is a Friday**. It is correct, and Chapter 59's *the Friday before* and *the afternoon of New Year's Day* both depend on the same fact.
2. **`chapter-0052.md:68`, *Sunday the eighth — no, the sixth.*** A counter clerk misremembers in front of a stranger and lands on the truth, which is 6 December, a Sunday. The stumble is the beat.
3. **`chapter-0055.md:70`, *That was Wednesday's idea … and Wednesday is a label room.*** A joke about a person conflated with their room, not a date. It reads oddly and it is not a calendar error.

**Out of scope, logged not touched.** The same corrected sweep run across `chapters/volume-01/` returns **six** phrases whose weekday does not match the pinned line: `ch15:7` (*Tuesday 21 October*), `ch27:206` and `ch30:286` (*Friday the first of November*), `ch30:5` (*Monday the fourth of November*), `ch35:240` (*Monday the twenty-first of October 1974*, a 1974 date, and 1974 in this book runs on the real calendar), and `ch43:141` (*Saturday 13 December 2024*, inside a quoted Board minute whose reopening is 09:50 on **Sunday** 13 December). **Volume 01 is complete and has had four review passes. This phase did not touch it, and Batch 0002 must not either.** Whoever opens a Volume 01 pass should run the same pattern and decide, per hit, whether it is a fault or a character's error.

## THE SUBJECT OF VOLUME 02, AS IT STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 60

**A row of flats is losing its name fields.** The horror is administrative and there is nothing in it that is supernatural, and the mechanism is now on the page in full:

> **A delivery walk sheet has a NAME field per address. A reason code, NCO — NO CURRENT OCCUPANT, printed at the foot of a change log, is marked *set by walk survey only*. The consequence of the code, printed in the same log, is: *record closed to delivery; occupier field not required; item returned or offered to neighbour.* A handheld will not hand a postman an item it cannot account for, so nothing is delivered, and a household that cannot be delivered to does not exist for anything else either, because everything else is fed off the same occupancy field.**

**The chain, four miles, and it is documented end to end:**

| # | System | Building | What it holds | What it is |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Field operations / delivery | delivery office, **Nether Kiln Lane** | the walk sheet, the change log, the reason code, the operator number | **upstream. A body presses a key at about four in the afternoon on a Tuesday.** |
| 2 | District addressing service | records office, **Marsh Lane**, about four miles away | the daily file, 04:1x, to about nine hundred subscribers; the supplied-file archive; the inbound change schedule; clause 7 | applies inbound changes from delivery codes **without local verification** |
| 3 | The Museum of Quiet Saints | **three quarters of a mile from the delivery office** | the Hall's threshold-group block: 412 rows, a filter, **not versioned, superseded values not retained** | **downstream, and has never been told it is downstream of a delivery sheet** |
| 4 | The electoral roll, the council tax base, the banks, the doctor's, the school | everywhere | fed off the same occupancy field | does not of itself alter the existence of the address or any person's legal residence |

**And the definition, which is the answer to the batch and the reason the batch's question is still open. It is the head of the district addressing service's own occupancy definition, on the first page of a walk-survey instruction given to about two hundred and thirty postmen, and it is two sentences:**

> **OCCUPANCY. The name of the person or organisation responsible for the connection of services at this address.**
> **Used for the recovery of supplies and for the routing of correspondence. A change of occupancy does not of itself alter the existence of the address, the number of persons at it, or any person's legal residence.**

**A name field in this city is a payee field. It is for the bill.** And the furthest thing from the beginning of the chain is a public museum that has printed somebody else's billing contact on a card about a bell for about a hundred and forty years.

## THE DATES THAT ARE NOW CANON, AND THEY ARE ALL FOUR DAYS AND EVERY ONE OF THEM MATTERS

- **Sat 5 Dec 2024, 16:22** — NCO keyed on a walk survey at **88 Sillick Lane**, operator **OP-2201**. **This is the date in both systems and it is the cliffhanger.**
- **Sun 6 Dec 2024, 03:41** — the delivery system's nightly refresh clears the occupier field on any record carrying NCO set in the previous twenty-four hours. Not a keying.
- **Sun 6 Dec 2024, 04:11** — the district's file goes out with the blank in it. **About four hours after. Upstream, and not by anybody's hand.**
- **Thu 10 Dec, 16:04** — 84 and 90 Sillick Lane, OP-2201. **Fri 11 Dec, 09:47** — 80 Sillick Lane, OP-2207.
- **26, 27, 28 November, about four in the afternoon** — **nine** addresses off a road called Broad Street, three days, OP-2201.
- **12 Dec 2024** — the Museum's **annual acknowledgement** to its Hall threshold group goes out. **It is printed off the block and it has nine blanks in it and nobody reads a print run in its own print room.** Nine returned undeliverable, 15 and 16 December. This is the first evidence the Museum had and it did not know it had it.
- **Thu 19 Nov 2024** — the determination notification, 412 cards, no blanks, posted. Cliff Sowerby's is delivered. (Chapter 47 and `state/continuity.md` both give 19 November as a **Thursday** on the pinned line.)
- **Wed 30 Dec 2024** — the proposed disposition posted. **Thu 31 Dec 2024, 15:00** — the Hall of Former Faiths shuts. **Wed 13 Jan 2025, 10:00** — it reopens. **Ten working days counted from the posting, 30 December included, with 1 January a public holiday and Saturdays and Sundays not counted: 30 and 31 December; 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11, 12 and 13 January. The tenth is the thirteenth.** The Director had the count done twice and minuted the day of the week. **A first draft said *the tenth working day of the year is the thirteenth*, which is not true on any convention — the thirteenth of January 2025 is the eighth working day of that year — and it was corrected in the review pass. Do not put that sentence back.**

**THE OTHER TWO ARITHMETICS IN THE BATCH WERE CHECKED IN THE SAME PASS AND ALL THREE NOW COMPUTE. Do not 'fix' them.**

| Arithmetic | Where | Method the text states | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Hall's closure is **ten working days** | Ch 58, on the notice and in the Director's mouth | from the posting, **30 December included**, 1 January a public holiday, weekends out: 30 and 31 Dec; 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11, 12, **13** Jan | **Exact.** The tenth is Wednesday 13 January 2025. |
| Condition two **failed for twenty-one hours** | Ch 55's opening line | 14:20 Wed 23 Dec + 21 hours | **Exact.** It lands at 11:20 on Thursday the 24th, which is where Ivo Pell is. |
| **Clause 7's twentieth working day** | Ch 60, and the handoff | from Sun 15 Dec, **first counted day Mon 16 Dec**, weekends out, 1 January a public holiday: 16–20 and 23–27 and 30–31 Dec, then 4–8 and 11, 12, **13** Jan | **Exact.** The twentieth is Wednesday 13 January 2025, and on Monday the 11th the count stands at eighteen of twenty, so *there are two days of it left* is exact. **This one was checked twice because it looked wrong, and it is right.** |
- **Wed 13 Jan 2025** — **clause 7 of the district supply agreement closes.** Twenty working days from the last of the nine. It is the same morning the Hall reopens. **This is the next ten days.**

## THE FIGURES, AND NONE OF THEM IS A SILLICK COUNT

`about 210` addresses on a round · `21` NCO entries in three weeks across four roads · `9` cleared on FLS-2, `9` on BWD-1, `3` on two other roads · `412` rows on the Museum's threshold group · `about 900` lines in the systems index · `247` entries with a location · `five` books that record a person and cannot say where they live · `411` + `about 90` items of dead post, of which `91` are the Museum's own and `9` are the annual acknowledgement · `700 × 400 mm`, `380 mm`, `120 mm` · `about 380` children and `about 200` adults at a school · `about 150` visitors a day · `about 90` people at a shut door · `2,904` district changes since 1 January · `about 140 years` of a rope line · `74.20` a bank balance · `4 miles` · `£1` · `35p` for a plastic wallet.

**THE FOUR COUNTS ARE UNTOUCHED. 94, 92 and 2 appear nowhere in Chapters 51–60 and are not referred to as figures. 61 does not appear as a figure. No total, no shortfall and no difference of any Sillick pay figure is stated anywhere, and no payroll subtraction is performed. Two count-lock collisions were found and removed in the drafting pass: a bank balance of 61.20 (now 74.20) and a house number, 92 Sillick Lane (now 80 Sillick Lane).** Elias Venn does take a fraction off a delivery sheet in Chapter 52 and it is dramatised as the horror of the chapter — the ruling of 26 November is that a difference is an adjudication, and he performs one, in his head, at a counter, about strangers, with nobody in the room who can rule against it. **That is the one arithmetic in the batch and it is the wrong one, and that is the point.**

## NEW DOCUMENTS, FORMATS, PEOPLE AND INSTITUTIONS

- **THE QUIET ACCORD ACKNOWLEDGMENT, QA/CR/1426, received 30 Dec 2024, signed S. TULLY, assessor.** *This schedule has no classification for activity observed outside a museum context. Your matter cannot be listed against a classification… No inspection slot is available before 6 February 2025. There is no emergency provision. I am not sorry. If you are about to display it, do not display it on the strength of a letter from this office.* **She is on the rota of 23 December 2024 and nobody in the building had ever spoken to her or heard her name said out loud.**
- **THE PLINTH 34 CARD, READ AT THE FOOT, 30 Dec 2024, 13:20.** The upper half is the description eighty people read in November. The lower half, which nobody has ever read, says: *Recovered on the closure of a textile works, Low Ward. Acq. 4471 / B. Municipal Works, unremarkable industrial object, retained for completeness of the Low Ward series.* **Three false claims and a name in the position where a name goes, on a public card, for about a hundred and twenty years, and the reason it survived is that the lie is at the bottom and the panel taped over the bottom belongs to a woman of fifty-four who is not Museum staff and has not come for it.** The tape has gone yellow. The panel is still on the case.
- **THE DOOR MAT CARD, cut 30 Dec 2024, 16:00, four inches by six.** Heading: **DOOR MAT.** Four lines of description ending *Function: a fixed-size surface for a person to stand on at a doorway while a person decides whether to open a door.* **Maker's line struck through, and under the line, in the finisher's own hand: *no name available*.** **The first public card in about a hundred and forty years in this building with a name in the field where a name goes, and the name is a sentence about the absence of a name.** No accession number, no collection line, no series, not in the schedule of collections, because there is no box for it.
- **THE DESCRIPTION, 22 Dec 2024, four minutes and eleven seconds, on Museum notepaper, with a steel rule, in the Hall of Former Faiths.** Coir on rubber, 700 × 400 mm, one wear band about 380 mm wide, 120 mm from the edge nearest the door, **the wear on the side that faces into the dwelling**. No inscription, no name, no number, no maker's mark. No accession number assigned and none proposed.
- **THE GOODS-OUT DOCKET** (Museum form, loading bay) with the foot line that stopped everything: *This docket records the movement of material from the loading bay of this Museum. It is not evidence of title. Material not owned by this Museum may be moved out only on the written authority of the Director, which must be attached.* And a counting clause: *A material count is made against this docket. The docket remains open on the desk of the post holding it until the item is on its pallet and the pallet is sealed.* **A door mat does not go on a pallet. The rule that was going to let it out is a rule about pallets.**
- **THE VISITORS' BOOK LINE, Thu 24 Dec 2024, 17:24 — the lawful door.** The mat leaves on no docket: *Entry in the visitors' book, ordinary column, and the counter's own line beneath it. No docket raised: no pallet, no material of this Museum, no authority required, the removal being by the person who presented the item and to the address from which he presented it.* Time in 10:12. Carried by **P. LOACH, services contract, grade field left empty at the counter's insistence.** Received by **FRANK DUNNING, 88 Sillick Lane, in his own name, in his own hand**, signature witnessed by M. PRIEST, who wrote *not a Museum object* in the reason column. **And the last line of that page reads *A. WARD, Dir., asked.*** — **the only document in the business in which a Director of this Museum has recorded that she was asked to do a thing rather than that she did it.**
- **THE SCHOOL FIRE RISK ASSESSMENT — RECORD OF ACTION.** *Item found: obstruction at exit, external door, ground floor, corridor side. Object: personal item brought into a classroom by a pupil. Removed from the entrance and stored.* School premises inventory **line 214**. **A school form, with no amendment box in it, and nobody had to find one.**
- **THE DISTRICT INBOUND CHANGE SCHEDULE** and its legend, printed 8 Jan 2025 from the supplied-file archive under **clause 7 of the supply agreement**: *No subscriber has ever queried a line.*
- **THE WALK-SURVEY INSTRUCTION, page one, the OCCUPANCY definition.** Quoted above. Free to any member of the public.
- **THE MAT — the practice, on a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet in a hall in Sillick Lane.** Named, timed, abandonable. *From three o'clock until you cannot read a number off a door.* **Anybody taking part may stop at any point, and stopping is not a failure, and nobody is to be asked afterwards why they stopped or why they did not go, and nobody is to be asked to say they have done it.** Whoever says a name writes it down and takes it home and puts it on a sheet on a table in the hall. **No institution has a form for it and five were asked and all five said no, correctly.**

## NEW PEOPLE, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN

- **Frank Dunning**, about sixty-five, **88 Sillick Lane**. Says *I've never had a letter. I've had a bin bag and a bank.* And: *nobody's asked me anything about it, and there's nobody to ask.* Names the practice. Says the floor: *the ones on the sheet we can get to. The ones off it we've not got a door.*
- **Roy Tilley**, about fifty, postman, **Fisher Lane and Sillick, FLS-2**, about two hundred and ten addresses, one man, the other long-term sick. **Signed Sunday 13 December: a name field doesn't go empty by accident — somebody's typing. The depot says the sheet is right, and the sheet's never not been right in my life.**
- **R.M.**, a counter clerk of about twenty-seven at the delivery office, **Nether Kiln Lane**. Refused him because he is not on the round, then wrote four lines of a change log on the back of a receipt in her own hand and said out loud in a room with a printer in it where it came from, and said: *you've been the fourth one this week and you're the first one who's read the code legend.* **First instrument anybody has handed anybody in this batch.**
- **Denise Hargreaves**, delivery office supervisor. **The depot's position, given correctly: the sheet's right.** Also: *It doesn't ask whether the house has gone. It asks whether we've got somebody to give it to. Those are not the same and that is the entire problem and it has been the entire problem since about 1994.*
- **Sonia Boulton**, about thirty-nine, letting agent, Sillick Lane, with a book. *That's not an employer, love. That's a place the money goes through.* And offered him the Thursday stair list, which is cash, which is not a job, and which is the thing you put on a reference while you get a job.
- **Marion Kelsh**, 54, school business manager, **St Anselm's (Hall Lane)**, nineteen years in post. Stopped it in nine minutes with a school form. *It's a good question. It's a better question than the one you came in with.* And: *Your museum has a card with a name on it, and my school has a book with a name on it, and we are the only two places in this county that could put a name on that mat. And the school isn't going to, because she's nine.* And the sentence the batch turns on: **a space is the most honest thing a card has ever had on it and it is the single most dangerous thing you can put on a public card in a museum, and both of those are true.**
- **Vera Pilling**, 81, of **6 Sillick Lane**. The first person to try it, and it cost her her chest. Said a dead man's name at a living man's door on 80 Sillick Lane and got *Who?* and worked out the correction herself: *you can't just say a name, you've to knock first.*
- **S. Tully**, about forty-five, **Registry assessor**, the first of a name on a rota to be said out loud in this building. See the acknowledgment above.
- **Mrs Toft**, about fifty, **records officer, district addressing service, Marsh Lane**, six years in post, keeper of a supplied-file archive nobody has ever asked her for. **A tenancy does not notify an addressing service and nobody has ever thought about why not**: the field at 80 Sillick Lane held a dead man's name from April to December while a living man lived there and had his post because a postman knocked.
- **Fenn**, surname only, label room, about four hundred cards, has never once been asked whether the name on one of them is right. *The reason a space is not allowed is that the last time this building had a space in a public room, somebody filled it in later, and I wasn't here, and I know because the foot of that card has got a body on it that doesn't exist, and that is what happens.*
- **Margaret Priest**, front desk, nineteen years, gave a man his own card back across a counter and said *because you came up this counter on Monday and asked me for it, and because I have been putting things in boxes on this counter for nineteen years and about four of them have ever come back out again.*
- **Ivo Pell**, 25, who did the dead letters on a Monday because a suspended man was in the room, and who wrote his own name and a number on the envelope for the first time since 8 December, and who discovered clause 7 by standing up, and who has to write a sentence about a person by Wednesday.
- **Anja Ward**, 53, who signed the second document of December and said the cost out loud **before** she signed, and had it minuted in that order, and recorded the day of the week on the minute, and said *we're going to be about ninety people at that door on the Friday the eighth and I have said the cost first on purpose because I am not going to be a woman who says it in January.*
- **Mara Iles**, 28, who cut no card and wrote a description and refused to make a card and left a blank and said *I have walked past the correct form for three days and it is a book with a ribbon in it and a woman at a counter*, and who on 7 January told Elias Venn, on the telephone, that nobody was ever going to give him a word, and it was not a kindness and not a punishment, **because a word that comes from a registrar in a building four miles away is not a word anybody in Sillick Lane can use at four o'clock in the afternoon when it is getting dark.**

## THE CONDITIONS, AND ALL FOUR ARE LIVE, AND ONE OF THEM FAILED TWICE

**Director's standing conditions of 15 October 2024, movement and access of Schedule 12 carried objects. Condition 3 suspends public access to the object between the posting of a proposed disposition and its execution, ten working days. Condition 4 (the Hall not to be opened to a school group, or to a party without a collections pass, while a Schedule 12 object in it is caged, moved, or out of its certified condition) remains satisfied: the object at plinth 34 has not been caged, moved or approached, and the Hall's closure of 31 December–13 January is a condition-three closure and not a condition-four closure. Annexe E remains in force and Annexe D is unaffected. The standing bar of 18 November — no school group in the Hall of Former Faiths at all — remains live and binds, and the Museum's own plinth card remains in place beneath the workers' panel, which is still taped on.**

**CONDITION 2 FAILED, TWICE, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT DOCUMENT IN THE BATCH.**

- **Wednesday 23 December 2024**, in a conservation office, over the mat from 88 Sillick Lane. A registrar drafted a movement docket, at about twenty past two in the afternoon, and it failed, and nobody in the building could say why for twenty-one hours, which is Thursday at about eleven. Condition 2 requires a countersignature by a person who is not an officer or employee of this Museum. The only person who can sign for a mat is the man it belongs to. **The moment a docket exists, condition 2 is engaged, and it says a Schedule 12 object is not to be removed except on a docket so countersigned — and it is not a Schedule 12 object, and the moment we make it one in order to get it out of the building, we have built ourselves a rule that says he cannot have it back.** A door mat cannot leave a public museum because there is no form for a person to write a person's name on when they are giving you a thing they own.
- **Tuesday 29 December 2024**, in a corridor at St Anselm's, over the classroom mat. The only person who is not an officer or employee of this Museum and who has anything to do with it is a girl of nine whose name is in biro on the underside of a corner of a mat, and the Museum cannot establish that the name belongs to a person at the address written beside it, and the school will not release a pupil's property to a person the school cannot identify.
- **The condition is the best condition anybody in this building has ever written. It is in force. It has not been displaced. It is not amended and is not to be amended by a later batch. Its failure is the finding, and the Director's sentence about it is on the page: *I have written a condition that can hold a door mat in a building for ever and I could not have told you that in October, and I have been a Director for nineteen years and I did not know, and I am not going to pretend I designed it and I am not going to pretend I did not.***

## THE LAWFUL DOOR, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE, AND IT IS A BOOK

**Six weeks of Volume 01 taught that every lawful door is a document. Volume 02's first ten days teach that the lawful door is a line in a book with a ribbon in it and a woman at a counter who has been there nineteen years, and that this is cheaper and worse and it works.**

| Door | Who opens it | What it costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The Museum's four conditions | a Director | they do not cover the case, and condition 2 has no subject |
| A goods-out docket | a pallet, a count, the Director's attached authority | a door mat does not go on a pallet |
| A movement docket, condition 2 | a person who is not an officer or employee | the owner's name is the thing that is gone |
| A Registry classification | a rota in Northglass, a slot on 6 February | the schedule has no classification for activity outside a museum context, and there is no emergency provision |
| **A line in the visitors' book** | **Margaret Priest, nineteen years** | **nothing. It cost eleven pounds to a removals man and it says *A. WARD, Dir., asked.* at the bottom.** |
| A school form | Marion Kelsh, a fire risk assessment | a child's name, and she will not |
| A sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet | nobody | it is not a form and it is not evidence and it dies with whoever keeps it |

## WHAT THE BATCH DID NOT DO, AND IT IS LISTED SO THAT THE NEXT BATCH DOES NOT MISTAKE ANY OF IT FOR AN OVERSIGHT

- **It did not describe the room at the east end of the Quiet Wing corridor.** Item 1 of the inquiry was done on Sunday 13 December 2024 at 09:50 by the Director with the Registrar, the metals conservator, and one person who is not Museum staff and is not a member of the public. **He found out about it by post on Tuesday 15 December and the first thing Volume 02 gave him was an invitation to a morning that had already happened and a door he could not have opened anyway, because the clause and the bar are incompatible by design.** The room has not been described and is not described here.
- **It did not name T. VENN or R.M.J.** The ability is not explained beyond a room he was in in the last week of October 2006 at fourteen and something in it that fused to him. The First Witness, the 312, the three locks and Tomas's counter-witness are all untouched.
- **It did not have the bell ask for anything.** One note at 10:54 on 27 November, one at 11:04 on 13 December, none since, and the crack in its shoulder is still open under the staple. It has lost a gallery and a fortnight because of a mat and nobody in the building knows whether that was a thing it did or a thing that happened.
- **It did not touch the bell at 11:52 on 11 November.** It is named in Chapter 60 as a thing he has never said to anybody, in his own hand, in a set of figures he cannot prove, and the word *or* in front of his own figures is still the honest part of the page. **He is not going to tell them, and there is nobody in this city he is allowed to tell.**
- **It did not touch the four counts, the shortfall total, the 1976 classification sheet, the unclaimed Loach line, the empty column in the 1974 accession register, the two women in a third row, Helen Venn's letter, the fifty-second line, the invoice bundle, or the school group's struck-through booking.**
- **It did not introduce a buyer, a market, a network, the Lantern Society, a new god, or a new antagonist.** The outline's *vanished housing block* is **not vanished**: the block is on a rounds sheet every Thursday and eleven households on it have answered their doors this month. The outline's *collector who has bought three objects whose owners were removed from the census* is **not touched at all**, and is recorded here as a deliberate omission, because Volume 02's pressure is local, civic and administrative and a buyer is not local. **Exactly one of the outline's four investigation focuses was touched in this batch, and it was the threshold, and it was one door mat in a hall in about four minutes, and it has no accession number and never will.**
- **It did not spend the cost curve.** **No second notebook entry of any kind, no memory cost, no time lost, no name gone.** The Chapter 8 two-hands entry is untouched. The fifty-seven minutes are untouched and were not escalated. The first major memory-displacement cost remains reserved for Volume 03. **The notebook he is writing in now is a new one, bought on the corner for thirty-five pence, with nine pages in it, and there are eleven lines on the first of them.**
- **It did not give him a stop word and did not let him acquire one.** He has none, never had one, did not learn one, and Mara Iles says on the page that she is the person who would have to and has decided not to. The word that the practice has is a *permission* and it belongs to a street and it is not his.
- **It did not make him a night guard, an ally, a romantic partner, or a man with standing anywhere.** His only lawful door is a witness notice under item 6, on notice, in company, not before eight, and his last appearance in a room was the twenty-second of December, and in a school corridor he is a man who stood by an office for an hour and was let stand there.

## THE NEW QUESTION, AND IT IS A QUESTION AND NOT AN ANSWER

> **What is a name field for.**
>
> Ivo Pell's answer, given twice, in a corridor and again at half past five on 30 December: **for finding him.** *It doesn't know that. It's a field, and you put a key in it, and the system's job is to go and get — and the system does not know a person from a postcode, it's a lookup, and it will go and get you a postcode just as fast. **It's not for the name.***
>
> The form's answer, which is the answer to a different question: **for the bill.**
>
> **And the question is not the one the form answers. The form answers what the field is. He is asking what it is for, and there are about nine hundred subscribers, and about two hundred and thirty postmen, and a Museum with a hundred and forty years of a rope line in it, and the furthest thing from the beginning of the chain is a public card about a bell, and nobody in the chain has ever been asked, and the reason nobody has ever been asked is that every one of them is a person doing a correct thing inside a department.**
>
> **So somebody has to be asked, in a room, with a document. That is the whole of what he has learned in a hundred and fifteen pages and a new notebook and thirty-five pence, and he cannot do it, and a man of twenty-five has to write a sentence about a person by Wednesday the thirteenth of January.**
