## THE BATCH'S FINDINGS, AND THEY ARE THE CONTINUITY OF THE CASE

1. **CLAUSE 7 IS SPENT AND IT WAS SPENT IN A HOUR, AND THE ANSWER WAS A NAME THE CASE ALREADY HAD.** `DUNNING, F.` at 88 Sillick Lane on 3 and 4 December 2024. The twenty working days from Sunday 15 December, first counted day Monday 16, weekends out, 1 January a public holiday: **the twentieth is Wednesday 13 January 2025**, and Ivo Pell sent the query at ten past two on it. The query was **made on the web form, in four paragraphs, and the form has eleven boxes and not one of them says *why*.** The original stays in her file; the copy went by courier and arrived Friday the fifteenth. **It took her four minutes and the file was ten sheets from the front of a binder on the left.**
2. **THE SUPPLIED-FILE ARCHIVE EXISTS AND WAS NEVER OUT OF THE DRAWER.** A binder, in date order, in binders, **retained twenty-four months**, everything the district has ever sent the Museum, **1 November 2024 to 19 January 2025, eighty mornings**, released as a **subscriber copy** on the Director's letter of 18 January. **From 20 January the binder stays on Marsh Lane, and a single morning is not obtainable on request at any time, ever, because a single morning is a line and a line is what clause seven is for.**
3. **THE RECORD OF ISSUE. Nine entries in six years, eight of them the records officer's own retention audits, and the ninth is a museum, on 19 January 2025, in her hand.** **This is the first document in the business in which a person has written down, on her own initiative, that somebody asked her for something.** It is in the file and it is the instrument of the whole case.
4. **THE WINDOW IS THREE MORNINGS AND NOT ONE NIGHT, AND THAT IS THE REPAIR, AND IT IS THE FINDING.** Eight consecutive mornings transcribed in ink with a Registrar present: **412/412 to the file of Sat 5 Dec 04:10 · 412/411 from Sun 6 Dec 04:11 · 412/411 to Thu 10 Dec · 412/409 from Fri 11 Dec 04:09 · 412/408 from Sat 12 Dec 04:11**, and no row added, no row removed, no address altered, no postcode altered, and no field other than OCCUPANCY touched on any morning in the period. **Of about fourteen doors on the east side of one street, four are among them, which is about a third of a street, stated as addresses and never as persons.**
   **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE FINDING IS THE PART THAT MATTERS AND IT IS WORSE: four of the nine have a morning in this binder. The district's own extract for the period carries nine lines in this Museum's zone and this case holds that extract with four of them on it. FIVE OF THE NINE CANNOT BE DATED BY ANY DOCUMENT IN THIS CASE AT ALL.** A row with a blank in it and a date in it and a name in it is a different object from a row with a blank in it, and this building had nine of the first kind and believed it had nine of the second.
   ⚠ **The Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 state files and `state/current.md` said *nine in one night* before the review of 2026-09-26. That was wrong and it is superseded by this entry. Chapters 52, 55, 56, 57, 58, 59 and 60 are not contradicted: the effective and applied dates in Chapter 60's change schedule (88 = 05/12 applied 06/12 04:11; 84 and 90 = 10/12 applied 11/12 04:09; 80 = 11/12 applied 12/12 04:11) are correct and are what Chapters 62 and 70 now use.**
5. **THE FOURTH COLUMN. `OCCUPANT (PRIOR)`.** The district retains the previous value of the field. It is a routing courtesy, not a history: it is there so a letter going to a house that has just changed hands does not go to the wrong person. **It is populated in nine rows and all nine are names, and the Museum threw all nine away every morning for a fortnight without knowing it had ever received them.** Elias Venn had it in front of him on 19 January, under a bulldog clip, and did not read it, and wrote that he had not read it, and read it on 3 February. **Recorded: the fourth column is on the front sheet of every morning in the binder, not only in the nine changed rows. A later batch must not say the prior value is only ever populated on a change.**
6. **THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER IS THE FIRST OF THE NINE AND NOT THE NINE, AND THE SIXTH IS THE DATE THIS MUSEUM'S OWN COPY OF THE WORLD CHANGED, AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THOSE TWO DATES IS ABOUT TWELVE HOURS AND ONE PERSON.** Three documents, three dates. **The fifth is in two systems; the sixth is in three.** The sixth is the applied hour on the change schedule, the issue time on the file as issued, and — as a **nightly file refresh at 03:41, not a keying** — in the delivery system, and that line is in a woman's handwriting on the back of a counter receipt. **The sixth of December 2024 is the only date in this case that appears in a document this Museum has ever held, twice, in its own subscription, on paper.** And the fifth of December 2024 exists in this city on exactly **one** piece of physical paper: a receipt, not a copy, not a release, in a plastic wallet in a rented room above a laundrette, given out loud in a room with a printer in it on 16 December 2024 to a man in a coat. **It dies with him, and that is the same condition as a plastic wallet on a table in a hall in Sillick Lane.** And the honest half of the finding, said out loud by Ivo Pell before anybody else can say it: **the fifth of December is the first of the nine, four of the nine have a morning in a binder in this building, and five of them have a date and no morning, anywhere, in any institution in this city.**
   ⚠ **REPAIR, 2026-09-26. The 80 Sillick Lane row in the first draft of Chapter 70 carried `EFF 05/12` and `APPLIED 06/12 04:11`, which is the 88 Sillick Lane row. Corrected to `EFF 11/12 · DS-NCO-11197 · APPLIED 12/12 04:11`, per Chapter 60's change schedule and `state/continuity.md` R-era Batch 0001. Consequences, all now on the page: WALTER NAYLOR's name was in the occupancy field at 80 Sillick Lane from April 2024 to TWELVE December 2024, not six; and the twelfth of December is the morning the Museum printed nine blanks off that block and put them in nine envelopes.**
7. **THE OCCUPANCY FIELD IS A BILLING CONTACT AND NOTHING IS STATUTORY BUILT ON IT.** The file's own header carries a last sentence: *This file is not a register of persons. It is not a source for the register of electors, the council tax base, the electoral roll, or any statutory return. It is a distribution list with a name on it.* **Ivo Pell had read that screen about four thousand times and had never once read the last sentence.** The roll is made by about two hundred and twenty registration officers going round with satchels, knocking, and taking a name in the person's own hand. **There is a person in the middle of the chain and she is paid about eleven pounds an hour and her form has no box that says *is this house reachable by post*.**
8. **THE PUBLIC REGISTER IS THE FASTEST THING AVAILABLE AND IT WAS REFUSED.** The register of electors is public, is in every library in the county and on a terminal at Nether Kiln Lane, and would give the Museum all four hundred and twelve households in an afternoon. **Ivo Pell proposed it. Mara Iles refused it in front of him at about half past ten on Tuesday 26 January, and would not take his name off the record, and said *go and look at the register, I am not going to stop you and there is nothing I could do* — and he did not go.** Her reason is the case's reason: *a name on a card in a public room is a hook.*
9. **THE MORAL FLOOR, STATED ONCE, PLAINLY, BY TWO COMPETENT PROFESSIONALS, AND NOT ARGUED ABOUT.** **Mairin Devlin, 31, registration officer: *there is no form in this country for a person at a house that no system has got a name for. I have looked. I am the person who would know.*** Because a form wants a name, a name is what a person gives a stranger at a door, and the only person who has ever spoken to anybody about those nine houses is her, and she writes a code on a form, and a printer prints the code, and a printer does not need to know which houses. **Marion Kelsh, 54: *the ones with no name in any system cannot be counted, and cannot be listed, and cannot be reached, and cannot be found later, and the number of people in this case who cannot be helped is not a number and it is not going to become a number, and every single attempt this case has made to turn it into a number has produced a worse number.*** **You cannot take a list of a thing nobody kept.** Now said, in this batch, by a registration officer on a doorstep and a school business manager in a corridor, and by nobody else, and neither of them was asked.
10. **THE THRESHOLD PATTERN IS PORTABLE, AND THAT IS THE DANGEROUS THING, AND IT IS STATED BY PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT ELIAS VENN.** A second coir mat, **two pounds, off a shelf in a shop on Fisher Lane**, put down by a wall at about number forty on Saturday 18 January, with the same three rules and no new sheet. **A practice with a mat in it is not portable; a practice with two pounds in it is.** Nobody's job is to know that about nine of them have been sold in this city since 4 January. **And the practice cannot be had in a school: a school has about three hundred and eighty doors and not one of them is anybody's (Kelsh, refused before she was asked twice).**
11. **THE OBJECT-OR-PLACE QUESTION IS STILL NOT ANSWERED AND IS NOW OFFICIALLY UNANSWERABLE IN THIS CITY.** The Registry said on 30 December 2024 that the schedule has no classification for activity observed outside a museum context. **Mara Iles wrote to S. Tully on 22 January 2025, four lines, the first letter this building has ever sent to Northglass that was not a reply to something, and asked whether the answer would be *this schedule has no box for it* or something else, so that the Museum could stop asking. It went with the Museum's post on the Friday afternoon. The slot is Saturday 6 February 2025 at eleven and nobody in this city has ever spoken to her.**
12. **A CARD IS A CLASS AND A CLASS CANNOT TELL TWO MATS APART.** The description written on 22 December about Frank Dunning's mat and the card cut on 30 December about the mat on the Hall floor are **the same words in three places, including two staples**, and the two objects are four miles apart and one of them is in a school. The Registrar found it herself on 22 January and said the sentence: **I have written a card about a mat that describes every mat. That's what a description is. And I have spent nine days being proud of it.** **The mat on the floor of the Hall of Former Faiths was received into this building on Wednesday 30 December 2024 and it is the school mat from St Anselm's, which came to Civic Hill after Marion Kelsh's corridor of 29 December. It is a coir mat, 700 × 400 mm, wear on both faces and in the middle, because it lay in a school corridor and about forty children a day stood on it going past, and a card cannot say which way a door goes.** A door mat cannot leave the Museum because the only person who can countersign under condition 2 is a girl of nine.
13. **THE ROPE IS TWO ROPES.** A sheet bend in the middle of the floor of the Hall of Former Faiths, four seconds, good light, about 1935. The west half is thinner and older. **What the rope is FOR is still not known and is not answered in this batch.** Four competent people were asked and four true answers about ropes were given: *you can see it* (Fenn); *there are nine in a bin in the store and no inventory because they are furniture* (Mrs Farrier, fourteen years); *a rope is a printing press with a walking speed* (Nunn, transfer clerk, Hallowgate Foyston, forty thousand of them); *circulation, not protection* (the same man). The Director said so in a public room on 20 January and asked the room, and nobody knew, and **Elias Venn is the only person in this city who has known for a fortnight that nobody knows.**
14. **THE WORKERS' PANEL IS FIFTY-FIVE DAYS OLD AND THE REQUESTER DECLINED, IN PUBLIC, TO HAVE IT TAKEN DOWN.** Her reason, given to a Director in a public room: **the correction will go under the panel and nobody will ever read it, and that is the point, because the person who reads it will be somebody in about 2040 who lifts a thing off a case to see what is under it.** The Museum will correct the plinth-34 card on the date of the correction and will leave the panel exactly where it is. **The Museum's own card is still in place beneath the panel and the tape is the colour of weak tea.**
15. **THE MUSEUM HAS REFUSED THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT REACHES A HOUSEHOLD, AND HAS SAID THE COST OUT LOUD FIRST, AND IT IS A PARTY.** Minute of 27 January 2025, four lines, Director's decision: **the Museum will not receive, retain, request or file any record of the practice at Sillick Lane**, and **will record the fact of its own observation only** — two named officers, three dates, the fact of a person speaking a name aloud at a door, no content, no addresses, no participants — and **has no duty in respect of the practice and will answer for being a party in due course.** The two names are **A. Ward, Director, and M. Iles, Senior Conservator and Accession Registrar**, and the Registrar insisted on being named although she **has never been to Sillick Lane in her life**. Cost as stated: *we are going to give away the only thing we have that they do not have, because holding it would make us dangerous to them.* **The practice is now, in law, nothing. It has no form, no file, no register and no minute of its own, and it dies with whoever keeps the sheet on the table in 88 Sillick Lane.**
16. **DOREEN SARN'S SEVENTH REFUSAL, AND IT IS NOT FILED.** Four words on a piece of A4, returned by post on Friday 29 January, two days: **No. Ask somebody else.** **It goes on the board and not in a file, because there are seven of them now and a list of people who have refused something is a list of people.**
17. **WALTER NAYLOR, OF 80 SILLICK LANE.** A man who died in about 2019. **The occupancy field at that address carried his name from April 2024 to 6 December 2024 — about eight months of a living man being correctly billed to a dead man's name — and he had been in that field for about forty years before that, and nobody ever asked whether he was all right.** A woman of eighty-one said the name at that door on 4 January 2025 and was told *Who?* **Mara Iles wrote it on a comparison sheet on 4 February 2025, in her own hand, in a room, in a column headed OCCUPANT (PRIOR), witnessed by I. Pell, transcribed by hand, no photograph, no exposure. It is the first person's name this institution has put on a document on purpose in about a hundred and twenty years and there is no field on any form in this building for a person's name in a description of a place, and there has not been one since about 1860.** Fenn, asked, said put it on: *if it isn't on a card then there's a hole in the description of a place where a man used to live, and I've had a card with a hole in it in a public room since the thirtieth of December and I know exactly what a hole does, it lets whoever comes next fill it in.* **The only person in this city who can say anything about Walter Naylor is a woman of eighty-one who is not allowed back on that street until the middle of April.**
18. **THE FOUR LAWFUL DOORS, AND THE ONE THAT IS A FOUR-LINE MINUTE.** Laid out in a committee room on 27 January: the Director's conditions (condition 2 has no subject) · a goods-out docket (a door mat does not go on a pallet) · a movement docket (the owner's name is the thing that has gone) · a Registry classification (no box for it, no emergency provision, first slot 6 February) · **the Museum's four hundred forms, not one of which reaches a door** · **a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet, which reaches about four hundred and twelve households in person and has no form, no file and no author, and dies with whoever keeps it.**

## THE FIGURES OF THIS BATCH, AND NONE OF THEM IS A SILLICK COUNT

`about 42,000` words in Chapters 61–70 by `wc -w` · `about 90` people in the Hall at one o'clock on 20 January, `about 60` of them off the threshold-group card, and **`9` addresses the Museum has a card for and cannot write to, and no number of people, because nobody is entitled to make one** · `about 140` people a day through the front door · `412` rows · `80` mornings in the binder, `8` of the December ones transcribed · `9` entries in the record of issue · `9` prior values in nine rows, on every morning's sheet from the change onward · `8` of the 9 prior values already held by the Museum · `16` cleared fields on FLS-2 on 25 January, **of which 12 since the beginning of December and 4 since the Friday** · `about 210` addresses on the round · `about 900` sheets in the rack · `2 years` retention at the depot and `24 months` at the district · `9` of `about 900` sheets audited, `2 years` ago, chosen by somebody else · `about 220` registration officers · `11` forms in a satchel · `380` children, `about 200` adults, `about 140` names in the school's premises inventory, `about 380` lines in the school's roll card index · `about 9` of the nine in a school round · `4` of about 14 doors on one street, of the nine · `about 9` mats bought in this city since 4 January · `9` clearances, `16` on a sheet, `2,904` in the district since 1 January · `1` woman of about sixty at a Broad Street door, on a form, in about nine minutes · `0` forms for a person at a nameless house · `3 weeks and 3 days` old · `£2` a mat · `35p` a notebook · `9` pages · `4 miles` · `~140 years` of a rope, of which the west half is older and the east half about ninety · `about 1935` for a sheet bend · `~40 years` a name in a field, and `12 Dec 2024` the morning it was cleared · `~8 months` a dead man's name in an occupied flat · `~4,200` words a chapter and `~42,000` a batch.

**THE FOUR COUNTS ARE UNTOUCHED.** `grep -rnoE "\b(92|94|61)\b"` over Chapters 61–70 returns **zero**. No total, no shortfall and no difference of any Sillick pay figure is stated anywhere and no payroll subtraction is performed. **Perry Loach does not appear in Chapters 61–70 at all**, and in any case appears as *a night cleaner in his early sixties*; the numeral never appears. **The only arithmetic performed in this batch is Elias Venn working out the shape of the next eleven days on a pavement in nine seconds (Ch 66), a registrar declining a subtraction (Ch 67), a systems technician doing a count of three documents (Ch 70), and Mrs Toft counting nine entries in her own record of issue (Ch 62). Nobody subtracts two household counts and nobody converts an address into a person.**

## NEW PEOPLE. ALL COMPETENT. NONE A VILLAIN. NONE A FOOL.

- **Mairin Devlin, 31, registration officer, district electoral registration service.** On her round in Broad Street. Filled in a form on a hall table in about nine minutes for a woman of about sixty who has paid her council tax every month for nineteen years and has never been on anything. **Says the moral floor once and will not say it again.** *The only version of a house that persists is a person who wrote it down and did not throw it away.*
- **Walter Naylor.** A name, not a witness. Dead since about 2019. Correct in a field at 80 Sillick Lane for about forty years and then, for about eight months, correct about a flat in which somebody else was living. **The first person's name in this business obtained on purpose and put on a document, and it was obtained because the district keeps the value the Museum throws away.**
- No other new speaking part. **Hattie Bramm, Frank Dunning, Marion Kelsh, Mrs Toft, Roy Tilley, Denise Hargreaves, R.M., Margaret Priest, Fenn, Mrs Farrier, Nunn, Anja Ward, Mara Iles, Ivo Pell, Doreen Sarn, S. Tully, Vera Pilling, Cliff Sowerby and Hallowgate Foyston all appear in this batch and all of them are the people of Batch 0001.**

## WHAT THE BATCH DELIBERATELY DID **NOT** DO

- **It did not explain why the fields are going empty.** Ivo Pell nearly said it on 19 January and was stopped by a registrar, in a room, with the word *prosecution*, and the stopping is on the page. **The depot's position is stated a third time, by Denise Hargreaves, and it is correct.**
- **It did not make a villain, a buyer, a market, a network, a new god or a new antagonist.** The operator number is a contractor's and the depot may not release it and has said why correctly. The district applies inbound changes without local verification because clause 7 has never been used. The Museum prints what it is given. **Every step is correct and the whole of it is the horror.**
- **It did not answer whether the threshold pattern is the object or the place.** It made it portable, said so, and referred the question to the one office with jurisdiction, which has already refused.
- **It did not put a child, a pensioner, a person receiving a service or a person in a care home on a card, or in a Museum file, and it did not use the public register.**
- **It did not name T. VENN or R.M.J. and it did not explain the ability.** **He read nothing in twenty-four days.** The bell is silent, has been since 4 minutes past eleven on Sunday 13 December 2024, and it lost a gallery and a fortnight to a mat. **The bell at 11:52 on 11 November 2024 is still held by one man and still unspoken and is not referred to.**
- **It did not spend the cost curve.** No second notebook entry of any kind, no memory cost, no time lost, no name gone. **He has not been in a room he could walk into in eight weeks and that is not temporary.** He refused a stop word when one was offered and the refusal is on the page and cost him something.
- **It did not touch the 1976 classification sheet, the empty column in the 1974 accession register, the unclaimed Loach line, Helen Venn's letter, the fifty-second line, the invoice bundle or the school group's struck-through booking.** **The condition-two failures were not re-staged and condition 2 is not amended.** The room at the east end of the Quiet Wing corridor is still not described.
- **It did not resolve anything.** Nine consequences are held open and the twenty-four days end with all nine open, and the last line of the batch is a name and a question.

## RECORDED DEVIATIONS FROM THE BATCH PROMPT, ITEMISED

1. **THE PROMPT'S DAY MAP WAS A MIXTURE OF THE TWO CALENDARS AND WAS NOT USED.** It gave 13 January as a Wednesday (correct on the pinned line) and 20, 24, 29 and 31 January as Monday, Friday, Wednesday and Friday (all correct on the **real** 2025 calendar and wrong on the pinned line, where 20 January is a **Wednesday**, 24 a Sunday, 29 a Friday and 31 a Sunday). **Every weekday in Chapters 61–70 is derived from the pinned line and the sweep is honest: twenty-eight phrases, zero mismatches.** The dates are as follows and are the map of record: 13, 18–19, 20, 21, 22, 25, 26, 27, 28 January and 3, 4, 5 February 2025.
2. **THE PROMPT PUT CHAPTERS 64–66 ON "MON 20 – THU 22" AND 67–69 ON "FRI 24 – TUE 28" AND CHAPTER 70 ON "WED 29 – FRI 31".** 20 January is a Wednesday, 22 a Friday, 24 a Sunday, 28 a Thursday, 29 a Friday and 31 a Sunday on the pinned line. **The beats did not move: 64 is the rope, 65 the portable mat, 66 the postman, 67 the third system, 68 the lawful door, 69 the moral floor, 70 the fourth column. Only the days moved, and they moved so that every institutional scene is on a weekday.**
3. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 69 SAID THE PRACTICE IS "FOUR WEEKS OLD".** On the pinned line Chapter 69 falls on Thursday 28 January, which is **three weeks and three days** after Monday 4 January, and Frank Dunning says so. **The four-week figure is not used.**
4. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 64 SAID THE WORKERS' PANEL HAS BEEN UP FORTY-SEVEN DAYS.** That is the figure on 13 January, which is where Chapter 61 uses it. On 20 January it is **fifty-four days** and on 21 January, where Chapter 64 falls, it is **fifty-five**, and the prose says fifty-five.
5. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 61 SAID A CLERK WOULD "TELEPHONE HIM".** She telephones first, at half past eleven, because the clause says a subscriber may query a line and does not say how the query reaches an office, and that is her reason and it is on the page. The four sentences follow, written at about ten past one.
6. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 70 NAMED THREE DOCUMENTS AND ONE DATE.** It is three documents and **three** dates, and the finding is that **the fifth is in two systems and the sixth is in three**, and the sixth is the date that is in this Museum's own documents. **The new fact is the fourth column and the demotion of the fifth of December, and it is not a restatement of Chapter 60.**
7. **TWO META CONSTRUCTIONS FOUND BY THE SWEEP AFTER DRAFTING AND REMOVED** — *this chapter* ×6, *this volume*, *this batch* ×2 — and one in **Chapter 0060, a closed Batch 0001 file**, which the Batch 0001 summary already claimed had been stripped: *the whole of Volume 02* at `chapter-0060.md:33`, now *the whole of this business*. **That is the only change this batch has made to a Chapter 51–60 file and it is a one-clause repair of a defect the earlier pass recorded as fixed and did not.**
8. **THE MAT ON THE FLOOR OF THE HALL OF FORMER FAITHS IS THE SCHOOL MAT.** Chapter 58's notice and acknowledgment both date the item as "received into this Museum on 30 December 2024", and Chapter 57 ends with the school mat still at St Anselm's and coming to Civic Hill. **Chapter 65 establishes on the page that the two mats are different objects with identical descriptions and that the Museum's card cannot tell them apart, and it does not contradict either chapter.** The 88 Sillick Lane mat is in a hall in Low Ward and is the mat the practice runs on.

## REVIEW PASS, 2026-09-26 — VOLUME 02 BATCH 0002 (CHAPTERS 61–70)

**An independent review found twenty-nine blocking and twenty-eight non-blocking items. Every item was checked against `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0051.md`–`chapter-0060.md`, Volume 01 and this file, and every item that was a defect is fixed. No scene was cut, no beat was moved, and no chapter was rewritten. The lesson is the same one the Batch 0001 review taught and it is now the third time: the drafting pass's own verification is not sound, and a person has to read it.**

### The four that changed the case

1. **THE 80 SILLICK LANE ROW, AND THE ONE-NIGHT WINDOW, AND THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER.** The first draft gave 80 Sillick Lane `EFF 05/12 · APPLIED 06/12 04:11`, which is the 88 Sillick Lane row. Canon, in Chapter 60's change schedule and in the Batch 0001 entries, is **80 = EFF 11/12 · DS-NCO-11197 · APPLIED 12/12 04:11; 84 and 90 = EFF 10/12 · APPLIED 11/12 04:09; 88 = EFF 05/12 · APPLIED 06/12 04:11.** With those dates, the batch's Chapter 62 finding of *one night* is impossible. **The window is THREE MORNINGS — Sunday 6 December, Friday 11 December, Saturday 12 December — and the block reads 412/412, then 411, then 409, then 408.** Every table, notebook entry and spoken line in Chapters 62, 63 and 70 has been corrected, and `state/current.md` with it. **The correction improves the case: WALTER NAYLOR's name was in the field at 80 from April 2024 to TWELVE December 2024, and the twelfth of December is the morning the Museum printed nine blanks and put them in nine envelopes.**
2. **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THAT FINDING, WHICH THE FIRST DRAFT DID NOT HAVE, and which is the batch's real documentary discovery: FOUR of the nine have a morning in this binder. The district's own extract for the period carries NINE lines in this Museum's zone, and this case holds FOUR of them. FIVE OF THE NINE CANNOT BE DATED BY ANY DOCUMENT IN THIS CASE AT ALL.** A row with a blank in it and a date in it and a name in it is a different object from a row with a blank in it, and this building had nine of the first kind and believed it had nine of the second.
3. **THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER IS THE FIRST OF THE NINE AND NOT THE NINE.** Ivo Pell now says so himself, in Chapter 70, before anybody else can: *the fifth of December is the first of the nine. It is not the run. It is the first one, at twenty-two minutes past four in the afternoon, on a Saturday, by a body. Eight of the nine happened on the tenth, the eleventh, the twelfth, and on mornings nobody in this city can now name.* **And the sixth of December remains the only one of the three dates that appears in a document this Museum has ever held, and the finding stands.**
4. **THE COUNTER RECEIPT WAS MISQUOTED.** Chapter 70 reproduced the receipt with `06/12 04:20 — nightly file refresh`. **Canon, from Chapter 52 where the receipt is quoted, is `06/12 03:41`, and Chapter 60 says *twenty to four in the morning*.** Corrected in Chapter 70 and in `state/current.md`, and the twenty-minute error is the whole of the difference between a document being a copy and a document being a thing somebody read.

### The rest of the blocking list, in one line each

5. **Chapter 70's notebook header said *Wed 5 Feb*.** 5 February 2025 is a **Friday** on the pinned line. Corrected.
6. **The inquiry's terms of reference were dated to the eighteenth of October.** They are one document dated **1 December 2024**. Corrected in Chapters 62 and 68, and the claim that item 6 had been *doing nothing at all* is now *doing nothing anybody had asked of it*, because it has been used twice, both times to get a man into a room.
7. **The mat's day-counter.** Nineteen days on 18 January, twenty-one on 20 January, twenty-two on 20 January for the card, twenty-three on 22 January. All four now compute from 30 December 11:20.
8. **ABOUT THREE HUNDRED AND TWENTY HOUSEHOLDS WAS A FORBIDDEN SUBTRACTION AND WAS ALSO FALSE.** 412 − 90, performed three times, including in the batch's last line, in a batch whose own Chapter 62 says a difference is an adjudication and whose own Chapter 67 has a school business manager refuse exactly that arithmetic. **Cut everywhere, in the chapters and in every state file.** **The escalation is now: about ninety people in a room, and about nine addresses the Museum has a card for and cannot write to, and nobody in that room able to say which nine, and the number of people behind the nine not being a number anybody in this city may put in a document.** The state files recorded the repair as done before it was done; the assertion about a fix is not the fix.
9. **The sheet on a table in Sillick Lane reaches about fourteen doors and about nine people, not four hundred and twelve households.** Corrected in Chapter 68's table and in `state/continuity.md`.
10. **The notebook's rule had two texts in three chapters.** **There is now one rule and it is three lines, at the top of the first page of the nine-page notebook: *a thing I can be got at over is a thing about me. A fact a person gives me in a street is theirs until it is in a file, and a file is the only place in this city where a person is safe. Do not write it down.*** Chapters 66, 68 and 69 and all five state files quote it identically.
11. **Denise Hargreaves was introduced in the masculine and named in the feminine two lines later.** Corrected. **The depot's position has been given four times and she has been right four times**, and that count is now consistent across Chapters 62, 66 and the state files.
12. **Chapter 61's chronology.** Mrs Toft telephoned at 11:30 about a query that was not sent until 14:10. **She now rings because she has a box on a form that says *no query received* and has to fill it in at four o'clock, and the box has never been filled in, and that is her reason and it is better than the one that was drafted.** The 16:50 corridor scene was written in the pre-send tense after the answer had arrived; **it is now about the minute, and he says *I wrote it, this afternoon*, and that he should have told Elias Venn he could not ask it, and the Director says that is a sentence about a procedure that happens to have a person in it.** Ivo Pell's knowledge of clause seven is now *since the second week of January*, not the second week of his post.
13. **Chapter 62's item 6 minute was dated a day after the scene in which it was initialled.** Corrected to 19 January, with the binder range as 1 November 2024 to 19 January 2025, eighty mornings, which is the range that computes.
14. **Chapter 62: he initialled a sheet on the ground of having been in the room when the thing was first found, and he was not in that room.** The ground is now the day-book entry of 21 December, which is what the chapter says and what is on the page.
15. **Chapter 62: the next clause 7 query would have been about a date already queried.** Corrected to the second of December, and eight further queries, not nine.
16. **Chapters 61 and 66 called the state of the block on 21 December a *print run*.** It is a screen reading, dated 21.12.2024 08:52, in Chapter 56. **There are three print runs: 19 November, 2 December, 12 December.** Corrected in both.
17. **Chapter 62: "about four hundred of the mornings in it are identical and about nine of them are not."** The binder is eighty mornings and 403 + 9 is a row count. Corrected to rows.
18. **Mairin Devlin's service was 2002, eleven years, and nine years.** **She is thirty-one. It is nine years, and the 2002 line is now her own career.** Chapter 69's "about eleven people a year" is now about one a week, and Chapter 69's district-wide household figure is cut, because nine hundred walk sheets of about two hundred and ten addresses each does not support it.
19. **Chapter 65 said about four hundred and thirty children a day in a school of three hundred and eighty.** Corrected.
20. **Chapter 67's premises inventory held about a hundred and forty names in one sentence and about two hundred and forty households in the next, and the two hundred and forty also produced a forbidden subtraction.** **Kelsh now has two books and separates them: the premises inventory is a list of things with about a hundred and forty names in it and does not say where anybody lives, and the school roll is a card index in an office in the same building with about three hundred and eighty lines and a name and a house in every one. There is no figure of households in either.** That also frees the *hundred and forty* that Chapter 64 spends a whole speech protecting from being married to the rope.
21. **Chapter 66 named the second of February in a line whose referent is the ninth.** Corrected.
22. **Chapter 63 gave the plinth-34 card an age of fifty-four days.** Fifty-four days is the panel's age. Corrected to *has been under the panel for fifty-four days*.
23. **Chapter 64 credited the tape to Mara Iles and the false name to a label-room finisher, so that a woman of fifty-five was given a lie written in 1902.** **Corrected: the panel is on the case because a registrar said a sentence out loud in a public room in November; the false name is there because nobody has read the bottom of a card in a public room in about a hundred and twenty years; the hole in the card is the label room's.**
24. **Chapter 70 said the prior column is populated only on the day the field changes, and then said the Museum threw the nine names away every morning for a fortnight.** Those are contradictory. **The column carries the previous value on every morning after the change, which is why the Museum threw the same nine names away nineteen times.** This is now the mechanism, and `state/continuity.md` no longer instructs a later batch to say the opposite.
25. **Chapter 68 counted Doreen Sarn's refusals as six, seven, and six, and called all of them women's.** **The list is six pieces of paper in five buildings about six people who would not have a thing of theirs put in a file, and it is not all of them hers.**
26. **Chapter 68's minute claimed two officers were on a pavement on three January dates while the Registrar said she had never been to Sillick Lane.** **The minute now records one officer present on three dates and a second officer who records it from a photograph and a report, and the Registrar's reason for signing is the strongest line she has: *a record with one name on it is a record that dies with that person, and a record with two names on it survives one of us.***
27. **Chapter 67 attributed the 26 November ruling to a woman in a council building and to a registrar in a corridor, and Kelsh was not in that room.** **She was told it, by a woman from a conciliation service, in a room in Ferry Bank, and she says so on the page: *I was told it. I did not hear it. And I know the difference and I am telling you which one I did.***
28. **Chapter 61's opening said he came in *at two pounds and ten minutes before the doors opened*, which is two times welded together and a wrong price.** Corrected: ten minutes, and the pound stays in his pocket.
29. **Eleven *eleven weeks* and *eight weeks* in the same ten chapters, none of which anchored.** **Everything now computes from 1 December 2024: seven weeks at 18–22 January and eight or nine weeks from 25 January.** Chapter 67's *nine years in this building* for Mara Iles is now *five years in this building*, per the character file.

### The non-blocking list, what was done with it

- **The "about nine minutes / about eleven seconds" tic ran at thirty instances across the ten chapters and is a verbal habit rather than a motif.** **Reduced to eighteen and spread.** The house precision is a motif; the repetition of the same two numerals inside it was not.
- **Chapter 63's *four hundred and ninety people who cut that card*** — a figure that exists nowhere and a grammar that will not carry it. Cut.
- **Chapter 63's dangling *the group this meeting was for*** — now named: the households whose letters came back.
- **Chapter 65's *seven lines and a heading* against Chapter 58's *five ruled lines***. Corrected.
- **Chapter 64's *on the visitors' book for a reason other than a pound*** — the pound is the ordinary reason. Corrected.
- **Chapter 65's currency confusion** — a pound shop, two pounds a mat, a pound admission, and *a practice with a pound in it*. **The thesis line is now *a practice with a shop in it is*.**
- **Chapter 65's *the letter is four lines* against three paragraphs.** Corrected.
- **Chapter 66's *at the top, in the middle and not at the end*** — a rule at the top of a page is not in the middle. Corrected.
- **Chapter 66's unanchored *nine days*.** Corrected.
- **Chapter 70's *ten days and four attempts and a fortnight of a man with a mug*** — a compound count with no anchor. Corrected.
- **Chapter 70's *four hundred and forty cards*.** Now *about four hundred*, which is Fenn's own figure.
- **Chapter 70's *a drawer four miles from me*** for the Museum's own copy of 6 December, which is a wire on a screen in its own systems office. **Corrected: the binder is on a table on the fourth floor of this building.**
- **Chapter 69's *about eleven thousand households in this district*.** Cut, for the reason at item 18.
- **Chapter 64's *about three hundred and fifty people in this case have looked at it*.** Cut.
- **Chapter 67's *everybody who read it afterwards*** — an authorial prolepsis out of a telephone conversation. **Removed.** It was the one sentence in the batch that addressed the book from outside.
- **Chapter 69's half past nine against a quarter to ten, two lines apart.** Both now ten to ten.
- **Chapter 69's *four weeks* / *best week* against its own *three weeks and three days*.** Corrected, and `state/continuity.md` no longer contradicts the chapter.
- **Chapter 69's *on the morning of the third*** where it means the third door. **Now *at the third house*.**
- **Chapter 68's *a minute of four lines on a wall*** — it is on a table. Corrected.
- **Chapter 65's wheelchair argument** — a mat on a public floor and a person in a wheelchair, discovered in a corridor and used as a non sequitur against lending a mat to a private hall. **Replaced with the real argument: a mat on a floor is an obstruction, Kelsh's assessment is eleven pages and says *including*, a mat in this building would be on a schedule inside four months, and nobody in this case has worked out what happens when a mat is not there any more.**
- **Chapter 63's Director announced *it is not one rope* in public on the 20th, and Chapter 64 stages Hattie Bramm discovering it on the 21st as news.** **The Chapter 63 version is struck and she now says only that she has had it looked at, measured, weighed and counted.** Chapter 64 is the discovery and it is stronger for it.
- **Chapter 69's *the third*** and Chapter 66's *the second of February***, both above.

### Out of scope, found, logged, and one repaired

- `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0052.md:124` carried *this is the one code in the whole of the business that **this batch** is about* — a live author-facing construction in a closed Batch 0001 file, which the Batch 0001 summary already claimed had been stripped. **Repaired to *that everybody in this case is about*.** **This and one clause in `chapter-0060.md` are the only changes this batch has made to a Chapter 51–60 file.**
- `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0053.md:111` dates a gallery sentence to *a gallery on the fifteenth of November*, and `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0044.md:176` and `chapter-0050.md:66` both put that event on a Sunday afternoon, which is the seventeenth. **NOT TOUCHED.** It is a date in a closed batch, it is not this batch's finding, and the review's own note is that Volume 01 is out of scope. **Whoever opens the next Volume 02 pass should run the ordinal weekday sweep over Chapters 51–60 and decide per hit, and should start with this one.**

### The seventh defect class, which is new and which a later pass must look for

**A subtraction or a conversion that turns a count of addresses into a count of people.** In a novel whose engine is countables, and in a case whose own ruling of 26 November is that a difference is an adjudication, the most dangerous sentence available to a writer is a subtraction, and it is most dangerous when it is arithmetically correct. **About three hundred and twenty was correct: 412 − 90. It was still the batch's worst sentence, it appeared in the last line, and four of the state files had recorded the repair as made before it was made.** The classes are now seven: a fact stated twice with different values; a date derived from a prompt rather than from the pinned line; a count-lock collision; author-facing meta language; a weekday label written from the real calendar instead of the pinned one; a piece of arithmetic nobody actually did; and a subtraction or a conversion between counts of things that are not the same kind of thing.

# FIXED BY VOLUME 02, BATCH 0003 (CHAPTERS 71–80), "THE MIDPOINT"

**Canon taken from finished prose. Where a drafted fact corrects the Batch 0002 handoff, the corrected fact is here and the handoff entry is marked superseded. The volume midpoint is in this batch, at Chapter 75, and it is a reversal of a reading and not of a monster.**

## THE DAY MAP AS DRAFTED, AND IT IS THE PINNED LINE

**Derived from the pinned line (`Fri 1 · Sat 2 · … · Sun 31 January`, then `Mon 1 · Tue 2 · Wed 3 · Thu 4 · Fri 5 · Sat 6 February 2025`, then `Mon 1 March` onward), NOT from the batch prompt's day map, whose weekday labels were two days out for every chapter from 72 onward.** ⚠ **The original version of this line ended *Verified by script … 47 phrases, zero real mismatches*, and that sentence was FALSE. The script it described converted word ordinals to numerals with a substitution that returns an empty string for *sixth*, *eleventh* and *twenty-second*, and silently skipped every phrase it could not parse. It reported zero while Chapters 71 and 72 were both stating the wrong weekday for 6 March 2025. It has been re-run with word ordinals handled properly and the real figure is in the `REVIEW PASS` section at the foot of this file: 93 phrases, 0 mismatches, 0 unparsed.**

**The batch runs Saturday 6 February to Sunday 28 February 2025: twenty-two days, three weeks and a day.**

## THE BATCH'S FINDINGS, AND THEY ARE THE CONTINUITY OF THE CASE

1. **THE FIRST REGISTRY SLOT IS SPENT AND IT PRODUCED TWO ANSWERS AND A DATE.** `this schedule has no box for it` is confirmed in writing on 6 February 2025, and the reason is the batch's hinge: **there is no object in the Museum's file.** The mat is not accessioned, has no accession number, no collection line, no series, and is on a plastic sheet in a public gallery; a description is not a classification; a card is a description. **The Registry has exactly one category for a place and it is `PLACE OF INTERMENT`, it requires a date of permanence, and S. Tully would not use it on a door mat.** That refusal is the volume's question made institutional and it is **NOT answered in this batch.**
2. **FORM A/3 IS NOT REFUSED AND NOT GRANTED AND HAS NEVER BEGUN.** A period runs from a determination; there is no determination; there cannot be one while there is no box. The reply went out of time at 09:00 on 6 February and is issued late with the lateness on its face. **It is listed: determination day Saturday 6 March 2025, assessor S. Tully, and a listed question is decided on a stated day with a stated assessor in the room. The Registry has never listed a question off a form that had never been a determination.** Box nine, *supplementary statement by another person in the matter*, has been in use once.
3. **`E. VENN` IS IN A FIELD AND IT IS A PAYEE FIELD AND IT IS CORRECT.** Ninth entry in the municipal fixed-positions file of 16 September 2024, cost centre **M.Q.S. 4**, grade blank, status active, remittance monthly and unbroken. **It is the only thing that has kept him a legal neighbour since 8 December 2024, and it dies if the post is closed, and he has told nobody and it is in the notebook.** S. Tully's sentence, once: *a person's legal neighbour is not a thing the Registry grants. It is a thing other systems happen to agree on by writing a name in a field, and none of them has ever asked the person.*
4. **THE MUSEUM'S THRESHOLD GROUP IS A FILTER AND NOT A LIST.** Selected by a postal round number and a district. A household that moves round inside the district stays on it; a household that leaves the district falls off it; **neither is a decision about a person and nobody in the building is told which.** The last complete copy of the four hundred and twelve names is the print run of 2 December 2024 in a box behind a counter, and nobody had ever put it next to anything. **A tenancy is the only document in this country that says a person is a neighbour of a place** — signed, dated, producible, and not one of the four hundred and twelve is the Museum's, and the Museum has never seen one.
5. **NEWTON BAY (ESTATES) LIMITED, freeholder. MORTON HAY & BAKER (LETTING & MANAGEMENT) LTD, 11 Pemberton Row, Low Ward, managing agent. Both of them found in about nine seconds by ringing a water authority, and both of them are on a bill, and neither of them is on any list this case had been given.** The Director's letter of 11 February asks **a question and not a document** and says a refusal would be the most useful thing the Museum will receive that month.
6. **THE PROGRAMME. Internal review paper of 24 August 2023. Nine blocks. About four hundred and twelve tenancies. Cycle date is the first day of each calendar quarter. A block enters programme on a cycle date; no tenancy in that block is renewed at the next term end; notices are served on the cycle date, at the earliest date permitted, and always on that day, on paper, by hand, at the property, by a person whose name is on the notice. Not one notice challenged, not one late.** Term-end windows: block three 20 May – 19 June 2024; **block four 20 October – 29 November 2024**; **block five 20 November – 17 December 2024**; **block six 17 February – 24 March 2025**; block seven enters 1 April 2025; block eight 1 July 2025; **block nine 1 October 2025, and after the first of October the programme in this ward is finished.** Broad Street is block four. **80, 84 and 90 Sillick Lane are block five.**
7. **THE MIDPOINT, AND IT IS A REVERSAL OF A READING.** It is not a curse, not a machine gone wrong, not a monster and not a haunting. It is a landlord running a lawful programme of about four hundred and twelve tenancies in nine blocks on a quarterly cycle since the summer of 2023, and **the machine is the last four inches and the four inches are about a fortnight and the fortnight is a man with a handheld.** **What is being taken from some households is not a home. It is the ability to be recognised as a legal neighbour: a name in a field, an address on a return, a knock that gets an answer.** Nobody is a villain. The document that carries it is a statement of programme signed by a letting agent, and it is correct.
8. **THE SECOND HALF OF THE REVERSAL, AND IT IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF.** There is a household in the ward whose tenancy ended in the programme and which is **owed nothing** and which appears on **no list of people owed anything** — not the practice sheet, not the hundred and forty in a drawer, not the public schedule of interim payments, not the inquiry, not the threshold group — and it is entirely fine, and it moved out in December, and it gave a forwarding address. **It cannot be named in a public room, because a name of a person in a public room is a hook, and the Director says so in a room of about a hundred and ten people on 17 February 2025 and declines to name a thing that would have made a good sentence.** Its tenancy file reads: **SCAIFE, T., 90 Sillick Lane. Term ended 12 December 2024. Notice served 1 October 2024 by hand at the property. Keys returned. Deposit returned in full. No arrears. No correspondence outstanding. Forwarding address given.** A letting agent's file destroys tenancy files **on a Friday**; the Museum's block keeps nothing; **three retention policies and none of them aligned, and nobody has ever put them on one page.**
9. **THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER IS DEMOTED FOR THE LAST TIME AND IT IS THE ONLY DATE IN THE CASE WITH A PERSON IN IT, AND THERE IS NO PERSON.** **The tenancy at 88 Sillick Lane is current, has been since about 2019, and runs to the twenty-second of August, and it has never been served with a notice.** A walk-survey code was set on it on the afternoon of Saturday the fifth of December 2024 at twenty-two minutes past four by a body, which means a man at a door, which means a man of about sixty-five was out. The field cleared at 03:41 the next morning. **The reason Elias Venn stood on that date for eight weeks is that it was the only date with a person in it and he did not want the case to be a machine, and he said so out loud in a room on 12 February 2025 and conceded it in about nine seconds.** The plastic wallet came out on a table for the first time and went back in.
10. **THE NINE BLANKS, CLASSIFIED. Four carry a date and all four are on the east side of Sillick Lane. Three of those four sit inside block five's term-end window and one does not, and the one that does not is 88 Sillick Lane. Five of the nine carry no date in any document in any institution in this city, and a programme cannot explain a row that cannot be placed on a day.** Ivo Pell's finding: **the five are the ones to be frightened of and the four are not, and it is the first time in nine weeks that anybody in this case has wanted to be frightened of a smaller number than the other one.**
11. **THE POSTMAN'S SHEET IS GONE AND THE PRICE OF BEING DECENT TO HIM IS ON THE PAGE. Roy Tilley's round changed on Tuesday 9 February 2025; FLS-2 went back into a rack of about nine hundred and will be binned in 2027. Nobody in this building knows what day of the week the ninth of February was, and neither does Elias Venn, and that is the price of a man on a pavement in January saying he would rather be a man who got moved than a man who knew.** He was right. Nobody will ever know what the sheet was.
12. **RULE THREE IS THE REASON THE PRACTICE CANNOT BE SCALED, AND NOBODY HAD EVER LOOKED AT IT.** *Anybody taking part may stop at any point, and nobody's asked afterwards why.* On the sheet since **8 January 2025** in a different hand. **A rota is a duty, and a duty a person can stop at any point without a form is a suggestion with a list on it, and a permission cannot be scaled.** The three rules in full: *1. You knock first. Then you say the name. 2. It runs from three o'clock until you can't read a number off the door. 3. Anybody taking part may stop at any point, and nobody's asked afterwards why.*
13. **THE MUSEUM CAN HOLD THE MAT AND CANNOT HOLD THE KNOCK.** A mat is two pounds and goes on a Friday count inside four months. A knock is not an object, has no accession number, and **there is no box for one in this county or this country, and the only reason there has not been one in four hundred years is that nobody has ever needed one, and about nine people in Low Ward do.** A statement is a promise to say nothing, and it is the first thing in this business that is one, and it will be read out **on the step at 88 Sillick Lane on Monday 22 March 2025** with the holder of the table deciding whether it is handed out.
14. **THE OBJECT-OR-PLACE QUESTION IS SHARPER AND IS STILL NOT ANSWERED.** A door mat has no date of permanence because you can buy another one for two pounds. A house has one because somebody wrote a piece of paper about when the term ends. **Nobody has said which the practice needs, and the Registrar has refused to say it in that hall, in February, in front of the man who keeps it.** The Registry's one place-box is for a burial. The volume's answer is not in this batch and `state/continuity.md` R-era Volume 02 reveal discipline governs.
15. **THE MORAL FLOOR IS USED AND IS NOT RESTATED.** Said in Mairin Devlin's twenty-one words (*there is no form in this country for a person at a house that no system has got a name for*), word for word, at a door, by a man quoting it, on 18 February 2025; and **nobody is going to ask Frank Dunning whether he was at home on the fifth of December, and the reason is arithmetic, not kindness** — the only word wanted from him is *no*, and a *no* about a Saturday afternoon is not about a field. And the floor's institutional form: **address not in roll following recorded change of occupancy, possible omission, date of change not in local copy** — three things about documents and no word about a person, written in a justification box for the first time in nine years, and it will be approved because it is not a lie.
16. **THE ROPE, AND THE ANSWER IS NOT A GOD.** A box of minutes 1934–1936 on the ground floor, opened for about twenty minutes on a stated request with a chair's initials on it, by a registrar, a label-room finisher and a key-board holder, **and not by the systems technician, who was not asked and who said in January that nobody would let him.** November 1935, a clerk of works: the cord line re-hung, the western portion perished at two stanchions, new line of the same appearance and no heavier, **the join to be made as the join the old line was made; stated purpose: CIRCULATION; the exhibition closed on the twenty-sixth; attendance over six weeks 11,400; the line moved four feet east to widen the route from the north door to the south door, fouled at the third stanchion on the last three Saturdays and again on the closing day; THE LINE IS NOT TO BE MOVED FOR THE PROTECTION OF THE OBJECTS. IT IS NOT ON THE SCHEDULE. IT IS FURNITURE.** Approved: J. H., Clerk of Works. **A folded list in the back of the September 1935 minute records nine carried patterns, with no maker, no date and no accession number for any of them, and it is the first time the number nine has been written down next to the things, and nobody in about a hundred and forty years has asked what it is a count of.** A transfer clerk said on 21 January 2025 that a rope is a printing press with a walking speed, and the sentence in the minute and the sentence in his mouth are the same sentence ninety years apart. **And a registrar said the sentence that keeps it out of a god, and asked for it to be minuted somewhere: a clerk of works answered one question and it is the right answer to that one, and it is not the answer to what a cord line is doing in a room with nine small things in it that people stop at, and no professional is going to answer that in a store, and I would like whoever reads this in four years to know that a professional looked at it and did not claim it.**
17. **THE PLINTH-34 CARD IS CORRECTED AND NOBODY WILL EVER READ IT, AND IT IS CORRECT.** Corrected **Thursday 25 February 2025**, three lines struck through and dated, a paragraph underneath, initialled **M. ILES** and **F., label room**. **The panel is ninety days old and it stays**, and a finding card — the first in about a hundred and forty years in this building that is a statement and not a description — is on a front desk in a plastic sleeve, and it says which plinth, what was corrected and on what date, that the panel stays and why, that no photograph of the correction has been made and none will be, and it is signed **F., label room**, and **there is no person's name on it and no reference to a minute on it.** **Fenn: you can card a bell; you cannot card a rope; there is no line on the form for it.** **A thing with a panel over it is a record that somebody asked.**
18. **THE MUSEUM HAS NOW BEEN ASKED FOR A NIL RETURN, IN A FORM, WITH A DATE ON IT.** Conciliation service, rule 9, 19 February 2025: a matter listed for a preliminary hearing **Thursday 18 March 2025 at 10:30**; the Museum notified as a party because of its own minute of 27 January; **a nil return is a valid return, due seven days before the hearing, which is Thursday 11 March 2025, which is the same day the schedule of collections goes to the Board.** A party that returns a document it does not wish to produce is worse for that party than a party that returns nothing.
19. **THE FIRST PERSON OUTSIDE THIS BUILDING TO ASK ELIAS VENN A QUESTION, AND THE ANSWER IS THE BATCH'S COST.** A partner of Halliward Griswold & Reeve, about fifty-two, of 22 Pike's Buildings, Irving Quay, for Newton Bay (Estates) Limited, on Wednesday 24 February 2025: **are you the person who writes it down.** Nine pages, thirty-five pence, in a coat, no copy, not filed, deliberately, under clause six. And then, in order: *I could probably get it, not lawfully, not easily, and I am telling you that I know that version exists* · *I am not going to do it, and I am not going to try, and I am telling you that I am not going to do it, and I want you to hear the shape of that sentence, which is I am telling you, and not I won't* · **if your evidence cannot be produced then it cannot be tested; an untestable witness in a listed hearing is not a protection, it is a hole in the case with a person standing in it.** He is sure. **Nobody in nine weeks had told him that not keeping a notebook is a risk to the case; everybody had told him it is the only honest arrangement; both are true and he had been carrying the comfortable one.**
20. **THE PRACTICE IS NOT A COVENANT AND CANNOT BE ONE, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL, NOT MORAL.** A covenant is two people and a date. There is nobody in the ward who can hold anybody to anything. **The minute somebody is keeping it, it is not what it is now: it would be the same three rules and there would be a fourth one that nobody wrote, and the fourth one would be *somebody is keeping it*.** Three weeks and a day produced **a date (the first of April, the first of July, the first of October, and after the first of October the programme in this ward is finished), a boundary (rule three), and a document somebody has to keep (a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet on a table, kept by a man of about sixty-five whose tenancy ends on the twenty-second of August and who is owed nothing and is on no list of people owed anything).** Nothing else. **And the open question, which is the batch's last line and is not *what a name field is for*: who keeps it.**
21. **THE NEW DOCUMENT, AND IT IS THE FIRST GOOD INSTRUMENT IN TWELVE WEEKS.** **Statement of Tenure Programme, Morton Hay & Baker (Letting & Management) Ltd, 11 February 2025, signed S. BOULTON, MRICS: about four hundred and twelve tenancies in nine blocks, a programme on a quarterly cycle entered into on or about 1 October 2023 on a programme paper of 24 August 2023 available to any person at no charge, every notice served in accordance with the Act, none challenged, the firm will confirm to any household in blocks one to six the date its notice was served and the date of the term end on production of the notice or of any part of it or of a description of it. No tenant named, no rent, no arrears, no correspondence, no date of birth. The firm's refusal to supply a schedule is repeated in the statement.** A record with a name on it and nothing else on it, produced on purpose, and readable in a hearing without a single person in it.
22. **HIS OWN RULE, SAID OUT LOUD, TO A STRANGER, AT A DOOR, ONCE.** *A fact a person gives me in a street is theirs until it is in a file.* He has said it to nobody before. **The cost is that in about a month he will not be able to say that he went.**

## NEW PEOPLE. ALL COMPETENT. NONE A VILLAIN. NONE A FOOL. NONE A SPECTATOR.

- **Sonia Boulton, about thirty-nine, MRICS, letting agent, Morton Hay & Baker (Letting & Management) Ltd, 11 Pemberton Row, Low Ward.** Eleven years in post. **Has a hardback book: every visit she has made since about 2019, in her hand, with a date. It is the only register in this case and she will not give it to anybody and says the same sentence about it as about the schedule.** She has refused six things since the autumn, in order: a council's estate team (about October), a ward councillor (November), an insurance broker (December), a journalist (January), a competitor (January), and a man in a coat through a records clerk in four seconds (December 2024) — **and the seventh is the Museum.** She came to a museum on a Thursday afternoon and read the programme out loud, in about nineteen minutes, with no tenancies and no names in it, and said the room could not write it down and that was the point, and that was her professional opinion about the Museum. **She is the person in the middle of this batch and she is not a villain and she has not done anything wrong.**
- **S. Tully, about forty-five, assessor, Quiet Accord Registry, `QA/CR/1426`, nineteen years in post, and she has never been spoken to by anybody in this case until 6 February 2025.** She read her own sentence back with the punctuation and said nobody had ever done that to her. She produced two answers on a Saturday morning, one of them out of time and issued late with the lateness on its face. She found a requester's name in a bureau field in four seconds. She said one sentence about legal neighbours and said it once and would not say it again. **She was right about everything and she is the reason the volume's question is now a question and not a proposal.**
- **An unnamed partner of Halliward Griswold & Reeve, 22 Pike's Buildings, Irving Quay, about fifty-two.** Not frightened of anybody in the building. Read the Museum's minute four times and worked out that a registrar's November sentence about hooks applies to his own client. Told the Director that her 17 February meeting is how a commercial programme left a museum, as a courtesy and not a threat. Asked a systems technician's own threshold question about whether she would say the same words in March. **And asked Elias Venn a question, and told him three things about his notebook, the third of which is the most important thing anybody has said to him in twelve weeks.**
- **A board trustee, about seventy, nine years on that board**, different from Mrs Vessey, who says almost nothing before lunch. **He objected first, on the ground that the Museum told the public twice — in a public room in November and in a minute on 27 January — that it would not put a person's name in a public document, and here is a man's name going into a Board paper. He asked for the word *hook* to be in the minute so that it would not be tidy, and for it to be recorded that the Chair had no view, and he said he has been going to look at a building for nine years and did not know it was possible that one of its schedules could contain a person.**
- **A woman of about thirty-four at the end of a road called Broad Street**, alone, who moved in on **Sunday 29 November 2024**, works, pays her rent, her tax and her bills, has voted since she was twenty-one, has never had a letter that was not returned, and is on no list and is owed nothing. ⚠ **SHE IS NOT NAMED. Not in this manuscript, not on a card and not in a file, and she is not to be named in Volume 02 without a decision recorded here first, because the batch's best beat is Chapter 76, in which he tells her to her face that he is not going to write her name down and then does not write it. An earlier version of this entry said she *is* named in the manuscript. That was false and it has been removed.** **She and Elias Venn are the only two people in this city in exactly the same position and neither of them knows the other exists.**
- **SCAIFE, T., of 90 Sillick Lane.** A name, not a witness, and a counter-example. Term ended 12 December 2024, keys returned, deposit returned in full, no arrears, no correspondence outstanding, forwarding address given. **Owed nothing. On no list. Not going to be knocked at.** The only person's name read in a room in this case since 4 February 2025, and it is on a sheet in a file in Low Ward, and it was not read in a public room, on purpose.
- **A man in his forties, of about nineteen years at an address in block six**, unnamed, who stood up in a public gallery on 17 February and asked what he was meant to say. Notice served 1 January 2025 by hand. **Term ends Thursday 18 March 2025.**
- **TWO SPEAKING PARTS WHO ARE NOT IN THE LIST ABOVE, AND WHO WERE NOT RECORDED ANYWHERE UNTIL THE REVIEW OF 2026-09-26 FOUND THEM UNRECORDED.** **A trustee of about sixty-four**, a different person from Mrs Vessey and a different person from the trustee of about seventy, who speaks in Chapter 72 (three times, in a room with four people in it) and in Chapter 73 (once), who says *it is the moral floor* and is told by the Registrar that it is a systems observation and is not to be said by a fourth person by accident in a committee room, and who is on the item-six side of the inquiry and not on the Board. He is competent and he is not a fool and he is not a villain. **A trustee of about sixty-four is not to be confused with the board trustee of about seventy in Chapter 78, who is a different person again and who says almost nothing before lunch.** ⚠ **The Batch 0003 pass listed *Denise Hargreaves* and *Doreen Sarn* as appearing in this batch. Neither appears. It listed *Marion Kelsh*, *Roy Tilley* and *R.M.* as appearing; each is referred to in Chapter 72 and none speaks. It listed *Hattie Bramm* twice. Corrected here.**

## WHAT THE BATCH DELIBERATELY DID **NOT** DO

- **It did not make a villain, a buyer, a market, a network, the Lantern Society, a new god or a new antagonist.** A landlord's programme is lawful, dated, checkable and owned by a company that has never been asked for anything in eighteen months, and the freeholder is on a bill and in a solicitor's letterhead and nowhere else.
- **It did not answer whether the threshold pattern is the object or the place.** It made the question institutional (`PLACE OF INTERMENT`, a date of permanence, a tenancy with a term) and **the Registrar refused to choose in the hall, and the volume's answer is not in this batch.**
- **It did not state a total, a shortfall or a difference, and it did not convert a count of addresses into a count of people.** The escalation of the batch is about four hundred and twelve tenancies in nine blocks, a school of about three hundred and eighty children and about two hundred adults, about nine addresses on Broad Street that this city has no form for, and one institution that can put a name on a card.
- **It did not restate the moral floor in the same words.** It was quoted in twenty-one words at a door by a man who was given it by other people, and it was *used* twice: to stop a question being asked of a man of sixty-five, and to write a true sentence in a justification box for the first time in nine years.
- **It did not give Elias Venn a stop word and nobody offered him one who had the standing to.** He was asked, by the man who keeps the practice, whether he had one, and said no, and was told *the street does not own it, I do.*
- **It did not spend the cost curve.** No second notebook entry, no memory cost, no time lost, no name gone. He **read nothing in three weeks and a day** and it is now a method and not a coincidence.
- **It did not re-stage the two failures of condition 2, and condition 2 is not amended and is not displaced.** Annexe E's stay is stricter than condition four and is not argued about. The standing bar of 18 November is live and is not referred to. The Museum's plinth card is still in place beneath the panel and the requester has still not been asked again.
- **It did not name T. VENN or R.M.J. and it did not explain the ability.** The room at the east end of the Quiet Wing corridor is still not described. **The bell is silent and the bell at 11:52 on 11 November 2024 is not referred to in this batch at all.**
- **It did not use the public register, and it did not put a child, a pensioner, a person receiving a service or a person in a care home on a card or in a file.**
- **It did not resolve anything.** Ten consequences are held open, the twenty-two days end with all ten open, and the last line of the batch is a question and not the old one.

## RECORDED DEVIATIONS FROM THE BATCH PROMPT, ITEMISED

1. **THE PROMPT'S DAY MAP HAD EVERY WEEKDAY LABEL FROM CHAPTER 72 ONWARD TWO DAYS OUT**, because it was built on the real 2025 calendar and not on the pinned line. It gave 10 February as a Monday (it is a **Wednesday**), 17 February as a Monday (**Wednesday**), 20 February as a Thursday (**Saturday**), 24 February as a Monday (**Wednesday**), 25 February as a Tuesday (**Thursday**) and 28 February as a Friday (**Sunday**). **The beats did not move and no date moved: 6, 10, 11, 12, 17, 18, 20, 24, 25 and 28 February 2025. Only the weekday words moved, and they moved to the pinned line. The day map of record is the table above and the sweep is honest: forty-seven weekday+date phrases, zero real mismatches.**
2. **THE PROMPT SAID THE PRACTICE IS "THREE WEEKS AND FOUR DAYS OLD" AT THE START OF THE BATCH.** On the pinned line it is **four weeks and five days** on 6 February, **six weeks and five days** on 20 February (Chapter 77) and **seven weeks and six days** on 28 February (Chapter 80). Frank Dunning says the figures himself, as he said "three weeks and three days" in Chapter 69, and all three compute from **Monday 4 January 2025**.
3. **THE PROMPT SAID THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER IS "A TUESDAY".** On the pinned line **5 December 2024 is a Saturday**, which is what Chapter 52, Chapter 58, Chapter 60 and Chapter 70 already say, and it is the day the whole of Chapter 74 rests on: **a walk survey at about four o'clock on a Saturday afternoon is not a decision.**
4. **THE PROMPT SAID THE BATCH'S CLOSE IS TWENTY-FOUR DAYS.** Six February to twenty-eight February is **twenty-two days, three weeks and a day**, and that is what the chapter says.
5. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 71 SAID "ANJA WARD" WOULD BE AMONG THOSE SPOKEN TO.** She is not. The people in the room in Northglass on 6 February 2025 are **Mara Iles, Ivo Pell, Elias Venn and S. Tully**, and the Director's own sentence at 11:00 in a corridor the night before is that the Museum is the last institution in the chain and must not lead with itself, which is also the midpoint's load-bearing half and is better for it.
6. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 76 SAYS "A REGISTRATION OFFICER WITH A SATCHEL, A MAN WITH A NOTEBOOK WHO CANNOT WRITE IT DOWN".** The man has **nothing in his hands at all** and does not take the notebook out, which is the second time in ten weeks — the chapter's own figure, and the case is ten weeks and four days old on 18 February, so the two agree — and is the chapter's cost. **He has not written anything down for three weeks at the end of it.**
7. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 78 SAYS THE COMPARISON SHEET HAS "A DEAD MAN'S NAME ON IT, AND NO ACCESSION NUMBER, AND THE REGISTRAR HAS TO EXPLAIN IT TO A TRUSTEE".** It does, and the trustee is about seventy and objects on a ground nobody had raised, which is better than the prompt's.
8. **THE PROMPT'S CARD 79 SAYS "THE NINTH OF THE NINE CARRIED PATTERNS".** It is handled as the number itself: **a folded list in the back of the September 1935 minute says nine**, with no maker, no date and no accession number for any of them, and it is the first time the number has been written down next to the things, and nobody has asked in about a hundred and forty years what it is a count of. **The answer is not given.**
9. **THE PROMPT ASKED FOR "ABOUT NINE ADDRESSES ON BROAD STREET THAT NOBODY HAS A FORM FOR" AND THE CHAPTER IS ABOUT ONE OF THEM AND ABOUT NINE.** Both are true and both are on the page, and the chapter is about a single household and about a pad of nine houses a registration officer has always had.
10. **TWO META CONSTRUCTIONS FOUND BY THE SWEEP AFTER DRAFTING AND REMOVED** — *this chapter* ×5, *this volume* ×2. The *this case* construction is the manuscript's own and is left. ⚠ **The first draft of this item also claimed that two *eighteen months* errors had been removed "where the case is twelve weeks old". That claim was false twice over: the removals were never made, and they should never have been made. *Eighteen months* is CORRECT and is now canon — the programme paper is dated 24 August 2023 (`state/continuity.md` item 6) and February 2025 is eighteen months later, and Chapters 73, 75 and 80 all say eighteen months and are right. The claim is struck.**
